Affine Springer fibers and depth zero L L -packets Thanks: This research was partially supported by the BFS grants 2016363 and 2020189. The research of R.B. was also partially supported by NSF grant DMS-2101507. The research of Y.V. was partially supported by the ISF grants 822/17 and 2091/21.
Roman Bezrukavnikov, Yakov Varshavsky

Abstract
. Let G G be a connected reductive group over a field F = 𝔽 q ​ ( ( t ) ) F=\mathbb{F}_{q}((t)) splitting over 𝔽 ¯ q ​ ( ( t ) ) \overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}((t)) . Following [ KV , DR ] , a tamely unramified Langlands parameter λ : W F → G L ​ ( ℚ ¯ ℓ ) \lambda:W_{F}\to{}^{L}G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}) in general position gives rise to a finite set Π λ \Pi_{\lambda} of irreducible admissible representations of G ⁡ ( F ) G(F) , called the L L -packet. The main goal of this work is to provide a geometric description of characters χ π \chi_{\pi} of π ∈ Π λ \pi\in\Pi_{\lambda} and of their endoscopic linear combinations χ λ κ \chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa} in terms of homology of affine Springer fibers. As an application, we prove that the sum χ λ st := ∑ π ∈ Π λ χ π \chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}:=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}}\chi_{\pi} is stable and show that the χ λ st \chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}} ’s are compatible with inner twistings. More generally, we prove that each χ λ κ \chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa} is ℰ λ , κ \mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa} -stable.



Introduction

0.1
.

The goal of this work.

Let
F
be a local non-archimedean field,
\Gamma_{F}
the absolute Galois group of
F
,

I^{w}_{F}\subseteq I_{F}\subseteq W_{F}\subseteq\Gamma_{F}
the wild inertia, the inertia and the Weil group of
F
,

\ell
a prime number different
from the characteristic of
F
,
G
a connected reductive group over

F
split over a maximal unramified extension
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
of
F
, and

{}^{L}G=\widehat{G}\rtimes\Gamma_{F}
the Langlands dual group of
G
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

The local Langlands conjecture predicts that every Langlands parameter
\lambda:W_{F}\to{}^{L}G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
gives rise
to a certain finite set
\Pi_{\lambda}
(called the
L
-packet
) of irreducible admissible representations of

G(F)
. It is also believed that at least in the tempered case a certain explicit linear combination

\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}}d_{\pi}\chi_{\pi}
of characters of representations from
\Pi_{\lambda}

is stable.

More generally (see
[
Lan
]
), every semisimple element
\kappa\in Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda)\subseteq\widehat{G}
in the centralizer of
\lambda
gives rise to an
endoscopic datum

\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
for
G
(see Section
4.1.5
(b)), and a certain explicit linear combination
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}}d_{\pi}(\kappa)\chi_{\pi}
is expected to be
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stable

(see Section
4.1.7
(d)). Furthermore, we have
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}=\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}
when
\kappa=1
, and
the
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}
’s spans the same vector space as
\{\chi_{\pi}\}_{\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}}
.

Though the conjectural generalized functions
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}
are
predicted in some cases, there is no general direct procedure producing the generalized
functions
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}
for a given
\lambda
. It is expected, however, that at least in the equal characteristic case
it should be possible to carry out such a construction using geometry of
\ell
-adic sheaves.
In the present article we realize this approach in a special case.

Conjugation equivariant sheaves on the loop group do not explicitly appear in this work. However, our result provides a new piece of evidence for existence of a loop group analogue of the theory of character sheaves: from this perspective our present results describe stalks of a hypothetical generic depth zero character sheaf (see
[
Lu3
]
, where hypothetical unipotent character sheaves on the loop group have been introduced, and
[
BKV1
]
, where related sheaves were used to categorify elements in Bernstein center).
We plan to return to this topic in a future work.

0.2
.

Main results.

Assume that
G
splits over a maximal unramified extension
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
of
F
, that Langlands parameter
\lambda
is
elliptic
, that is, does not factor through
{}^{L}M\subseteq{}^{L}G
for a proper Levi subgroup
M\subsetneq G
, that
\lambda
is
tamely ramified
, that is,
I_{F}^{w}\subseteq\operatorname{Ker}\lambda
, and that
\lambda
is in a
general position
, that is,
Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda(I))\subseteq\widehat{G}
is a maximal torus
\widehat{T}
.
In this case,
\lambda
factors through
{}^{L}T\subseteq{}^{L}G
for a certain elliptic maximal unramified torus
T\subseteq G
over
F
, we have
Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda)=\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
.

This setting was considered first in the work
[
KV
]
, where the expected
L
-packet
\Pi_{\lambda}
and generalized functions
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
were introduced (for all fields). Moreover, the
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stability of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
was proven there in the mixed characteristic case by analytic methods (see
[
KV
, Theorem 2.1.6]
and Section
0.11
below). Slightly later, the stability for
\kappa=1
was also shown in
[
DR
]
by a similar method.

Assume now that
F=\mathbb{F}_{q}((t))
. The main goal of this paper is to give a geometric description of the restriction of

\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
to the set of regular semisimple elements of
G(F)
. Namely, we express it in terms of a trace of Frobenius on the derived coinvariants of the homology of affine Springer fibers.
1
1

1

We do it under the assumption that the characteristic
p
of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
satisfies
p>2h
, where
h
is the Coxeter number of
G
.
In particular, our results can be viewed as analogs of Lusztig’s conjectures
[
Lu4
]
for non-unipotent depth zero representations.

More precisely, to every compact element
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
one can associate the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
at
\gamma
. By the local Langlands correspondence for tori, the Langlands parameter
\lambda
gives rise to an
\ell
-adic local system
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
of rank
|W|
, the cardinality of the Weyl group
W
of
G
(the local system is trivial geometrically but it carries a nontrivial action of Frobenius, see Sections
0.3
,
0.4
).

Generalizing
[
Lu2
]
,
[
Yun
]
, we equip each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
with an action of the semidirect product
\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
of the extended affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}_{G}
of
G
and the absolute Galois group of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
Moreover, we show that
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}]
-module, hence the group cohomology

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))
is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})]
-module, which is non-zero only for finitely many pairs
i,j
.

One of the main results of this work asserts that we have an equality

(0.1)

\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))),

where
\sigma\in\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
denotes the arithmetic Frobenius element. In order to prove the result, we establish a geometric description of
\chi_{\pi}(\gamma)
for every
\pi\in\Pi_{\lambda}
(see Section
0.5
).

Using identity (
0.1
) and (a group analog of) the result of
[
Yun
]
on compatibility between the Springer–Lusztig action
and the centralizer action (see Section
0.10
), we show that the restriction
\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
is stable, that is,
for every pair of stably conjugate elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
we have an equality

\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}(\gamma)=\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}(\gamma^{\prime})
. Furthermore, we show that the stable characters
\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
are compatible with inner twistings.
2
2

2

By a theorem of Harish–Chandra, in the mixed characteristic case the restriction of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}
to the regular semisimple locus is a locally constant function, which is locally
L^{1}
.
Therefore, to show stability of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}
(and compatibility with inner twistings) it suffices to show the corresponding assertions for restrictions to the regular semisimple locus.
On the other hand, the analogue of the theorem of Harish-Chandra does not seem to be known in equal characteristic.

We also obtain a similar geometric description of
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}

for each
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, and deduce (using theorem of Yun) that
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
is
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stable.

Thus we provide the first proof of stability and endoscopy property (on the regular semisimple locus) for the above
L
-packets in the equal characteristic case. In fact, it may also be possible to deduce these results from the mixed characteristic case
established in
[
KV
]

by the techniques of
[
Ka
]
.
Thus we view the geometric interpretation of characters and of their endoscopic property
rather than the proof of stability per se as the main novelty of this work.

We now describe our results in more detail and outline the strategy of proofs.

0.3
.

The construction of
[
KV
]
.

(a) By the local Langlands correspondence for tori, the Langlands parameter
\lambda:W_{F}\to{}^{L}T\subseteq{}^{L}G

corresponds to a tamely ramified homomorphism

\theta=\theta_{\lambda}:T(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}.

Let
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q}
be the ring of integers and the residue field of
F
, respectively. Since
T
is unramified, it has a natural structure
T_{\mathcal{O}}
over
\mathcal{O}
, hence gives rise to a torus
\overline{T}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Since
\theta
is tamely ramified, the restriction
\theta|_{T(\mathcal{O})}:T(\mathcal{O})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
factors through a character

\overline{\theta}:\overline{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

(b) Let
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
be an embedding, which is stably
conjugate to the inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
. Since
\mathfrak{a}(T)\subseteq G
is a maximal elliptic torus, there exists
a unique parahoric subgroup
G_{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq G(F)
such that

\mathfrak{a}(T(\mathcal{O}))\subseteq G_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

We denote by
LG
the loop group ind-scheme of
G
, by
L^{+}(G_{\mathfrak{a}})\subseteq LG
the arc-group scheme of the parahoric subgroup
G_{\mathfrak{a}}
,
3
3

3

that is, the arc-group scheme of the corresponding Bruhat–Tits group scheme over
\mathcal{O}
, whose generic fiber is
G
.

by
M_{\mathfrak{a}}
the quotient of
L^{+}(G_{\mathfrak{a}})
by its pro-unipotent radical, by
\overline{\mathfrak{a}}:\overline{T}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{a}}
the map induced by
\mathfrak{a}
.

Let
R_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\overline{\theta}}
be the virtual Deligne–Lusztig representation of
M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})

corresponding to the maximal torus
\overline{\mathfrak{a}}(\overline{T})\subseteq M_{\mathfrak{a}}
and
character
\overline{\theta}
. Since character
\theta
is in general position,

R_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\overline{\theta}}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation up to a sign.

(c) Let
Z(G)
be the center of
G
, and denote by
R_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\theta}
the representation of group
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=G_{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot Z(G)(F)
such that the restriction of
R_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\theta}
to
G_{\mathfrak{a}}
is the inflation of
R_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\overline{\theta}}

and restriction of
R_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\theta}
to
Z(G)(F)
is the restriction of
\theta
.

Next, let
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
be the induced representation
\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}}^{G(F)}(R_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\theta})
. Then each
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation up to a sign.

(d) Let
\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}_{2},\ldots,\mathfrak{a}_{n}
be a set of representatives of
conjugacy classes of embeddings
T\hookrightarrow G
, which are stably
conjugate to the inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. For every
i=1,\ldots,n
, we can consider the
general position

\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\mathfrak{a}_{0})\in H^{1}(F,T)
. Then, by the Tate-Nakayama duality, for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
we can form
a pairing
\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\mathfrak{a})\rangle\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

We denote by
\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}:=\sum_{i}\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\theta}}

the sum of characters of the
\pi_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\theta}
’s. More generally, for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
,
we set
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\mathfrak{a}_{0})\rangle\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\theta}}
. By definition, we have

\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}=\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}
when
\kappa=1
.

0.4
.

Geometric interpretation of
\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}
.

We now describe our geometric expression (
0.1
) for the restriction of

\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}
to the locus
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
of compact
elements of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
in more detail.
4
4

4

This implies the description of the restriction to the locus of all regular semisimple elements, because characters

\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\theta}}
are supported on elements which are products of a compact element and a central element.

(a) By Lang’s isogeny, a character
\overline{\theta}:\overline{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
gives rise to a rank one local system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
on
\overline{T}
, equipped with a Weil structure.

(b) Let
\overline{T}_{G}
be the “abstract Cartan” of the loop group
LG
of
G
, that is,
\overline{T}_{G}
is a torus over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
,
which is canonically isomorphic to the quotient
\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
for every Iwahori subgroup scheme
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
(over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
) with pro-unipotent radical
\mathbf{I}^{+}
.
5
5

5

Notice that though
\mathbf{I}
is only defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, its Frobenius Galois conjugate
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}
is another Iwahori subgroup scheme of
LG
, hence we have a natural isomorphism between
\overline{T}_{G}=\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
and
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}/{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}^{+}\simeq{}^{\sigma}(\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+})={}^{\sigma}\overline{T}_{G}
. Therefore the torus
\overline{T}_{G}
, which is a priori is only defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, has a natural structure over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.

(c) Let
\mathbf{I}
be an Iwahori subgroup scheme of
LG
, defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and containing the arc-group scheme

L^{+}(T)=L^{+}(T_{\mathcal{O}})
of
T
. Then
\mathbf{I}
defines an isomorphism
\varphi=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
, where the isomorphism
\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
is induced by the composition
L^{+}(T)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
. Such an isomorphism
\varphi
we call
admissible
.

(d) Consider the quotient
\overline{W}=\overline{W}_{G}:=\widetilde{W}_{G}/\Lambda_{G}
, where
\widetilde{W}_{G}
is the extended Weyl group of
G
and
\Lambda_{G}:=X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G})
is a lattice of cocharacters of
\overline{T}_{G}
. Then
\overline{W}
is non-canonically isomorphic to the Weil group
W=W_{G}
of
G
, and the collection of admissible isomorphisms
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
form a
\overline{W}
-torsor,
and is stable under the
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
-action.

(e) Every admissible
\varphi
gives rise to the rank one local system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta})
on
\overline{T}_{G}
. Then it follows from part (d) that
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}:=\bigoplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}
is naturally a
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
of rank
|\overline{W}|
, equipped with a Weil structure. Also we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}\simeq\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\overline{w}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})

for every
\varphi
.

(f) For every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
, let
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
be the affine Springer fiber of
G
at
\gamma
, and
let
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
be the natural projection. Namely,
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
classifies the locus of all
[g]\in LG/\mathbf{I}
such that
g^{-1}\gamma g\in\mathbf{I}
and
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}([g])
is
the projection of
g^{-1}\gamma g\in\mathbf{I}
under the natural projection
\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}\simeq\overline{T}_{G}
, where
\mathbf{I}
is a fixed Iwahori subgroup.

(g) Since
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}
is a local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
equipped with a Weil structure, its pull back
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}:=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is a (geometrically trivial) Weil local system on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
.

We show (see Proposition
2.2.7
) that each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is equipped with a
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-action. Moreover, we have a decomposition

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})\simeq\bigoplus_{\varphi}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}),

where
\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})
a (geometrically trivial) rank one local system on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
, and an element
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
induces an isomorphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}\circ\varphi}),

where
\overline{w}\in\overline{W}
is the class of
w
, for every
\varphi
.

(h) It turns out (see Proposition
3.3.2
) that each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is a
finitely generated
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-module, therefore the group homology

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))

is a finite dimensional vector space equipped with an
action of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Moreover,
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))

is non-zero only for finitely many pairs
(i,j)
. Thus

H_{*}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})):=\sum_{i}\sum_{j}(-1)^{i+j}H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))

is a virtual finite dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})]
-module, and our identity (
0.1
) can be rewritten as

(0.2)

\chi_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{st}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{*}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))).

To prove (
0.2
), we provide a geometric interpretation of
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}
for each
\mathfrak{a}
as in Section
0.3
(b).

0.5
.

Geometric interpretation of
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}
: geometrically elliptic case.

(a) Fix an admissible isomorphism
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
(see Section
0.3
). Let
G^{\operatorname{sc}}
be the simply connected covering of the derived group of
G
,
\widetilde{W}:=\widetilde{W}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
the affine Weyl group of
G
,
\Lambda:=X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G^{sc}})
the group of cocharacters,
T_{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq G
the image of
\mathfrak{a}
, and
\varphi_{\alpha}:\overline{T}_{\mathfrak{a}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
the composition
\varphi\circ\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
.

(b) We choose an Iwahori subgroup scheme
\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\supseteq L^{+}(T_{\mathfrak{a}})
of
LG
defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
such
\varphi_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}}=\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}
. Then the Galois conjugate
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
is another Iwahori subgroup of
LG
, so we can consider their relative position
\widetilde{w}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}:=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}}\in\widetilde{W}
, getting thus an element
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}:=\widetilde{w}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\sigma\in\widetilde{W}\sigma\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
.

By construction, element
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
is unique up to a
\Lambda
-conjugacy, and each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is naturally a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\rangle]
-module.

(c) Assume that
G
is semisimple, and that element
\gamma
is geometrically elliptic. In this case, the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
is a scheme of finite type over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, hence each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-vector space.

Therefore
H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a virtual finite-dimensional

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\rangle]
-module, and our result asserts that in this case we have an equality

(0.3)

\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

In particular, the right hand side of (
0.3
) is independent of the choice of
\varphi
and
\mathbf{I}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
.

(d) The identity (
0.3
) is an affine analog of a theorem of Lusztig (see
[
Lu1
]
or
[
Lau
]
), asserting that characters of Deligne–Lusztig representations of reductive groups over finite fields can be described as the traces of Frobenius on the cohomology of Springer fibers.

The interpretation of
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}
in general is based on the following purely algebraic construction.

0.6
.

Generalized trace
(see Appendix A)
.

(a) Let
\Lambda
be a finitely generated free abelian group, and let
\psi\in\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda)
be an
elliptic
automorphism of finite order, where
elliptic
means that
\Lambda^{\psi}=\{0\}
. Consider the semi-direct product
\Delta:=\Lambda\rtimes\langle u\rangle
, where
\langle u\rangle
is the infinite cyclic group generated by
u
, and
u
acts on
\Lambda
as
\psi
, that is,
u\lambda u^{-1}=\psi(\lambda)
for every
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

(b) Recall that a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Delta]
-module
M
is called
u
-locally finite
, if it is a union of
u
-invariant finite dimensional vector
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-subspaces.

(c) To a
u
-locally finite
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Delta]
-module
M
, which is finitely generated as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module,
we associate a
generalized trace

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
characterized by the condition that the assignment

M\mapsto\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)
has the following three properties:

(i) (
additivity
) for every short exact sequence
0\to M^{\prime}\to M\to M^{\prime\prime}\to 0
, we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)=\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M^{\prime})+\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M^{\prime\prime});

(ii) we have
\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)=\operatorname{Tr}(u,M)
, if
M
is finite-dimensional over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
;

(iii) for a one-dimensional representation
V
of
\langle u\rangle
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
, viewed as a representation of
\Delta
, trivial on
\Lambda
,
we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}V)=\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)\cdot\operatorname{Tr}(u,V).

Explicitly, we choose a
\psi
-stable finite subset
\Lambda_{1}\subseteq\Lambda
, generating
\Lambda
as a monoid and a
u
-invariant finite-dimensional subspace
M_{0}\subseteq M
, which generates
M
as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module. This data gives a
u
-stable increasing filtration

\{M_{n}\}_{n}
of
M
, where
M_{n+1}=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{1}}\mu(M_{n})\subseteq M
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Then every
M_{n}
is finite-dimensional over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
, we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(u,M)=\operatorname{Tr}(u,M_{n})\text{ for every sufficiently large }n.

0.7
.

Geometric interpretation of
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}
: the general case.

(a) Notice that in the notation of Section
0.5
each
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\rangle]
-module
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
-locally finite and finitely generated as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.

(b) Let
Z_{0}\subseteq Z(G)
be the maximal split torus, and let
\Lambda_{0}:=X_{*}(Z_{0})
be the group of cocharacters. Then we have a natural isomorphism
\Lambda_{G}/\Lambda_{0}\simeq\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}
and the group of coinvariants
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}
is naturally a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\rangle]
-module, which is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
-locally finite and finitely generated as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}]
-module.

(c) Moreover, since the torus
T_{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq G
is elliptic, we see that the automorphism
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}|_{\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}}\in\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}})
is elliptic. Therefore all assumptions of Section
0.6
are satisfied, thus we can form a generalized trace
\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}})
for each
i
, hence a generalized trace

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}).

(d) The main technical result of this work asserts that for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
, we have an equality

(0.4)

\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}).

0.8
.

Geometric interpretation of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}
.
Fix
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
.

(a) Fix an admissible isomorphism
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
(see Section
0.5
), let
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
be an inclusion, and let
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}
be as in Section
0.5
(b). Then
\varphi
induces an isomorphism
X_{*}(T)\simeq X_{*}(\overline{T})\simeq X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G})=\Lambda_{G}
. By construction, it interchanges the action of
\sigma
on
X_{*}(T)
with the action of
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}
on
\Lambda_{G}
. Therefore
element
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}=\operatorname{Hom}(X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{F}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times})

can be viewed as a character
\kappa:\Lambda_{G}\ltimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}\rangle\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
, trivial on
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}
.

(b) As in Section
0.3
,
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a representation of
\Lambda_{G}\ltimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}\rangle
. Let

(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}
be a one-dimensional representation of
\Lambda_{G}\ltimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}\rangle
, corresponding to the character
\kappa
from part (a). Taking tensor product, we get a representation

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}:=H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}

of
\Lambda_{G}\ltimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}\rangle
. As in Section
0.4
(h), it is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.

(c) Consider an element
\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\in\overline{W}
such that
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi
and an element

\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}:=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\sigma\in\overline{W}\sigma\subseteq\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
. Then
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
is the image of

\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\varphi}
, so the space

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})
is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module. As in Section
0.4
(h), it is non-zero only for finitely many pairs
(i,j)
, so we can form a virtual finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module

H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}):=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}).

One of our main results asserts that we have an equality

(0.5)

\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})).

0.9
.

Outline of proofs.

(a) First we show identity (
0.4
). In the case when
G
is semisimple and
\gamma
is geometrically elliptic
(hence identity (
0.4
) specializes to identity (
0.3
)), the assertion follows from an analogous assertion for finite groups (Lusztig’s theorem
[
Lu1
, Propositions 8.15 and 9.2]
) using Lefschetz trace formula and the observation that
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}(\gamma)
can be written as an orbital integral of the character of the Deligne–Lusztig representation (compare
[
Ar
]
).

The proof in the general case is much more technically involved and uses in addition finiteness properties of the affine Springer fibers, a group version of a theorem of Yun
[
Yun
]
(see Section
0.10
below) and the injectivity property of the homology of affine Springer fibers established in
[
BV
]
.

(b) Next we claim that identity (
0.2
) is equivalent to the identity (
0.5
) for
\kappa=1
.
Indeed, for every
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module
V
, which is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}]
-module, and every
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have an equality (see Section
5.2.1
)

\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},V))=\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V)^{\overline{W}})=\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{w}\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V)).

Using this identity, the assertion follows from the identity
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}=\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\overline{w}_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})
.

(c) It remains to deduce identity (
0.5
) from identity (
0.4
). Assume that
G
is semi-simple and simply connected. In this case, we have a formula

(0.6)

\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}))=\sum_{\widetilde{u}}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}),

where
\widetilde{u}
runs over a set of representatives of the set of
\Lambda_{G}
-conjugacy classes in

\widetilde{W}\sigma\subseteq\widetilde{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
, whose image in
\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
equals
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
.

Indeed, this follows from a purely algebraic assertion (see Proposition
A.4.6
(b)) asserting that for every
u,\Lambda
and
M
as in Section
0.6

we have an equality

(0.7)

\operatorname{Tr}(u,H_{*}(\Lambda,M))=\sum_{\widetilde{u}}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\mu u,M),

where
\mu u
runs over the set of
\Lambda
-conjugacy classes in
\Lambda u
.

(d) To show formula (
0.7
), we note that both of its sides are additive in
M
. Set
\Lambda_{\psi}:=\Lambda/(\psi-1)\Lambda
.
Using localization theorem in equivariant
K
-theory we reduce to the case, when
M=M_{\xi}
corresponds to a character

\Lambda\rtimes\langle u\rangle\to\Lambda_{\psi}\times\langle u\rangle\to\Lambda_{\psi}\overset{\xi}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}.

In this case, we have to show that the equality

\operatorname{Tr}(\psi,H_{*}(\Lambda,M_{\xi}))=\sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_{\psi}}\lambda(\xi).

If
\xi
is non-trivial, then both sides of the last equality
vanish, while if
\xi
is trivial, both sides are equal to
\det(1-{\psi},\Lambda)=|\Lambda_{\psi}|
.

(e) By Galois cohomology arguments, the correspondence
\mathfrak{a}_{i}\mapsto\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\varphi}
defines a bijection between the set representatives of conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
, and the set of representatives of conjugacy classes in the summation of the right hand side of formula (
0.6
). Moreover, unwinding definitions we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})=\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\mathfrak{a}_{0})\rangle\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

Therefore identity (
0.5
) is an immediate consequence of the combination of formulas (
0.4
) and (
0.6
). For a general reductive group
G
, the strategy is similar.

0.10
.

A group analog of a theorem of Yun and applications.
Our arguments rely heavily on the following result,
which is essentially due to Yun
[
Yun
]
.
7
7

7

More precisely,
Yun proved the corresponding result for Lie algebras using global
methods, while we deduce its group version (see Theorem
2.3.4
) using the
topological Jordan decomposition and quasi-logarithm maps (see Appendix B).

(a) Each the homology group

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is equipped with an action of the loop group
LG^{0}_{\gamma}:=L(G_{\gamma}^{0})
of the connected centralizer

G^{0}_{\gamma}=(G_{\gamma})^{0}\subseteq G
, commuting with the action of
\Lambda_{G}
. Moreover,
this action factors through the group of connected components

\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
.

(b) Note that there is a canonical homomorphism

\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{W_{G}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
of group algebras (see Section
2.3.3
(c)).
Namely, we have natural isomorphisms
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}]

and
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{W_{G}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[c_{\widehat{G}}]
, where algebras on the right are the algebras of regular functions and

c_{\widehat{G}}
denotes the Chevalley space of
\widehat{G}
.

Under these identifications, homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
is defined to be the pullback map

(\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma})^{*}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[c_{\widehat{G}}]\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}]
, where
\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}\to c_{\widehat{G}}
denotes the composition

\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}\overset{\iota}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}\overset{\nu_{\widehat{G}}}{\longrightarrow}c_{\widehat{G}},

where
\iota
is the map
g\mapsto g^{-1}
, and
\nu_{\widehat{G}}
is the Chevalley map.

(c) Theorem of Yun asserts that there is a finite filtration of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
, stable under the action of
\Lambda_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
, such that the induced action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{W_{G}}
on each graded
piece is induced from the action of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
via
homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
.

(d) It is well-known that each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
-module.
Using the theorem of Yun from part (c) we deduce that each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a finitely
generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{W_{G}}
-module, hence a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.
8
8

8

The statement that homologies of affine Springer fibers are finitely generated over
\widetilde{W}_{G}
appears also as Conjecture 3.6 in
[
Lu3
]
. It is also
mentioned in
loc. cit.
that the statement should follow from the result of
[
Yun
]
.

This finiteness property is needed in order to make sure that the right hand side of formulas (
0.2
), (
0.4
) and (
0.5
) above
are defined.

(e) Theorem of Yun also implies that
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
acts unipotently on each homology group

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))
. Combining this fact with formula (
0.2
), stability of
\chi^{\operatorname{st}}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
follows.

9
9

9

In a work in progress
[
BKV2
]
we found an alternative (purely local) proof of the fact that each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}]
-module. However,
we do not know an alternative proof of stability.

(f) More generally, theorem of Yun plays a central role in showing that the restriction
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
is
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stable for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
. Namely, using the algebra isomorphism
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}]
,
each representation
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})
of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
gives rise to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{A}
on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
and theorem of Yun implies that
\mathcal{A}
is supported on the finite subset

(\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma})^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1}))
.

Combining this fact with formula (
0.5
) we conclude that
\chi^{\kappa}_{\lambda}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)}
is
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stable, if the center
Z(G)
of
G
is assumed to be connected. To show the assertion in general, we observe that the assertion for

G
follows from that for
G_{1}:=G\times^{Z(G)}T
and that the center of
G_{1}
is connected.

0.11
.

Comparison with
[
KV
]
.

(a) While the method of the current paper is very different from that of
[
KV
]
,
the proofs of the
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stability of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
have one common feature: though the arguments in both papers are mostly local, each of them uses one crucial ingredient, whose proof is global. Namely, the argument of
[
KV
]
is based on a generalization of a theorem of Waldspurger
[
Wa
]
asserting that Fourier transform preserves
\mathcal{E}
-stability, whose proof uses Arthur–Selberg trace formula, while the argument of the current paper is based on a theorem of Yun, described above, whose proof uses geometry of the Hitchin fibration.

(b) As our proof of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stability of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
is mostly geometric, the argument for a general
\kappa
is not significantly more difficult than the case of
\kappa=1
. On the other hand, the situation is very different in the setting of
[
KV
]
.
Namely, there the
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
-stability of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
is deduced from the fact that the restrictions of
\chi_{\lambda}^{\kappa}
to topologically unipotent elements are compatible with inner twistings, and even the formulation of the latter assertion is significantly
more difficult for
\kappa\neq 1
.

0.12
.

Remark.

It is tempting to speculate that the parallel roles of the Arthur–Selberg trace formula and of the geometry of the Hitchin fibration in the two approaches to the present story
(see Section
0.11
(a))
can be explained by a direct connection between the two theories. In particular, one can hope for a categorification of the trace formula
involving sheaves on the Hitchin space.

0.13
.

Notation and conventions.
Let
k
be an algebraically closed field, and
\ell
be a prime different from the characteristic of
k
.

(a) For a field
L
, we denote by
\overline{L}
and
L^{\operatorname{sep}}
the algebraic and the separable closure of
L
, respectively, and by
\Gamma_{L}
the absolute Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(L^{\operatorname{sep}}/L)
of
L
. We denote by
\mathbb{F}_{q}
the finite field with
q
elements and by
\sigma\in\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
the arithmetic Frobenius.

(b) Let
Y
be a scheme of finite type over
k
.
For every
\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}^{b}(Y,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we set
H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}):=H^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})^{*}
. Every morphism
f:Y^{\prime}\to Y
induces a morphism on homology
f_{*}:H_{i}(Y^{\prime},f^{*}\mathcal{F})\to H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})
. For an ind-scheme

Y=\operatorname{colim}_{i}Y_{i}
we denote by
H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})
the inductive limit
\operatorname{colim}_{i}H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F}|_{Y_{i}})
, where
\mathcal{F}|_{Y_{i}}
denoted the
*
-pullback of
\mathcal{F}
to
Y_{i}
.

(c) For every scheme
Y
of finite type over a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, we will identify every
\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}^{b}(Y,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
with the corresponding
Weil sheaf, that is, a pair
(\mathcal{F},\varphi)
, where
\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}^{b}(Y\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
and
\varphi
is an isomorphism

\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathcal{F}
. Also to simplify the notation, we will always write
H^{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})
instead of

H^{i}(Y\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q},\mathcal{F})
and similarly for
H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})
.

(d) Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
K=k((t))
, and let
G^{\operatorname{rss}}\subseteq G
be the locus of regular semisimple elements. Following Yun
[
Yun
, Section 1.4]
we assume that either
\operatorname{char}k=0
or
\operatorname{char}k>2h
, where
h
is
the Coxeter number of
G
.

(e) In Sections 4 and 5 we assume that
k=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and that

G
is a connected reductive group over
F=\mathbb{F}_{q}((t))
, usually split over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}:=F\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

0.14
.

Plan of the paper.
The paper is organized as follows:

In Section 1 we introduce basic notation and formulate a theorem of Lusztig
[
Lu1
]
describing characters of
Deligne–Lusztig representations in terms of the cohomology of Springer fibers.

In Section 2 we recall the construction of the Lusztig action of the extended affine group on the homology of the affine Springer fibers, slightly generalizing
[
Lu2
]
. We also formulate a group version of a theorem of Yun mentioned above.

In Section 3, we will establish finiteness properties of the homology affine Springer fibers, which we need later.

In Section 4 we review basic notions related to endoscopy, classify maximal unramified tori in unramified reductive groups over
p
-adic fields,
and formulate our main theorems. The proofs of these results are carried out in Section 5.

We finish this paper by two appendixes used in Section 5 and having independent interest:

In Appendix A we introduce the generalized trace, described above, and show its basic properties.

In Appendix B we study properties of the affine Springer fibers and deduce the group version of a theorem of Yun
from its original (Lie algebra) version.

0.15
.

Acknowledgements.
The idea of this work dates back to 2009,
and its present form has been influenced by the many discussions with David Kazhdan and Zhiwei Yun
held since then.
10
10

10

The first draft of this work under the title “Homology of affine Springer fibers, characters of Deligne–Lusztig representations, and stability”, appeared around 2011 and was cited in
[
Yun
]
and
[
BKV1
]
.
It is a pleasure to express our gratitude to both of them.
Our key point here is reduction of stability to a geometric question resolved in
[
Yun
]
, so we are also grateful to Yun for this contribution that made our work possible.
The first author wants to thank Bob Kottwitz for discussions that influenced his understanding of the subject reflected, in particular, in this work.

1.
Springer fibers and Deligne–Lusztig representations

In this section we will recall mostly standard facts concerning action of the Weyl group on the cohomology of Springer fibers
and its relation with Deligne–Lusztig representations.

1.1.
The abstract Cartan and the Weyl group

Let
H
be a connected algebraic group over an algebraically closed field
k
.

1.1.1
.

The construction
. Following
[
DL
, Section 1.1]
, we define the abstract Cartan
T_{H}
of

H
, the Weyl group
W_{H}
of
H
, and the set of simple reflections
S_{H}\subseteq W_{H}
.

(a) For every pair
(T,B)
, where
B\subseteq H
is a Borel subgroup and
T\subseteq B
is a maximal torus, we set
W_{H,T}:=N_{H}(T)/T
, where
N_{H}(T)\subseteq H
is the normalizer of
T
, and let
S_{H,B,T}\subseteq W_{H,T}
be the corresponding set of simple reflections. Then group
W_{H,T}
acts on
T
by conjugation, and the inclusion of groups
N_{T}(H)\hookrightarrow H
induces a bijection of sets
W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}B\backslash H/B
.

(b) For every two pairs
(B,T)
and
(B^{\prime},T^{\prime})
as in part (a),
there exists a unique isomorphism
\phi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T^{\prime}
, which is of the form

t\mapsto hth^{-1}
for some
h\in H
such that
h(B,T)h^{-1}=(B^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.
Then isomorphism
\phi
induces a group isomorphism
W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H,T^{\prime}}
and a bijections
S_{H,T,B}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}S_{H,T^{\prime},B^{\prime}}
.

(c) We denote by
T_{H}
the projective limit
\lim_{(B,T)}T
, taken over the set of pairs

(B,T)
as in part (a) where the transition maps are as in part (b), and call
T_{H}
the
abstract Cartan
of
H
.
Similarly, we set
W_{H}:=\lim_{(B,T)}W_{H,T}
and call it the
abstract Weyl group
of
H
, and
denote by
S_{H}:=\lim_{(B,T)}S_{H,B,T}\subseteq W_{H}
the
set of simple reflections
in
W_{H}
.
Note that
T_{H}
is a torus, and the Weyl group
W_{H}
naturally acts on
T_{H}
.

1.1.2
.

Properties.

(a) By construction, for each pair
(B,T)
as in Section
1.1.1
(a), we have a canonical isomorphism
\varphi_{T,B}:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}

of tori,
a canonical isomorphism
W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H}
of groups, and a canonical bijection
S_{H,T,B}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}S_{H}
of sets.

(b) For every pair
(T,B)
as in Section
1.1.1
(a), we have a natural isomorphism of tori

T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}B/R_{u}(B)
, defined to be the composition
T\hookrightarrow B\to B/R_{u}(B)
. Moreover, the composition
B/R_{u}(B)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T\overset{\varphi_{T,B}}{\longrightarrow}T_{H}
is independent of
T
. We denote by
\operatorname{pr}_{B}
the composition

\operatorname{pr}_{B}:B\to B/R_{u}(B)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
.

(c) For every two Borel subgroups
B,B^{\prime}\subseteq H
, we can define
their relative position
w_{B,B^{\prime}}\in W_{H}
to be the image of

h\in H
such that
hBh^{-1}=B^{\prime}
under the composition
H\to B\backslash H/B\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H}
(for some maximal torus
T\subseteq B
).

By direct calculation, one sees that
w_{B,B^{\prime}}
is independent
of the choice of
T\subseteq B
, and that for every three Borel
subgroups
B,B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}
containing a maximal torus
T
, we have an equality

w_{B,B^{\prime\prime}}=w_{B,B^{\prime}}\cdot w_{B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}}
.

(d) Consider the
Chevalley space

c_{H}:=\operatorname{Spec}k[H]^{H}
of
H
, where
H
acts on
k[H]
via the adjoint action,
and let
\nu_{H}
be the natural projection
H\to c_{H}
.

Then
\nu_{H}

induces a morphisms
\nu_{T_{H}}:T_{H}\to c_{H}
defined as a limit of morphisms

\nu_{H}|_{T}:T\to c_{H}
, taken over all
(T,B)
as in Section
1.1.1
(a).

Moreover, morphism
\nu_{T_{H}}
induces an isomorphism
W_{H}\backslash T_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}c_{H}
, and the restriction

\nu^{\operatorname{rss}}_{T_{H}}:T^{\operatorname{rss}}_{H}\to c^{\operatorname{rss}}_{H}
to the
regular semisimple locus
c_{H}^{\operatorname{rss}}\subseteq c_{H}
is a
W_{H}
-torsor.

1.1.3
.

Admissible isomorphisms.
Let
T\subseteq H
be a maximal torus.

(a) We say that an isomorphism
\varphi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
of tori is
admissible
,
if there exists a Borel subgroup
B\supseteq T
of
H

such that
\varphi=\varphi_{T,B}
(see Section
1.1.2
(a)).

(b) Note that for every two Borel subgroups
B,B^{\prime}\supseteq T
, we have a
formula
\varphi_{T,B^{\prime}}=w_{B,B^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,B}
.
Therefore the set of admissible isomorphisms is a
W_{H}
-torsor,
and the map
B\mapsto\varphi_{T,B}
is a bijection between the set of
Borel subgroups of
H
containing
T
and the set of admissible isomorphisms

T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
.

(c) Note that every admissible isomorphism
\varphi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
induces an isomorphism

\varphi^{-1}_{*}:X_{*}(T_{H})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T)
between groups of cocharacters, hence an isomorphism

\varphi^{-1}_{*}:L[X_{*}(T_{H})]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}L[X_{*}(T)]
between the corresponding group algebras
for every field
L
. Moreover, since the set of admissible isomorphisms is a
W_{H}
-torsor (see part (b)),
its restriction

\varphi_{*}^{-1}|_{L[X_{*}(T_{H})]^{W_{H}}}:L[X_{*}(T_{H})]^{W_{H}}\hookrightarrow L[X_{*}(T_{H})]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}L[X_{*}(T)]

to the algebra of
W_{H}
-invariants is independent of
\varphi
, and makes
L[X_{*}(T)]
a finite
L[X_{*}(T_{H})]^{W_{H}}
-algebra.

1.1.4
.

Remark.
Notice that an isomorphism
\varphi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
of tori is admissible if and only if

\nu_{T_{H}}\circ\varphi=\nu_{H}|_{T}
. Indeed, for every admissible isomorphism
\varphi
, the equality
\nu_{T_{H}}\circ\varphi_{T,B}=\varphi|_{T}

follows by the definition of
\nu_{T_{H}}
(see Section
1.1.2
(d)). Thus the
assertion follows from the fact that
\operatorname{Aut}_{c_{H}}(T_{H})=W_{H}
, and that the set of
admissible isomorphisms is a
W_{H}
-torsor.

1.1.5
.

Rationality.
Let
H
be a connected reductive group over
k
, defined over its subfield
f\subseteq k
.
Then we claim that the abstract Cartan
T_{H}
has a natural structure of a torus over
f
, the Weyl group
W_{H}
is equipped with a
\Gamma_{f}
-action, the action of
W_{H}
on
T_{H}
is
\Gamma_{f}
-equivariant, and the subset
S_{H}\subseteq W_{H}
is
\Gamma_{f}
-invariant.
Furthermore,
T_{H}
is split over
f
, if
H
is split over
f
.

Proof.

First we claim that if
H
splits over
f
, then
T_{H}
has a natural structure of a split torus over
f
. Indeed, choose a maximal torus
T
of
H
split over
f
and a Borel subgroup
B\supseteq T
of
H
. Then the canonical isomorphism
\varphi_{T,B}:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
from Section
1.1.2
(a)) gives to
T_{H}
a structure of a split torus over
f
, and it is easy to see that this structure is independent of the choice of
(T,B)
.

Since
H
splits over
f^{\operatorname{sep}}
, it follows from the proven above that
T_{H}
has a natural structure of a torus over
f^{\operatorname{sep}}
. Next we claim that for every element
\tau\in\Gamma_{f}
of the absolute Galois group of
f
we have natural isomorphisms
{}^{\tau}T_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
and
\tau:W_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H}
.

Choose a maximal torus
T
of
H
defined (and hence split) over
f^{\operatorname{sep}}
and a Borel subgroup
B\supseteq T
of
H
(automatically defined over
f^{\operatorname{sep}}
). Then the Galois conjugates
{}^{\tau}B\subseteq H
and
{}^{\tau}T\subseteq{}^{\tau}B
are a Borel subgroup and a maximal torus of
H
as well, and it is easy to see that the composition

\varphi_{{}^{\tau}T,{}^{\tau}B}\circ{}^{\tau}\varphi_{T,B}^{-1}:{}^{\tau}T_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}{}^{\tau}T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}

is independent of the choice of
(T,B)
.

Similarly, the map
\tau:T\to{}^{\tau}T:t\mapsto{}^{\tau}t
induces a group isomorphism

\tau:W_{H,T}=N_{H}(T)/T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}N_{H}({}^{\tau}T)/{}^{\tau}T=W_{H,{}^{\tau}T},

and the composition

W_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H,{}^{\tau}T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H}

is independent of the choice of
(T,B)
. Finally, the assertion about
S_{H}
follows from the fact that isomorphism

\tau:W_{H,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{H,{}^{\tau}T}
maps
S_{H,B,T}
to
S_{H,{}^{\tau}B,{}^{\tau}T}
.
∎

1.2.
The Grothendieck–Springer resolution and the
W_{H}
-action

Suppose that we are in the situation of Section
1.1
.

1.2.1
.

Construction.

(a) Note that we have a commutative diagram

(1.1)

\begin{CD}[\frac{B}{B}]@>{\operatorname{pr}_{B}}>{}>T_{H}\\
@V{[p_{H}]}V{}V@V{}V{\nu_{T_{H}}}V\\
[\frac{H}{H}]@>{\nu_{H}}>{}>c_{H}\end{CD}

of Artin stacks, where the top horizontal morphism is a composition of the map
[\frac{B}{B}]\to[\frac{T_{H}}{T_{H}}]
, induced by

\operatorname{pr}_{B}
(see Section
1.1.1
(c)) with the canonical projection
[\frac{T_{H}}{T_{H}}]\to T_{H}
, and morphism
[p_{H}]
is induced by the inclusion
B\hookrightarrow H
.

(b) Note that Artin stack
[\frac{B}{B}]
is smooth of dimension zero, while morphism
[p_{H}]
is proper and small.
Therefore for every pair of local systems
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
on
[\frac{B}{B}]
, the
push-forwards
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{1}:=[p_{H}]_{*}\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{2}:=[p_{H}]_{*}\mathcal{F}_{2}
are perverse
sheaves on
[\frac{H}{H}]
, and the restriction map

\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{1}|_{U},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{2}|_{U})

is an isomorphism for every open dense substack
U\subseteq[\frac{H}{H}]
.

(c) Let
\mathcal{L}\in D_{c}^{b}(T_{H},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
be a local system. Since
\nu_{T_{H}}\circ w=\nu_{T_{H}}
for each
w\in W_{H}
, we
have a canonical isomorphism

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}:(\nu_{T_{H}})_{*}\mathcal{L}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}(\nu_{T_{H}})_{*}(w_{*}\mathcal{L}).

Moreover, for
w_{1},w_{2}\in W_{H}
, the isomorphism
(w_{1}w_{2})_{*}\mathcal{L}\simeq(w_{1})_{*}(w_{2})_{*}\mathcal{L}
identifies

a_{w_{1}w_{2},\mathcal{L}}
with
a_{w_{1},(w_{2})_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{w_{2},\mathcal{L}}
.

(d) Consider local system
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}:=\operatorname{pr}_{B}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
on
[\frac{B}{B}]
, and set
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{L}}:=[p_{H}]_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
. Notice that over the regular semisimple locus
c^{\operatorname{rss}}_{H}
the
diagram (
1.1
) is Cartesian. Therefore it follows from
proper base change that the isomorphism
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}

from part (c) induces an isomorphism

a^{\operatorname{rss}}_{w,\mathcal{L}}:\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{L}}|_{[\frac{H^{\operatorname{rss}}}{H}]}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{w_{*}\mathcal{L}}|_{[\frac{H^{\operatorname{rss}}}{H}]}.

(e) It now follows from part (b) that the isomorphism
a^{\operatorname{rss}}_{w,\mathcal{L}}
from part (d) uniquely extends
to an isomorphism
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{L}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{w_{*}\mathcal{L}}
, which we will denote again by

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}
.

1.2.2
.

Springer fibers.

(a) For a Borel subgroup
B\subseteq H
, we set
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}:=H/B
. Note that if
B^{\prime}\subseteq H
is another Borel subgroup, then there exists

g\in H
such that
B^{\prime}=gBg^{-1}
, and the map
hB\mapsto hBg^{-1}=hg^{-1}B^{\prime}
defines an isomorphism
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B^{\prime}}
,
independent of the choice
g
.

We set
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}:=\lim_{B}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}
and call it the
flag variety
of
H
. By definition, we have a
canonical isomorphism
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}
for every Borel subgroup
B\subseteq H
.

(b) The group
H
acts naturally on each
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}
, and that each isomorphism
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B^{\prime}}
is
H
-equivariant.
Therefore the group
H
naturally acts on
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}
such that the identification
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H,B}
from part (a) is
H
-equivariant.

(c) For every element
\gamma\in H
, we denote by
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma}\subseteq\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}
the scheme of fixed points of

\gamma
, called the
Springer fiber
. Then for every Borel subgroup
B\subseteq H
, we have a Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma}@>{}>{}>\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{H}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{pt}\\
@V{\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}V{}V@V{\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}V{}V@V{}V{\eta_{\gamma}}V\\
[\frac{B}{B}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{H}{B}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{H}{H}],\end{CD}

where
\eta_{\gamma}
is the map corresponding to
\gamma
, and
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}
is the map
hB\mapsto[h^{-1}\gamma h]
.

(d) For a local system
\mathcal{L}
as in Section
1.2.1
(c), let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
be as in Section
1.2.1
(d), and denote the pullback
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\in D_{c}^{b}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
again by
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
.

Notice that it follows from proper base change applying to the Cartesian diagram of part (c) that the fiber of
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{L}}
at
[\gamma]\in[\frac{H}{H}]
is naturally isomorphic to
H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. Therefore the isomorphism
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}
of Section
1.2.1
(e) induces an isomorphism

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}:H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{w_{*}\mathcal{L}}).

1.3.
Characters of Deligne-Lusztig representations

Let
H
be a connected reductive group over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, defined over a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.

1.3.1
.

Maximal subtori over finite fields.

(a) Following
Deligne-Lusztig (
[
DL
]
) we consider pairs
(T,B)
,
where
T\subseteq H
is a maximal torus defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and

B\supseteq T
is a Borel subgroup of
H
defined over

\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Then
{}^{\sigma}B\supseteq T
is another Borel
subgroup of
H
, and we let
w_{B}
be the relative
position
w_{B,{}^{\sigma}B}\in W_{H}
(see Section
1.1.1
(d)).

Deligne–Lusztig showed that the correspondence

(T,B)\mapsto w_{B}
is a bijection between
conjugacy classes of pairs
(B,T)
as above and elements of
W_{H}
.

(b) Recall that the correspondence

B\mapsto\varphi_{T,B}
gives a bijection between
Borel subgroups
B\subseteq H
containing
T
and
admissible isomorphisms
T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
(see Section
1.1.3
(b)).
For every admissible isomorphisms
\varphi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
, we
define
B_{\varphi}\supseteq T
to be the unique Borel
subgroup such that
\varphi_{T,B_{\varphi}}=\varphi
, and
set
w_{\varphi}:=w_{B_{\varphi}}
.

(c) Using the identity

{}^{\sigma}\varphi_{T,B}=\varphi_{T,{}^{\sigma}B}=w_{B,{}^{\sigma}B}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,B}=w_{B}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,B}

(see
Section
1.1.3
(b)) for each pair
(T,B)
as in part (a), we get
that
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi
for
each pair
(T,\varphi)
as in part (b).

(d) Let
X_{*}(T):=\operatorname{Hom}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{m},T)
and

X_{*}(T_{H}):=\operatorname{Hom}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}(\mathbb{G}_{m},T_{H})
be the groups of
cocharacters defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. By part (c), we have an equality

\sigma\circ\varphi\circ\sigma^{-1}=w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi:X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T_{H}),

hence an equality

w_{\varphi}\circ\sigma=\varphi\circ\sigma\circ\varphi^{-1}:X_{*}(T_{H})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T_{H}).

In particular, a torus
T
is unisotropic if and only if

X_{*}(T)^{\sigma}=\{0\}
if and only if
X_{*}(T_{H})^{w_{\varphi}\circ\sigma}=\{0\}
.

1.3.2
.

Notation.
(a) Let
T\subseteq H
be a maximal torus defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, let

{\theta}:T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
be a character, and let

\varphi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{H}
be an admissible isomorphism (see
Section
1.1.3
(a)).

(b) Recall that character
\theta
gives rise to the one-dimensional local
system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
on
T
. Then isomorphism
\varphi
gives
rise to local systems

\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta})
on
T_{H}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}}
on
[\frac{B}{B}]
, hence to the perverse sheaf
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}}
on
[\frac{H}{H}]
(see Section
1.2.1
(d)).

(c) Note that local system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
is equipped with an isomorphism
\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
. Therefore using Section
1.3.1
(c) we have
a natural isomorphism

\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}=\sigma_{*}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}=({}^{\sigma}\varphi)_{*}\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}({}^{\sigma}\varphi)_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta}=\mathcal{L}_{\theta,{}^{\sigma}\varphi}=\mathcal{L}_{\theta,w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi},

which induces isomorphisms

\sigma_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi}
and
\sigma_{*}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi}
.

(d) Similarly, for every
w\in W_{H}
we have an isomorphism

w_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,w\circ\varphi}
, hence by Section
1.2.1
(e) it
induces an isomorphism

a_{w,\theta,\varphi}=a_{w,\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}}:\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,w\circ\varphi}.

(e) Composing isomorphisms of parts (c) and (d), one get an isomorphism

a_{\theta,\varphi}:\sigma_{*}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,w_{\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}.

(f) As in Section
1.2.2
(d), for every
\gamma\in H(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, we denote the pullback
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}
on
\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma}
by

\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}
. Recall that the fiber of
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}
at point
[\gamma]\in[\frac{H}{H}]
is isomorphic to

H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
. Therefore, taking the fiber at
[\gamma]
of the isomorphism
a_{\theta,\varphi}
of part (e), we get an isomorphism

a_{\theta,\varphi,\gamma}:H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}).

1.3.3
.

Remark.
Let
\operatorname{Fr}_{q}:[\frac{H}{H}]\to[\frac{H}{H}]
be the
geometric Frobenius morphism over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Then we have a
canonical isomorphism
\sigma_{*}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fr}_{q}^{*}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}
. Therefore the isomorphism
a_{\theta,\varphi}
of Section
1.3.2
(e) can be viewed as

\operatorname{Fr}^{*}_{q}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta,\varphi}
.

The following important result of Lusztig (see
[
Lu1
, Propositions 8.15 and 9.2]
or
[
Lau
, Corollary 2.3.2]
) is crucial for our
work.

Theorem 1.3.4
.

Assume that there exists a character
\theta_{0}:T(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
in general position, and
let
R_{T}^{\theta}
be the virtual Deligne–Lusztig representation of
H(\mathbb{F}_{q})
,
corresponding to a pair
(T,\theta)
. Then for every
\gamma\in H(\mathbb{F}_{q})
we have an equality

(1.2)

\operatorname{Tr}(R_{T}^{\theta}(\gamma))=\operatorname{Tr}(a_{\theta,\varphi,\gamma},H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}\operatorname{Tr}(a_{\theta,\varphi,\gamma},H^{i}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

2.
Affine Weyl groups, and homology of the affine Springer fibers

2.1.
The extended affine Weyl group

2.1.1
.

Set up.

(a) Let
K:=k((t))
be the field of Laurent power series over
k
, and denote by

\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}_{K}=k[[t]]
the ring of integers.

(b) Let
G
be a split reductive connected group over
K
, and let

G^{\operatorname{sc}}
be the simply connected covering of the derived group
G^{\operatorname{der}}
of
G
.

(c) For a split torus
T
over
K
, we denote by
T_{\mathcal{O}}
the corresponding torus
over
\mathcal{O}
and by
\overline{T}
the special fiber of
T_{\mathcal{O}}
, also referred as the
reduction
of
T
.

(d) Let
LG
be the loop group of
G
, and let
\mathbf{T}:=L^{+}(T_{\mathcal{O}})
be the arc-group.
We denote by
\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{P}
the Iwahori and the parahoric subgroup schemes of
LG
, respectively.

2.1.2
.

Construction.
Generalizing the construction of Section
1.1.1
(a), we are going to define
the extended affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}_{G}
of
G
, its subgroup
\Lambda_{G}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
, the set of simple affine refections
\widetilde{S}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
, the affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
, the decomposition
\widetilde{W}_{G}\simeq\widetilde{W}\rtimes\Omega_{G}
, and the torus
\overline{T}_{G}
over
k
.

(a) Consider the set of pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
, there
T\subseteq G
is a
maximal split torus over
K
and
\mathbf{I}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
an
Iwahori subgroup of
LG
. We set
\widetilde{W}_{G,T}:=N_{LG}(LT)/\mathbf{T}=N_{LG}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}
,
\Lambda_{G,T}:=LT/\mathbf{T}=N_{LG}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G,T}
,
let
\overline{T}_{G,T}
be a torus over
k
defined to be the reduction of
T
, set
\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}}:=N_{LG}(\mathbf{I})/\mathbf{I}
,
and let
\widetilde{S}_{T,\mathbf{I}}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G,T}
be the set of simple affine reflections, corresponding to
\mathbf{I}
.

(b) For each two such pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
as in part (a),
there exists a unique isomorphism of tori
\phi:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T^{\prime}
such that

\phi
is the map
t\mapsto gtg^{-1}
for some
g\in L(G^{\operatorname{sc}})

such that
g\mathbf{I}g^{-1}=\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
. Again, isomorphism
\phi
induces isomorphisms

\widetilde{W}_{G,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}_{G,T^{\prime}}
,
\Lambda_{G,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Lambda_{G,T^{\prime}}
,
\overline{T}_{G,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G,T^{\prime}}
,
\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
and a bijection

\widetilde{S}_{T,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{S}_{T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
.

(c) By analogy to the finite-dimensional case, we set
\widetilde{W}_{G}:=\lim_{T,\mathbf{I}}\widetilde{W}_{G,T}
,
\Lambda_{G}:=\lim_{T,\mathbf{I}}\Lambda_{G,T}
,

\Omega_{G}:=\lim_{T,\mathbf{I}}\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}}
,
\widetilde{S}:=\lim_{T,I}\widetilde{S}_{T,\mathbf{I}}
, and
\overline{T}_{G}:=\lim_{T,\mathbf{I}}\overline{T}_{G,T}
.

(d) We denote by
\widetilde{W}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
the subgroup, generated by
\widetilde{S}
. It is easy to see that
the subgroup
\widetilde{W}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
is normal, that the canonical morphism
\widetilde{W}\rtimes\Omega_{G}\to\widetilde{W}_{G}

is an isomorphism, and that we have a canonical isomorphism
\widetilde{W}\simeq\widetilde{W}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
.

(e) For every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, we denote by
W_{J}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
the subgroup, generated by
J
.

2.1.3
.

Properties.

(a) For each pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a), we have a canonical
isomorphisms
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
,
\widetilde{W}_{G,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}_{G}
,
\Lambda_{G,T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Lambda_{G}
and a bijection
\widetilde{S}_{T,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{S}
.

(b) We denote by
\overline{W}=\overline{W}_{G}:=\widetilde{W}_{G}/\Lambda_{G}
the quotient group and
by
\pi_{G}:\widetilde{W}_{G}\to\overline{W}_{G}
the natural projection. The group

\overline{W}
naturally and faithfully acts on
\overline{T}_{G}
. For each pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a),
the isomorphisms of part (a) induce an isomorphism
\overline{W}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}N_{LG}(LT)/LT\simeq N_{G}(T)/T
.

(c) We claim that there is a natural isomorphism of groups
\Lambda_{G}\simeq X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G})
, where
X_{*}(-)

denotes the group of cocharacters (over an algebraic closure). Namely, for every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a), we have
an isomorphism
X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LT/\mathbf{T}
of groups, which maps
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)
to the class of
\lambda(t)\in T(k((t)))=LT(k)
.
Moreover, this isomorphism together with those of part (a) gives rise to the composition

\Lambda_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Lambda_{G,T}=LT/\mathbf{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G}),

which is independent of the choice of
(T,\mathbf{I})
.

(d) For every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a), the composition
\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
, where
\mathbf{I}^{+}\subseteq\mathbf{I}
is the pro-unipotent radical, induces an isomorphism
\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
of tori over
k
. Moreover, the composite isomorphism
\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}
is independent of
T
.
We denote by
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}
the composition
\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
.

(e) For every two Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
of
G
, we can
define their relative position
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{W}
to be
the image of an element
g\in LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
such that
g\mathbf{I}g^{-1}=\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
under the
composition
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}\to\mathbf{I}\backslash LG^{\operatorname{sc}}/\mathbf{I}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}
. Also we denote by
\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\in\overline{W}
the
projection of
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{W}
to
\overline{W}
. As in the finite-dimensional case, for
every three Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}
containing
\mathbf{T}
, we have an equality

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}
.

2.1.4
.

A canonical isomorphism.

Consider triple
(T,\mathbf{I},B)
, where
(T,\mathbf{I})
is an Section
2.1.2
(a), and
B\supseteq T
is a Borel subgroup of
G
. This triple gives rise to an isomorphism of groups

\phi:W_{G}\overset{\ref{E:weylprop}(a)}{\longrightarrow}W_{G,T}=N_{G}(T)/T\overset{\ref{E:prop}(b)}{\longrightarrow}\overline{W}_{G}

and to a
\phi
-equivariant isomorphism of groups

\psi:X_{*}(T_{G})\overset{\varphi^{-1}_{T,B}}{\longrightarrow}X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T})\overset{\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}}{\longrightarrow}X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G})\overset{\ref{E:prop}(c)}{\longrightarrow}\Lambda_{G},

where
X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T})
is a canonical isomorphism. Moreover,
\psi
gives rise to a
\phi
-equivariant isomorphism of groups algebras

L[X_{*}(T_{G})]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}L[\Lambda_{G}]
for every field
L
, hence to an isomorphism of algebras

(2.1)

L[X_{*}(T_{G})]^{W_{G}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}L[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G}}.

Furthermore, isomorphism (
2.1
) is independent of the choice of
(T,\mathbf{I},B)
.

2.1.5
.

Admissible isomorphisms
.

(a) Let
T\subseteq G
be a split
maximal torus over
K
. We say that an isomorphism

\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
is
admissible
, if there
exists an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
of
G
such that

\varphi=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}
(see Section
2.1.3
(a)).

(b) For every two Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
, we
have a formula
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}
. In particular, every two admissible isomorphisms

\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
are
\overline{W}
-conjugate.

2.1.6
.

Parahoric subgroups.

(a) For each parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}

of
LG
, let
\mathbf{P}^{+}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
be the pro-unipotent radical.
Then
M_{\mathbf{P}}:=\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{+}
is a connected reductive group over
k
.

(b) For every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a) satisfying
\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
, the quotient
\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}:=\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{P}^{+}
is a Borel subgroup of
M_{\mathbf{P}}
, and the composition

\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{P}^{+}=\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}

induces an embedding
\overline{T}\hookrightarrow\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}
, identifying
\overline{T}
with a maximal torus of
\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}
.

(c) We claim that there is a natural isomorphism
\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}
and a natural embedding
W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{W}_{G}
.
Namely, for every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in part (b), the projection
\mathbf{P}\to M_{\mathbf{P}}
induces isomorphisms

(2.2)

\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\ref{E:prop}(d)}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}/R_{u}(\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}})\overset{\ref{E:weylprop}(b)}{\longrightarrow}T_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}

and

N_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}N_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}(\overline{T})/\overline{T}\overset{\ref{E:weylprop}(a)}{\longrightarrow}W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}.

Moreover, both isomorphism (
2.2
) and the composition

W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}N_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow N_{LG}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\overset{\ref{E:prop}(a)}{\longrightarrow}\widetilde{W}_{G}

are independent of the choice of
(T,\mathbf{I})
.

By construction, the embedding
W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{W}_{G}
maps
S_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}
into
\widetilde{S}
.

(d) We denote by
J_{\mathbf{P}}\subseteq\widetilde{S}
the image of the embedding
S_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{S}
from part (c) and call
J_{\mathbf{P}}
the
type
of
\mathbf{P}
. Then the embedding
W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{W}_{G}
induces an isomorphism
W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}W_{J_{\mathbf{P}}}
.

(e) For every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
, the correspondence
\mathbf{P}\mapsto J_{\mathbf{P}}
induces
a bijection between parahoric subgroups
\mathbf{P}\supseteq\mathbf{I}
and proper subsets
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
. The inverse map we denote
by
J\mapsto\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
.

(f) Let
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
be two Iwahori subgroups of
LG
contained in

\mathbf{P}
. Then
\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}
and
\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{P}}
are
two Borel subgroups of
M_{\mathbf{P}}
(see part (b)), and its relative position

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
equals the image of
w_{\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}},\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{P}}}\in W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}
under the embedding of part (c).

Indeed, choose
g\in\mathbf{P}
such that
g\mathbf{I}g^{-1}=\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
.
Then the image
\overline{g}\in M_{\mathbf{P}}
of
g
satisfies

\overline{g}\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}\overline{g}^{-1}=\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{P}}
, so the assertion follows from
definitions of relative positions (see Sections
1.1.1
(d) and
2.1.2
(e)).

2.1.7
.

Affine roots.
To every
G
as above, one can associate the set of roots
\overline{\Phi}=\overline{\Phi}_{G}
of
G
, the set of affine roots
\widetilde{\Phi}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{G}
and a subset of positive affine roots
\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}\subseteq\widetilde{\Phi}
:

(a) To every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a) we denote by
\overline{\Phi}_{T}\subseteq X^{*}(\overline{T}_{G})
the image of the set of roots

\Phi(G,T)\subseteq X^{*}(T)
under the canonical isomorphism
X^{*}(T)\simeq X^{*}(\overline{T})\simeq X^{*}(\overline{T}_{G})
.

The torus
T
acts on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}G
, which induces a decomposition
\mathfrak{g}=\bigoplus_{\overline{\alpha}\in\{0\}\cup\Phi_{T}}\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{\alpha}}
.
Next, we denote by
\widetilde{\Phi}_{T}
be the set of pairs
\alpha=(\overline{\alpha},\mathcal{L})
, where
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}_{T}
and
\mathcal{L}\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{\alpha}}
is an
\mathcal{O}
-lattice. Finally, we denote by
\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}_{T,\mathbf{I}}\subseteq\widetilde{\Phi}_{T}
be the set of affine roots
\alpha=(\overline{\alpha},\mathcal{L})
such that
\mathcal{L}
is contained in
\mathfrak{I}:=\operatorname{Lie}\mathbf{I}
.

(b) Note that if
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
is another pair as in Section
2.1.2
(a), then the canonical isomorphism
T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T^{\prime}
from Section
2.1.2
(b) induces bijections
\overline{\Phi}_{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\Phi}_{T^{\prime}}
,
\widetilde{\Phi}_{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{\Phi}_{T^{\prime}}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}_{T,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}_{T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
.

Thus, as in Section
