2.1.2
(b), we set
\overline{\Phi}:=\lim_{(T,\mathbf{I})}\overline{\Phi}_{T}
,
\widetilde{\Phi}:=\lim_{(T,\mathbf{I})}\widetilde{\Phi}_{T}
and

\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}:=\lim_{(T,\mathbf{I})}\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}_{T,\mathbf{I}}
. For
\alpha\in\widetilde{\Phi}
we write
\alpha>0
(resp.
\alpha<0
) if

\alpha\in\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}
(resp.
\alpha\notin\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}
).

2.1.8
.

Splitting.

Let
\mathbf{P}\subseteq LG
be a hyperspecial parahoric subgroup.

(a) We claim that a choice of
\mathbf{P}
gives rise to a subset
\overline{\Phi}^{+}\subseteq\overline{\Phi}
of
positive
roots and an identification
\widetilde{\Phi}\simeq\overline{\Phi}\times\mathbb{Z}
such that
\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}
corresponds to pairs
(\alpha,r)\in\overline{\Phi}\times\mathbb{Z}
such that either
r>0
or (
r=0
and
\alpha\in\overline{\Phi}^{+}
).

Namely, let
(T,\mathbf{I})
be as in Section
2.1.7
(a) such that
\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
. Since
\mathbf{P}
is hyperspecial, the canonical isomorphism

X^{*}(\overline{T}_{G})\simeq X^{*}(\overline{T})
induces a bijection between
\overline{\Phi}_{T}
and the set of roots
\Phi(M_{\mathbf{P}},\overline{T})
of
M_{\mathbf{P}}
with respect to
\overline{T}
. Then a subset
\overline{\Phi}^{+}\subseteq\overline{\Phi}
corresponds under this bijection to a subset
\Phi^{+}(M_{\mathbf{P}},\overline{T},\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}})
of positive roots
\Phi(M_{\mathbf{P}},\overline{T})
relative to the Borel subgroup
\overline{B}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{P}}=\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{P}^{+}\subseteq M_{\mathbf{P}}
.

Moreover, the bijection
\widetilde{\Phi}_{G}\simeq\overline{\Phi}\times\mathbb{Z}
is characterised by the condition that
(\overline{\alpha},\mathcal{L})\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{T}

corresponds to a pair
(\overline{\alpha},r)\in\overline{\Phi}_{T}\times\mathbb{Z}
, where
r
is the smallest integer such that
t^{r}\mathcal{L}\subseteq\operatorname{Lie}\mathbf{P}
.

(b) A choice of
\mathbf{P}
defines a section of the projection
\pi_{G}:\widetilde{W}_{G}\to\overline{W}
, thus gives rise to decompositions
\widetilde{W}_{G}\simeq\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\overline{W}
and
\widetilde{W}\simeq\Lambda\rtimes\overline{W}
, where we set
\Lambda:=\Lambda_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
. Namely, for each pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in part (a), notice that the composition

\zeta:N_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow N_{LG}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}=N_{LG}(LT)/\mathbf{T}\to N_{LG}(LT)/LT\overset{\ref{E:prop}(b)}{\underset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\overline{W}

is an isomorphism, and the composition

s:\overline{W}\overset{\zeta^{-1}}{\underset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}N_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow N_{LG}(\mathbf{T})/\mathbf{T}\overset{\ref{E:prop}(a)}{\underset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{W}_{G}

is a section of
\pi_{G}
. Moreover, section
s
is independent of the choice of
(T,\mathbf{I})
.

The following variant of a notion of
[
BV
]
plays a central role in Theorem
3.3.15
below.

2.1.9
.

m
-regularity.

(a) For every elements
w\in\widetilde{W}
and
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
, we denote by
|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|
the number of all affine roots

\alpha\in\widetilde{\Phi}
such that
\alpha=(\overline{\alpha},\mathcal{L})
and we have

\text{ either }(\alpha>0\text{ and }w^{-1}(\alpha)<0)\text{ or }(\alpha<0\text{ and }w^{-1}(\alpha)>0).

(b) Notice that the expression
|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|
coincides with the absolute value of the usual pairing
\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle

when
w=\lambda\in\Lambda\subseteq\widetilde{W}
.

(c) Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We say that an element
w\in\widetilde{W}
is
m
-regular
if for every
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
we have
|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|\geq m
.

(d) By definition, for every elements
w\in\widetilde{W}
and
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
, we have an equality

|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|=|\langle w^{-1}(\overline{\alpha}),w^{-1}\rangle|.

Therefore an element
w
is
m
-regular if and only if element
w^{-1}
is
m
-regular.

2.1.10
.

Geometric description.
The expression
|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|
from Section
2.1.9
(a) has the following geometric description:

Choose a pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.7
(a), then we have an equality
\operatorname{Ad}g(\mathfrak{I})=\mathfrak{I}
for every
g\in\mathbf{T}
,
thus we can form an
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
\operatorname{Ad}w(\mathfrak{I})\subseteq\mathfrak{g}
, and we set
\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}}:=\mathfrak{I}\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}}:=\operatorname{Ad}w(\mathfrak{I})\cap\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{\alpha}}
.

Then
\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}}
be two
\mathcal{O}
-lattices in a one-dimensional vector
K
-space
\mathfrak{g}_{\overline{\alpha}}
,
and the expression
|\langle\overline{\alpha},w\rangle|
is equal to
\dim_{k}(\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}}/\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}})
, if
\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}}\subseteq\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}}
, and is equal to
\dim_{k}(\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}}/\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}})
, if
\mathfrak{I}_{w,\overline{\alpha}}\supseteq\mathfrak{I}_{\overline{\alpha}}
.

2.1.11
.

m
-regularity in the sense of
[
BV
]
.

(a) Assume that we are given a parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}\subseteq LG
thus a splitting
\widetilde{W}\simeq\Lambda\rtimes\overline{W}
(see Section
2.1.8
(b)). In this case, we have another (more elementary) notion of
m
-regularity, used in
[
BV
]
.
Namely, an element
\lambda u\in\widetilde{W}
with
\lambda\in\Lambda
and
u\in\overline{W}
is called
m
-regular in the sense of
[
BV
, Introduction]
if for every

\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
, we have an inequality
|\langle\overline{\alpha},\lambda\rangle|\geq m
.

(b) Clearly, the notion of
m
-regularity introduced in Section
2.1.9
is not equivalent to that of
[
BV
, Introduction]
.
Moreover, the notion of
[
BV
]
depends on a choice of parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}\subseteq LG
, and thus less canonical and does not satisfy a crucial property of Section
4.2.2
(d) we need.

(c) In the situation of part (a), for every
\lambda\in\Lambda,u\in\overline{W}
and

\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}
, we have an inequality

|\langle\overline{\alpha},\lambda\rangle|-1\leq|\langle\overline{\alpha},\lambda u\rangle|\leq|\langle\overline{\alpha},\lambda\rangle|+1.

Therefore in an element
w\in\widetilde{W}
is
m
-regular in the sense of Section
2.1.9
, then it is
(m-1)
-regular in the sense of
[
BV
, Introduction]
, and if it is
m
-regular in the sense of
[
BV
, Introduction]
, then it is
(m-1)
-regular in the sense of
Section
2.1.9
. Moreover, by Section
2.1.9
(b), the two notions coincide when
w=\lambda\in\Lambda
.

2.2.
Affine Springer fibers and
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-action.

Suppose that we are in the situation of Section
2.1
.

2.2.1
.

Affine flag variety.

(a) For every Iwahori subgroup schemes
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
, we set

\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}}:=LG/\mathbf{I}
. For every pair of Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
of
LG
there exists
h\in L(G^{\operatorname{sc}})
such that

h\mathbf{I}^{\prime}h^{-1}=\mathbf{I}
. Then the map
g\mathbf{I}\mapsto g\mathbf{I}h=gh\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
defines an
LG
-equivariant isomorphism
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
, independent of
the choice of
h
.

Thus we can form a projective limit
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}:=\lim_{\mathbf{I}}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}}
and call it the
affine flag variety
of
G
.
In particular, for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
, we have a canonical
LG
-equivariant isomorphism
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG/\mathbf{I}
.

(b) Notice that for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
, the maps
h\mapsto(g\mathbf{I}\mapsto hg\mathbf{I})
and
u\mapsto(g\mathbf{I}\mapsto g\mathbf{I}u^{-1}=gu^{-1}\mathbf{I})

define commuting actions of
LG
and
\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}}
in
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}}
. Furthermore, these actions are compatible with isomorphisms

\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
and
\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Omega_{G,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
, thus induce an action of
LG\times\Omega_{G}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
.

(c) More generally, to every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, and every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
, we associate an ind-scheme
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\mathbf{I}}:=LG/\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
, where parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
was defined in Section
2.1.6
(e).
As in part (a), for every two Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
of
LG
we have a canonical isomorphism
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
, and
we denote by
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}
the limit
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}:=\lim_{\mathbf{I}}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\mathbf{I}}
. We have a natural
LG
-equivariant
projection
\pi_{J}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}
.

2.2.2
.

Affine Springer fibers.

(a) For every element
\gamma\in LG(k)=G(K)
, we denote by
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
(resp.
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}
) the closed sub-ind-scheme of
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
(resp.
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}
) of
\gamma
-fixed points. Explicitly, for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
, the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}
consists of all
g\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}\in LG/\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
such that
g^{-1}\gamma g\in\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
. We denote the
natural projection
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}
by
\pi_{J}
.

(b) We have a natural projection map
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
, which sends
g\mathbf{I}
to
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}(g^{-1}\gamma g)
. For each local system

\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, we set
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}:=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
.

(c) For every
g\in LG
, the (left) action of
g
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}
induces an isomorphism
l_{g}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,g\gamma g^{-1}}
.
Moreover, the projection
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
of part (b) decomposes as a composition

\operatorname{red}_{g\gamma g^{-1}}\circ l_{g}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G,g\gamma g^{-1}}\to\overline{T}_{G}
.

(d) Notice that since the actions of
LG
and
\Omega_{G}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
commute, for every
u\in\Omega_{G}
the automorphism
u:\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
preserves

\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
. Moreover, the following diagram is commutative

(2.3)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}@>{\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}>{}>\overline{T}_{G}\\
@V{u}V{}V@V{}V{\overline{u}}V\\
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}@>{\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}>{}>\overline{T}_{G}.\end{CD}

Indeed, this follows from the identity
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}(ug^{-1}\gamma gu^{-1})=\overline{u}(\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}(g^{-1}\gamma g))
.

(e) For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
and every local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
we can consider the
i
-th homology group

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. Then for every element
g\in LG
the isomorphisms
l_{g}
of part (c) induce isomorphisms

l_{g}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,g\gamma g^{-1}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

(f) Let
G_{\gamma}\subseteq G
be the centralizer of
\gamma
. Then each subscheme
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
is
LG_{\gamma}
-invariant, and the projection
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}
is
LG_{\gamma}
-equivariant with respect to the trivial action of

LG_{\gamma}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
(see part (c)). Therefore the local system
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
from part (b) is
LG_{\gamma}
-equivariant, and the induced action of
LG_{\gamma}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is given by isomorphisms
l_{g}
from part (e).

2.2.3
.

Remark.
Note that the image of the projection
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
is a single
\overline{W}
-orbit. In particular,
\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}\subseteq\overline{T}_{G}
is finite, thus there exists a
(non-canonical) isomorphism
\mathcal{L}|_{\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\operatorname{rk}\mathcal{L}}
, hence
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\operatorname{rk}\mathcal{L}}
.

2.2.4
.

Main particular case.

We will be mostly interested in the case, when
\gamma\in LG(k)\simeq G(K)
is a regular semisimple element of
G
.
Then, arguing as in
[
KL
]
, each reduced ind-scheme
(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma})_{\operatorname{red}}
is actually a scheme, locally of finite type over
k
.

2.2.5
.

The
W_{J}
-action and
\Omega_{G}
-action on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
.

For every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
and an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
, we set
\mathbf{P}_{J}:=\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
,
M_{J}:=M_{\mathbf{P}_{J}}
, and

\overline{B}_{J}:=\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{P}_{J}^{+}\subseteq M_{J}
.

(a) Note that we have the following natural Cartesian diagram

(2.4)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}@>{\widetilde{\operatorname{red}}_{\gamma}}>{}>[\frac{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{I}}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{\overline{B}_{J}}{\overline{B}_{J}}]@>{\operatorname{pr}_{\overline{B}_{J}}}>{}>\overline{T}_{G}\\
@V{\pi_{J}}V{}V@V{}V{[\pi_{\mathbf{P}_{J}}]}V@V{}V{[p_{M_{J}}]}V\\
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}@>{\widetilde{\operatorname{red}}_{J,\gamma}}>{}>[\frac{\mathbf{P}_{J}}{\mathbf{P}_{J}}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{M_{J}}{M_{J}}].\end{CD}

(b) Recall that we have a natural isomorphism
W_{J}\simeq W_{M_{J}}
(see Section
2.1.6
(d)). Using the Cartesian diagram of part (a) and proper base change one gets that for every
w\in W_{J}\simeq W_{M_{J}}
the isomorphism
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}
from Section
1.2.1
(e) induces an isomorphism

a_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}:(\pi_{J})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}(\pi_{J})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}}

on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}
.

(c) Using identifications
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})=H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, the isomorphisms of part (b) induce isomorphisms

a_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})\text{ for each }w\in W_{J}.

Moreover, these isomorphisms are independent of the choice of
\mathbf{I}
.

(d) Using Section
1.2.1
(f), the isomorphisms of part (c) satisfy

a_{J,w_{1}w_{2},\mathcal{L}}=a_{J,w_{1},\overline{w_{2}}_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{J,w_{2},\mathcal{L}}\text{ for all }w_{1},w_{2}\in W_{J}.

(e) Using commutative diagram (
2.3
), for every
u\in\Omega_{G}
, one get an isomorphism
u_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\mathcal{F}_{\overline{u}_{*}(\mathcal{L})}
of local systems on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
, hence an isomorphism

a_{\Omega,u,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{u}_{*}\mathcal{L}}).

(f) By construction, the isomorphisms of part (e) satisfy

a_{\Omega,u_{1}u_{2},\mathcal{L}}=a_{\Omega,u_{1},\overline{u_{2}}_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{\Omega,u_{2},\mathcal{L}}\text{ for all }u_{1},u_{2}\in\Omega_{G}.

2.2.6
.

Truncated version.

(a) Since projection
\pi_{J}
is proper, for every closed subscheme
Y\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
the image
Y_{J}:=\pi_{J}(Y)\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J}
is closed as well. Hence we have a commutative diagram

(2.5)

\begin{CD}Y_{\gamma}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\\
@V{\pi^{Y}_{J,\gamma}}V{}V@V{}V{\pi_{J,\gamma}}V\\
Y_{J,\gamma}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma},\end{CD}

where we put
Y_{\gamma}:=Y\cap\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
and
Y_{J,\gamma}:=Y_{J}\cap\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J,\gamma}
.

(b) Assume furthermore that
Y=\pi_{J}^{-1}(Y_{J})
. Then the diagram (
2.5
) is Cartesian, so repeating the argument of Section
2.2.5
, for every
w\in W_{J}
we get a natural isomorphism

a^{Y}_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}}),

lifting the isomorphism
a_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}
from Section
2.2.5
(c).

(c) In particular, in the situation of part (b), the isomorphism
a_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}
from Section
2.2.5
(c) induces an isomorphism

H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}}),

where
H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
denotes the image of the canonical map
H_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
.

Proposition 2.2.7
.

The isomorphisms of Sections
2.2.5
(c),(e) and
2.2.2
(e) have the following properties:

(a) For proper subsets
J_{1}\subseteq J_{2}\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
and an element
w\in W_{J_{1}}\subseteq W_{J_{2}}
, the isomorphisms
a_{J_{1},w,\mathcal{L}}
and

a_{J_{2},w,\mathcal{L}}
coincide.

(b) For a proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
and elements
w\in W_{J}
and
u\in\Omega_{G}
, the element
uwu^{-1}
belongs to
W_{u(J)}
, and we have an equality of isomorphisms

a_{u(J),uwu^{-1},\mathcal{L}}=a_{\Omega,u,\overline{w}_{*}(\overline{u}^{-1})_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{J,w,(\overline{u}^{-1})_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{\Omega,u^{-1},\mathcal{L}}.

(c) There exists a unique collection of isomorphisms

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})

parameterized by
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
such that

(i)
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}=a_{J,w,\mathcal{L}}
for each
w\in W_{J}
;

(ii)
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}=a_{\Omega,w,\mathcal{L}}
for each
w\in\Omega_{G}
;

(iii)
a_{w_{1}w_{2},\mathcal{L}}=a_{J,w_{1},\overline{w}_{2*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{w_{2},\mathcal{L}}
for all
{w}_{1},{w}_{2}\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
.

(d) For every
g\in LG
, set
\gamma^{\prime}:=g\gamma g^{-1}
. Then for every
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
the following diagram is commutative

\begin{CD}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})@>{a_{w,\mathcal{L}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})\\
@V{l_{g}}V{}V@V{}V{l_{g}}V\\
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})@>{a_{w,\mathcal{L}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}}).\end{CD}

Proof.

(a) follows from definitions using Cartesian diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J_{1},\gamma}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Fl}_{G,J_{2},\gamma}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
[\frac{\mathbf{I}}{\mathbf{I}}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{\mathbf{P}_{J_{1}}}{\mathbf{P}_{J_{1}}}]@>{}>{}>[\frac{\mathbf{P}_{J_{2}}}{\mathbf{P}_{J_{2}}}].\end{CD}

(b) is straightforward.

(c) Formally follows from a combination of parts (a),(b) and the assertions of Section
2.2.5
(d),(f):

Recall that the affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}
is generated by
\widetilde{S}
subject to relations
(s_{1}s_{2})^{m_{s_{1},s_{2}}}=1
for each
s_{1},s_{2}\in\widetilde{S}
. Therefore there exists at most one collection of isomorphisms
\{a_{w,\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in\widetilde{W}}
satisfying condition (iii) and such that
a_{s,\mathcal{L}}=a_{\{s\},s,\mathcal{L}}
for each
s\in\widetilde{S}
. Next, combining part (a) with
the assertion of Section
2.2.5
(d) we see that such a collection
indeed exists and satisfies property (i). Finally, the existence of collection of isomorphisms
\{a_{w,\mathcal{L}}\}_{w\in\widetilde{W}}
satisfying (i)-(iii) follows from decomposition
\widetilde{W}_{G}=\widetilde{W}\ltimes\Omega_{G}
, part (b) and property of Section
2.2.5
(f).

(d) is straightforward.
∎

Proposition
2.2.7
immediately implies the following corollary.

Corollary 2.2.8
.

(a) Each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a equipped with an action of the group
\Lambda_{G}
,
commuting with the
LG_{\gamma}
-action of Section
2.2.2
(f).

(b) Moreover, if
\mathcal{L}
is an
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system, then each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

is equipped with an action of
\widetilde{W}_{G}
, commuting with the
LG_{\gamma}
-action of Section
2.2.2
(f).

2.2.9
.

Notation.
Let
i:G^{\operatorname{sc}}\to G
be the natural map.

(a) We set
\operatorname{Fl}:=\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
. Notice that we have a natural
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
-equivariant embedding

\eta:\operatorname{Fl}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
. Indeed, every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
gives
rise to the Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}:=i^{-1}(\mathbf{I})\subseteq LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
and we define
\eta
to be the map

g\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}\mapsto i(g)\mathbf{I}
.

(b) For every element
\gamma\in LG
, we set
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}:=\operatorname{Fl}\cap\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
. Explicitly, for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
, the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
consists of all
g\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}\in LG^{\operatorname{sc}}/\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}
such that
g^{-1}\gamma g\in\mathbf{I}
.

(c) For every
u\in\Omega_{G}
, consider the composition
\eta_{u}:\operatorname{Fl}\overset{\eta}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
.
Then the induced maps

\bigsqcup_{u\in\Omega_{G}}\eta_{u}:\bigsqcup_{u\in\Omega_{G}}\operatorname{Fl}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\text{ and }\bigsqcup_{u\in\Omega_{G}}\eta_{u}:\bigsqcup_{u\in\Omega_{G}}\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}

are universal homeomorphisms (see Lemma
B.2.3
(b) below).

The proof of the following assertion is given in Section
B.2.10
below.

Lemma 2.2.10
.

(a) For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
w\in\widetilde{W}
the isomorphism
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}
from Proposition
2.2.7
(c) restricts to an isomorphism

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}(\mathcal{L})})
.

(b) The subspace
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\subseteq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is

\Lambda
-invariant, and have a natural isomorphism of
\Lambda_{G}
-representations

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\Lambda}^{\Lambda_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

(c) Moreover, if
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}
-equivariant, then the subspace
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\subseteq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is

\widetilde{W}
-invariant, and we have a natural isomorphism of
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-representations

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

2.2.11
.

Functoriality.

(a) Every isomorphism
\phi:G\overset{\thicksim}{\to}G^{\prime}
of split reductive groups over
K
induces an isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime}}
of affine flag varieties, an isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G^{\prime}}
of tori, an isomorphism
\widetilde{W}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}_{G^{\prime}}
of extended affine Weyl groups, etc.

For example,
if
T\subseteq G
is a maximal split tori and
\mathbf{I}
is an Iwahori subgroup scheme of
LG
, containing
\mathbf{T}
, then

T^{\prime}:=\phi(T)\subseteq G
is a maximal split tori and
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}:=\phi(\mathbf{I})
is an Iwahori subgroup scheme of
LG^{\prime}
containing
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}:=L^{+}(T^{\prime})
, hence

\phi
induces an isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\simeq LG/\mathbf{I}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG^{\prime}/\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime}}
. Moreover, the latter isomorphism
is independent of the choice of
(T,\mathbf{I})
.

(b) The isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime}}
is
\phi
-equivariant, therefore it induces an isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)}
of affine Springer fibers for every
\gamma\in LG
.

(c) A local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
gives rise to a local system
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:=\phi_{*}(\mathcal{L})
on
\overline{T}_{G^{\prime}}
.
Since the isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G^{\prime}}
is
\overline{W}_{G}\simeq\overline{W}_{G^{\prime}}
-equivariant, we conclude that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is

\overline{W}_{G^{\prime}}
-equivariant, if
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}_{G}
-equivariant.

(d) An isomorphism
\phi_{*}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)}
from part (b) induces an isomorphism

\phi_{*}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})

for every
\gamma\in LG
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

(e) For every
g\in LG
and
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
, the following diagram is commutative

\begin{CD}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})@>{a_{w,\mathcal{L}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})\\
@V{\phi_{*}}V{}V@V{}V{\phi_{*}}V\\
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}})@>{a_{w,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}).\end{CD}

(f) Furthermore, if the local system
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}_{G}
-equivariant, then it follows from part (e) that the isomorphism of part (d) is
\phi_{*}:\widetilde{W}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}_{G^{\prime}}
-equivariant, and thus induces an isomorphism

\phi_{*}:H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G^{\prime}},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\prime},\phi(\gamma)},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}))

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
.

2.2.12
.

Inner automorphisms.

Let
\phi:G\overset{\thicksim}{\to}G
be an inner isomorphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}
for some
g\in G^{\operatorname{ad}}(K)=L(G^{\operatorname{ad}})(k)
.

(a) Consider projection
LG^{\operatorname{ad}}\to LG^{\operatorname{ad}}/LG^{\operatorname{sc}}\simeq\Omega_{G}
, let
\omega_{g}\in\Omega_{G^{\operatorname{ad}}}
be the image of
g
, and
let
\overline{w}_{g}\in\overline{W}
be the image of
\omega_{g}
under the composition
\Omega_{G}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{W}_{G}\to\overline{W}
. Unwinding the definitions,
the induced morphism
\phi_{*}:\overline{T}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
equals
\overline{w}_{g}
.

(b) It follows from part (a) for every
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\phi_{*}(\mathcal{L})\simeq\mathcal{L}
.

(c) Assume that
g\in LG^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma}
, that is,
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}(\gamma)=\gamma
. Then, combining part (b) and Section
2.2.11
(f), we see that
for every
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, the automorphism
\phi=\operatorname{Ad}_{g}
of
G
induces an automorphism
\phi_{*}=(\operatorname{Ad}_{g})_{*}

of
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
. Moreover, the correspondence
g\mapsto(\operatorname{Ad}_{g})_{*}
induces the action of
LG^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma}
on
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
.

2.3.
Compatibility of actions

2.3.1
.

Kottwitz isomorphism.
For every torus
S
over
K
, we have a natural isomorphism

\pi_{0}(LS)\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}
, where
(-)_{\Gamma_{K}}
denotes coinvariants, functorial in
S
. Moreover, it is characterized
by the condition that a cocharacter
\lambda\in X_{*}(S)
corresponds to the connected component of
\lambda(t)\in S(K)=LS(k)
when
S
is split. Indeed, the functor
S\mapsto\pi_{0}(LS)
satisfies the conditions of
[
Ko2
, Section 2.1]
. Therefore it is canonically isomorphic to

S\mapsto X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}
by
[
Ko2
, Lemma 2.2]
.

2.3.2
.

The canonical map.
Let
S\subseteq G
be a maximal torus over
K
.

(a) We denote by
\operatorname{pr}_{S}
the composition

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(T_{G})]^{W_{G}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)]\overset{\operatorname{pr}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LS)],

where

\bullet
the first isomorphism is the inverse of isomorphism (
2.1
) from Section
2.1.4
;

\bullet
the second map was defined in Section
1.1.3
(c);

\bullet
and the last map is the Kottwitz isomorphism from Section
2.3.1
.

(b) We denote by
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{S}
the composition
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G}}\overset{\operatorname{pr}_{S}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LS)]\overset{\iota}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LS)]
, where
\iota
induced by the inverse map
\iota:LS\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LS:g\mapsto g^{-1}
.

(c) Notice that both
\operatorname{pr}_{S}
and
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{S}
make
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LS)]
a finite
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G}}
-algebra.

2.3.3
.

Set up.
Fix an element
\gamma\in G(K)=LG(k)
, and let
\mathcal{L}
be a local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
.

(a) By Section
2.2.2
(f), each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is equipped with an action of the group

LG_{\gamma}
. Moreover, since the action any connected algebraic group induces a trivial action on the homology, the action of
LG_{\gamma}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
factors through the action of the group of connected components
\pi_{0}(LG_{\gamma})
.

(b) Using Corollary
2.2.8
(a) each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is therefore
equipped with an action of the group
\Lambda_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG_{\gamma})
. Furthermore, it is even
equipped with an action of the group
\widetilde{W}_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG_{\gamma})
, if the local system
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}_{G}
-equivariant (see Corollary
2.2.8
(b)).

(c) Assume now that
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(K)
. Then
G^{0}_{\gamma}:=(G_{\gamma})^{0}\subseteq G
is a maximal torus over
K
, so the construction of Section
2.3.2
applies, and we set
LG^{0}_{\gamma}:=L(G^{0}_{\gamma})
,
\operatorname{pr}_{\gamma}=\operatorname{pr}_{G,\gamma}:=\operatorname{pr}_{G^{0}_{\gamma}}
and
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{G,\gamma}:=\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{G^{0}_{\gamma}}
. Note that
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
is a subgroup of
\pi_{0}(LG_{\gamma})
.

The following assertion is a crucial technical result of the paper
and will be proved in Appendix B.

Theorem 2.3.4
.

For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists a finite
filtration
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{j}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, stable under
the action of
\Lambda_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
,
such that the action of a subalgebra
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
on each graded piece

\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is induced from the action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(L{G}_{\gamma})]
via homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
. Furthermore, this filtration can be chosen to be stable under the action of
\widetilde{W}_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
, if
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}_{G}
-equivariant.

Our strategy will be to deduce the result from an analogous result for Lie algebras shown by Yun
[
Yun
]
(see Theorem
B.3.4
and Remark
B.3.5
).

3.
Finiteness of homology of affine Springer fibers

3.1.
Finiteness of homology

Let
L
be an algebraically closed field.

Lemma 3.1.1
.

Let
X
be a scheme locally of finite type over
L
equipped with an action of a group
\Lambda

such that there exists a closed subscheme
Y\subseteq X
of finite type
over
L
such that
\Lambda(Y)=X
.

If
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
is Noetherian, then for every
\Lambda
-equivariant
object
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{c}(X,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
and every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
the

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module
H_{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
is finitely generated.

Proof.

Notice that for every distinguished triangle

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\to

in
D^{b}_{c}(X,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
we have an exact sequence of

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-modules

H_{i}(X,\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})\to H_{i}(X,\mathcal{F})\to H_{i}(X,\mathcal{F}^{\prime}).

Since
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
is Noetherian, the assertions
for
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
implies that for
\mathcal{F}
. Applying
this to the distinquished triangle

\iota_{*}\iota^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\to j_{*}j^{*}\mathcal{F}\to,

where
j:U\hookrightarrow X
is a
\Lambda
-invariant open
embedding, and
\iota:X\smallsetminus U\hookrightarrow X
is the corresponding closed
embedding, we get that the assertion for
(X,\mathcal{F})
follows, if we
show assertions for
(U,j^{*}\mathcal{F})
and
(X\smallsetminus U,\iota^{!}\mathcal{F})
.

Since we can always assume that the assertion for
(X\smallsetminus U,\iota^{!}\mathcal{F})
holds by the Noetherian induction on
Y
, in the
course of the proof we can replace
X
by its open non-empty

\Lambda
-equivariant subset
U
and
Y
by
Y\cap U
.

Let
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
be the set of all irreducible components
of
X
. Then the union
\bigcup_{\alpha\neq\beta}(X_{\alpha}\cap X_{\beta})\subseteq X
is closed.
Indeed, it suffices to show that for every open subscheme
V\subseteq X
of finite type over
L
, the intersection

V\cap(\bigcup_{\alpha\neq\beta}(X_{\alpha}\cap X_{\beta}))\subseteq V
is closed.
It suffices to show that the set of the
X_{\alpha}
’s such that
V\cap X_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
is finite.
But this follows from the fact that every
V\cap X_{\alpha}
is either empty or an irreducible component
of
V
and the fact that
V
has finitely many irreducible components.

Replacing
X
by its open subscheme
X\smallsetminus\bigcup_{\alpha\neq\beta}(X_{\alpha}\cap X_{\beta})
,
we can assume that each connected component
X_{\alpha}
of
X
irreducible. Moreover,
enlarging
Y
one can assume that
Y
is a union of connected
components of
X
. But then
H_{i}(X,\mathcal{F})
is generated as a

\Lambda
-module by its finite dimensional subspace
H_{i}(Y,\mathcal{F})
.
Therefore it is finitely generated.
∎

3.1.2
.

Notation.
Let
X
be either a scheme locally of finite type
over
L
or an
ind-scheme
over
L
, that is, an inductive limit
\operatorname{colim}_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
of schemes of finite type over

L
such that all transition maps are closed embeddings.

(a) By a
filtration
on
X
, we mean an increasing sequence of closed subschemes

X_{1}\subseteq X_{2}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq X
of
X
such that
X=\bigcup_{i}X_{i}
.

(b) Assume that
X
is equipped with an action of a group
\Lambda
,
equipped with a filtration
\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i}
(see Section
A.4.2
(a)).

We say that a filtration

\{X_{i}\}_{i}
on
X
is
compatible
with
\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i}
, if
we have an inclusion

\Lambda_{i}(X_{j})\subseteq X_{i+j}
for each
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
.

Moreover, we say that a filtration
\{X_{i}\}_{i}
is
finitely generated

over
\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i}
, if each
X_{i}
is of finite type over
L
, and there
exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
i>N
, we have an equality

X_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{i-j}(X_{j})
.

(c) As in section Section
A.4.2
(d), filtration
\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i}
gives rize to a filtration on
the group algebra
A:=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
, and we denote by

R(A)=\bigoplus_{i}A_{i}
the corresponding Rees algebra.

For every

\Lambda
-equivariant object
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{c}(X,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
and every

j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the graded vector space

R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F})):=\bigoplus_{i}H_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})

is a graded
R(A)
-module: an element
\lambda\in\Lambda_{i}
induced a closed embedding

\lambda:X_{i^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow X_{i+i^{\prime}}:x\mapsto\lambda(x)
, hence induces a morphism

\lambda_{*}:H_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})\to H_{j}(X_{i+i^{\prime}},\mathcal{F})
, dual to the
pull-back
\lambda^{*}:H^{j}(X_{i+i^{\prime}},\mathcal{F})\to H_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})
.

(d) For every
i
, we denote by
H^{\prime}_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})\subseteq H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F})

the image of the natural map
H_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})\to H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F})
, and
set

R(H^{\prime}_{j}(X,\mathcal{F})):=\bigoplus_{i}H^{\prime}_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F}).

Then the natural surjective maps
H_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})\to H^{\prime}_{j}(X_{i},\mathcal{F})
induce a surjective homomorphism

R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))\to R(H^{\prime}_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
of graded
R(A)
-modules.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

In the situation of Section
3.1.2
, assume that
\Lambda
acts
on the set of irreducible components of
X
with finite stabilizers, that

R(A)
is Noetherian and that filtration
\{X_{i}\}_{i}
is finitely generated
over
\{\Lambda_{i}\}_{i}
.

Then for every
\Lambda
-equivariant object
\mathcal{F}\in D^{b}_{c}(X,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
and every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
the
R(A)
-modules

R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
and
R(H^{\prime}_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
are finitely generated.

Proof.

Since
R(H^{\prime}_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
is a factor module of
R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
, it suffices to show the assertion for

R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
i>N
we have

X_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{i-j}(X_{j})
. In particular, we have
X=\Lambda(X_{N})
, thus we are in
the situation of Lemma
3.1.1
with
Y=X_{N}
.

Now our argument is very similar to that of Lemma
3.1.1
: Notice
that for every distinguished triangle

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\to

in
D^{b}_{c}(X,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
we have an exact sequence of

R(A)
-modules

R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}))\to R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))\to R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}^{\prime})).

Since
R(A)
is a Noetherian ring, the assertion
for
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
implies that for
\mathcal{F}
. Thus, arguing
as in Lemma
3.1.1
, we can replace
X
by its open

\Lambda
-equivariant subscheme, thus assuming that each connected
component
X_{\alpha}
of
X
irreducible. In this case,
\Lambda
acts
on the set of connected components of
X
with finite stabilizers.

Next we reduce the assertion to the case when each
X_{i}
is a
union of (possible empty set) of connected components of
X
:
Since
X_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{i-j}(X_{j})
for each
i>N
, it is
enough to show the assertion only for
i=1,\ldots,N
. In other
words, we want to get to the situation that for every
X_{\alpha}

and every
i=1,\ldots,N
the intersection
X_{\alpha}\cap X_{i}
is
either
X_{\alpha}
or empty.

Notice that for every
i=1,\ldots,N
, the intersection
X_{\alpha}\cap X_{i}
is non-empty only for
finitely many
X_{\alpha}
’s. Therefore there exist only finitely many pairs
(X_{\alpha},X_{i})

such that
\emptyset\neq X_{\alpha}\cap X_{i}\subsetneq X_{\alpha}
, and we will call such a pair

bad
.

Assume that a bad pair
(X_{\alpha},X_{i})
exists. Since the stabilizer of

X_{\alpha}\subseteq X
in
\Lambda
is finite, the union
\Lambda(X_{\alpha}\cap X_{i})\subseteq X
is closed.
Thus, replacing
X
by an open
\Lambda
-equivariant subset
X\smallsetminus\Lambda(X_{\alpha}\cap X_{i})
, we decrease the number of bad pairs by at least one. This process will stop after finitely
many steps.

By the shown above, we can assume that each
X_{i}
is a union of
(possible empty set) of connected components of
X
. Since

X_{i}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{i-j}(X_{j})
for each
i>N
, we deduce that
the
R(A)
-module
R(H_{j}(X,\mathcal{F}))
is generated by its finite
dimensional subspace
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}H_{j}(X_{j},\mathcal{F})
. Therefore it
is finitely generated.
∎

3.2.
Combinatorics of the affine Weyl groups

In this subsection we assume that
G
is a split, semisimple and simply connected group over
K
, fix a pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
2.1.2
(a), and set
\widetilde{W}:=\widetilde{W}_{G}
and
\overline{W}:=\overline{W}_{G}
.

3.2.1
.

Bruhat decomposition and length function.

(a) Recall that the inclusion
N_{LG}(LT)\hookrightarrow LG
induces a bijection

\widetilde{W}\simeq N_{LG}(LT)/LT\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathbf{I}\backslash LG/\mathbf{I},

which depends on
\mathbf{I}
, but is independent of
T
.

(b) For every element
g\in LG
, we denote by
w(g)\in\widetilde{W}
the unique
element such that
g\in\mathbf{I}w(g)\mathbf{I}
, and let
l(g)=l_{\mathbf{I}}(g)
be the length of
w(g)
.

(c) Consider the embedding
\eta:\Lambda=X_{*}(T_{G})\simeq X_{*}(T)\hookrightarrow LT\subseteq LG
, induced by the embedding
\lambda\mapsto\lambda(t):X_{*}(T)\to LT
.
Note that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda\subseteq\widetilde{W}
the length
l(\lambda)
coincides with
l_{\mathbf{I}}(\eta(\lambda))
(see part (b)).

(d) For shortness, we will usually omit
\eta
, that is, write
\lambda\in LG
instead of
\eta(\lambda)
.

3.2.2
.

Properties
.

(a) Recall that for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
and every simple affine reflection
s=s_{\alpha}
,
we have
\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}s\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}ws\mathbf{I}
, if
ws>w
, and
\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}s\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}ws\mathbf{I}\cup\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}
, is
ws<w
.

(b) Using part (a) and induction, we see that for every
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}
,
we have an inclusion

\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}\subseteq\bigcup_{w^{\prime\prime}\leq w^{\prime}}\mathbf{I}ww^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{I}.

Moreover, we have

\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}ww^{\prime}\mathbf{I}
if and only if
l(ww^{\prime})=l(w)+l(w^{\prime})
.
Similarly, we have an inclusion

\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}\subseteq\bigcup_{w^{\prime\prime}\leq w}\mathbf{I}w^{\prime\prime}w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}.

(c) By part (b), for every
g,h\in LG
we have
l(gh)\leq l(g)+l(h)
. Moreover, we have

l(gh)=l(g)+l(h)
if and only if
\mathbf{I}g\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}h\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}gh\mathbf{I}
, in which case we have
w(gh)=w(g)w(h)
.

(d) It is a standard fact that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda
, we have an equality

(3.1)

l(\lambda)=\sum_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}}\max\{\langle\overline{\alpha},\lambda\rangle,0\}.

In particular, we have
l(w(\lambda))=l(\lambda)
for every
w\in\overline{W}
.

3.2.3
.

Length functions.
Let
T^{\prime}\subseteq G
be a (not necessarily split) torus.

(a) Assume that
T^{\prime}
is maximal, and let
\varphi:T^{\prime}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{G}
be an admissible isomorphism (over
\overline{K}
).
Then
\varphi
induces an isomorphism
X_{*}(T^{\prime})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T_{G})\simeq\Lambda
. Hence the length function on
\Lambda\subseteq\widetilde{W}
induces a length
function on
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
. Moreover, since
\varphi
is unique up to a
\overline{W}
-action, while the length function on
\Lambda
is
\overline{W}
-invariant
(see Section
3.2.2
(d)), the length function
l_{T^{\prime}}(\cdot)
on
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
is independence of
\varphi
.

(b) For an arbitrary torus
T^{\prime}
, choose a maximal torus
T^{\prime}_{1}\supseteq T^{\prime}
of
G
. Then we have
X_{*}(T^{\prime}_{1})\supseteq X_{*}(T^{\prime})
, so
the construction of part (a) defines a length function
l_{T^{\prime}}:=l_{T^{\prime}_{1}}|_{X_{*}(T^{\prime})}
on
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
. We claim that
the length function
l_{T^{\prime}}
is independent of the maximal torus
T^{\prime}_{1}
. Indeed, if
T^{\prime}_{2}\supseteq T^{\prime}
is
another maximal torus, then there exists
g\in Z_{G}(T^{\prime})
such that
gT^{\prime}_{1}g^{-1}=T^{\prime}_{2}
, so the assertion follows from part (a).

(c) It follows from part (b) that for every torus
T^{\prime\prime}\supseteq T^{\prime}
of
G
, we have an equality

l_{T^{\prime}}=l_{T^{\prime\prime}}|_{X_{*}(T^{\prime})}.

(d) Assume now that
T^{\prime}
is split over
K
. Then we have a natural embedding
X_{*}(T^{\prime})\hookrightarrow LT^{\prime}\subseteq LG:\lambda\mapsto\lambda(t)
.
Hence for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
can also consider the length function
l_{\mathbf{I}}
on
X_{*}(T^{\prime})
.

Lemma 3.2.4
.

In the situation of Section
3.2.3
(d), assume that
L^{+}(T^{\prime})\subseteq\mathbf{I}
. Then the length function
l_{T^{\prime}}
of
Section
3.2.3
(b) equals
l_{\mathbf{I}}
of Section
3.2.3
(d).

Proof.

First, if
T^{\prime}\subseteq G
is a maximal split torus, is clear (compare Section
3.2.1
(b)). Next, using observation
Section
3.2.3
(c) it suffices to show that there exists a maximal split torus
T\supseteq T^{\prime}
of
G
such that
L^{+}(T)\subseteq\mathbf{I}
.
But this is clear: since
T^{\prime}
is split, the centralizer
H:=Z_{G}(T^{\prime})
a split connected group,
and
\mathbf{I}_{H}:=\mathbf{I}\cap LH
is an Iwahori subgroup of
LH
. Then any maximal split torus
T\subseteq H
such that
L^{+}(T)\subseteq\mathbf{I}_{H}
satisfies the required property.
∎

The following assertion plays a central role in the proof of Theorem
3.3.13
below.

Lemma 3.2.5
.

Let
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\Lambda
be a subgroup. For every finite subset

A\subseteq\widetilde{W}
there exists
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that such that for
every
g\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\cdot\mathbf{I}A\mathbf{I}
we have either
l(g)\leq r
or
there exists
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
such that
\lambda\neq 1
and

l(g)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{-1}g)
.

The proof of Lemma
3.2.5
is based on the following claim.

Claim 3.2.6
.

Let
\Lambda^{\prime}\subseteq\Lambda
be a sub-semigroup such that for every root
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}

we have either
\langle\overline{\alpha},\Lambda^{\prime}\rangle\geq 0
or

\langle\overline{\alpha},\Lambda^{\prime}\rangle\leq 0
.

Then for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
there
exists
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that for every
g\in\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}
and

\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
we have
l(\lambda\lambda_{0}g)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}g)
.

Proof.

To expose the structure of the argument we divide it into steps.

Step 1.
For every
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, we have

l(\lambda\lambda^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{\prime})
. Indeed, the assertion follows from equality (
3.1
)
and our assumption on
\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Step 2.
For every affine root

\widetilde{\alpha}\in\widetilde{\Phi}
there exists

\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
we have

\lambda\lambda_{0}(\widetilde{\alpha})>0
if and only if
\lambda_{0}(\widetilde{\alpha})>0
.

Indeed, for every affine root
\widetilde{\alpha}=(\alpha,k)
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
we have

\lambda(\widetilde{\alpha})=(\alpha,k-\langle\alpha,\lambda\rangle)
. So the assertion follows from our assumption
on
\Lambda^{\prime}
.

Step 3.
Assume that elements
w\in\widetilde{W}
and
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
satisfy the property that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
we have
l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}w)
. Then for every
\lambda,\lambda_{0}^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, we have

l(\lambda\lambda^{\prime}_{0}\lambda_{0}w)=l(\lambda\lambda^{\prime}_{0})+l(\lambda_{0}w)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{\prime}_{0})+l(\lambda_{0}w)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{\prime}_{0}\lambda_{0}w),

where the middle equality follows from Step 1, while the first and the last equalities follow by assumption.

Step 4.
For every finite subset
A\subseteq\widetilde{W}
, there
exists
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
and
w\in A
, we have an equality

l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}w)
.

Proof.

Using Step 3, it is enough to show the assertion in the case when
A=\{w\}
consists of one
element. We are going to prove the assertion by induction
on
l(w)
. If
l(w)=0
, then, by Step 1, every
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
satisfies
the property.

Assume now that
l(w)>0
and choose a simple affine reflection

s=s_{\alpha}
such that
w^{\prime}:=ws<w
. Hence, by the induction
hypothesis, there exists
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

(3.2)

\text{for every }\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime},\text{ we have }l(\lambda\lambda^{\prime}_{0}w^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{\prime}_{0}w^{\prime}).

Next, by Step 2, there exists
\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

(3.3)

\text{for every }\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}\text{ we have }\lambda\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{0}(\lambda^{\prime}_{0}w^{\prime}(\alpha))>0\text{ if and only if }\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{0}(\lambda^{\prime}_{0}w^{\prime}(\alpha))>0.

We claim that
\lambda_{0}:=\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{0}\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
satisfies the required property. Using formula (
3.2
) and Step 3, it suffices to show that for every
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
we have an equality

l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w)-l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})=l(\lambda_{0}w)-l(\lambda_{0}w^{\prime}),

or, equivalently that
(\lambda\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})(\alpha)>0
if and only if
(\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})(\alpha)>0
. But this follows from formula (
3.3
).
∎

Step 5.
We claim that element
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, satisfying property of Step 4 for a finite subset
A:=\widetilde{W}^{\leq w}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
, satisfies property of the claim.

Indeed, by Section
3.2.2
(b), for every
g\in\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}
there exists
w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq w}
such that

\lambda_{0}g\in\mathbf{I}\lambda w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}
. Then, by our assumption, for every element
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, we have
l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})
.
Thus, we have
\lambda\lambda_{0}g\in\mathbf{I}\lambda\mathbf{I}\cdot\mathbf{I}\lambda w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}\lambda\lambda_{0}w^{\prime}\mathbf{I}
(by Section
3.2.2
(b)), therefore

l(\lambda\lambda_{0}g)=l(\lambda\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}w^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}g),

as claimed.
∎

Corollary 3.2.7
.

For every finitely generated semigroup
\Lambda^{\prime}\subseteq\Lambda
satisfying
the assumption of Claim
3.2.6
and a finite set
A\subseteq\widetilde{W}
, there exists
r\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every
g\in\Lambda^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{I}A\mathbf{I}
we have
either
l(g)\leq r
or there exists
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that

\lambda\neq 1
,
\lambda^{-1}g\in\Lambda^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{I}A\mathbf{I}
and

l(g)=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda^{-1}g)
.

Proof.

We are going to prove the assertion by induction on the minimal number of generators

\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n}
of the semigroup
\Lambda^{\prime}
. If
n=0
, the assertion is clear.

Note that it is enough to show the assertion in the case when
A=\{w\}
consists of one
element. In this case, by Claim
3.2.6
, there exists
\lambda_{0}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
such that for every
g^{\prime}\in\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}
and

\lambda\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, we have
l(\lambda\lambda_{0}g^{\prime})=l(\lambda)+l(\lambda_{0}g^{\prime})
.

Write
\lambda_{0}
as
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}^{m_{i}}
. For every
i=1,\ldots,n
we denote by
\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\Lambda^{\prime}

the sub-semigroup generated by
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\neq i}
.
Then the assertion follows for each

\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
by induction hypothesis, and we claim that the assertion for
\Lambda^{\prime}
follows from that for the
\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
’s.

More precisely, let
r_{i}
be the constant associated to the sub-semigroup
\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}
and set

A_{i}:=\{w^{\prime}w\,|\,w^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{i}^{m_{i}}\}
, and we claim that constant
r:=\max_{i=1}^{n}r_{i}
satisfies the property
for
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\{w\}
.

Indeed, this follows from the decomposition

\Lambda^{\prime}=(\Lambda^{\prime}-\{1\})\lambda_{0}\cup(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{i}}\Lambda^{\prime}_{i}\lambda_{i}^{j}),

our choice of
\lambda_{0}
and inclusion

\lambda_{i}^{j}\cdot\mathbf{I}w\mathbf{I}\subseteq\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{i}^{j}}\mathbf{I}w^{\prime}w\mathbf{I}\subseteq\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{i}^{m_{i}}}\mathbf{I}w^{\prime}w\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}A_{i}\mathbf{I}

(which uses Section
3.2.2
(b) and observation that
\lambda_{i}^{j}\leq\lambda_{i}^{m_{i}}
for all
j\leq m_{i}
).
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
3.2.5
.

Proof of Lemma
3.2.5
.

Consider the vector space
V:=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
over
\mathbb{R}
. Then the hyperplanes

\{H_{\overline{\alpha}}:=\ker\overline{\alpha}\}_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\Phi}}
decompose
V
as a union of faces. For each
closure of a face
F
, we set
\Lambda^{\prime}_{F}:=\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\cap F
. Then by
Gordan’s lemma (see, for example,
[
Ew
, Lemma 3.4, page 154]
), each
\Lambda^{\prime}_{F}
is a finitely generated semigroup satisfying
the assumption of Claim
3.2.6
.
Since
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{F}\Lambda^{\prime}_{F}
, the assertion follows from Corollary
3.2.7
.
∎

3.2.8
.

Length filtrations.

(a) For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we set
\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}=\{w\in\widetilde{W}\colon l(w)\leq n\}
. Since
l(ww^{\prime})\leq l(w)+l(w^{\prime})
, we conclude that

\{\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}\}_{n}
is a filtration of a monoid
\widetilde{W}
(see Section
A.4.2
(a)).

(b) For every subgroup
\Delta\subseteq\widetilde{W}
, we consider the induced filtration
\{\Delta^{\leq n}\}_{n}
of
\Delta
, where

\Delta^{\leq n}:=\Delta\cap\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}
.

Corollary 3.2.9
.

For every subgroup
\Delta\subseteq\Lambda
, the length filtration
\{\Delta^{\leq n}\}_{n}

is finitely generated. Therefore the Rees algebra
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Delta])
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra, hence a Noetherian ring.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from Lemma
3.2.5
for
A=\{1\}
. Since
\Delta
is commutative, the second assertion follows from the first one
and Section
A.4.2
(e).
∎

3.2.10
.

Example.

Since filtration
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
is
\overline{W}
-invariant (see Section
3.2.2
(d)), it induces
a filtration on
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}}
, and the corresponding Rees algebra satisfies
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})\simeq R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])^{\overline{W}}
.
Since
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra (by Corollary
3.2.9
), we therefore conclude that
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})
is a finitely generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra as well, hence a Noetherian ring.

3.2.11
.

Notation.

(a) For
u\in\widetilde{W}
, we set
\widetilde{W}^{\leq u}:=\{u^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}\,|\,u^{\prime}\leq u\}
. For
u,v\in\widetilde{W}
, we denote by

\widetilde{W}^{\leq u}\cdot\widetilde{W}^{\leq v}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
the set of products
\{u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\,|\,u^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq u},v^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq v}\}
.

(b) Let
\widetilde{S}
be the set of simple affine reflections of
G
(see Section
2.1.2
). For every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, let

W_{J}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
be the subgroup generated by
J
.

The following lemma is well-known to specialists.

Lemma 3.2.12
.

(a) For every
u,v\in\widetilde{W}
there exists a unique element
w:=u*v
of
\widetilde{W}
(called the Demazure product) such
that the product
\widetilde{W}^{\leq u}\cdot\widetilde{W}^{\leq v}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
equals
\widetilde{W}^{\leq w}
.
Moreover,
u*v=uv^{\prime}=u*v^{\prime}
for some
v^{\prime}\leq v
with
l(uv^{\prime})=l(u)+l(v^{\prime})
.

In particular, we have
l(u*v)\leq l(u)+l(v)
, and equality
u*v=uv
holds if and only if
l(uv)=l(u)+l(v)
. Similarly,

u*v=u^{\prime}v=u^{\prime}*v
for some
u^{\prime}\leq u
with
l(u^{\prime}v)=l(u^{\prime})+l(v)
.

(b) For every non-empty subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, let
w_{J}\in W_{J}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
be the longest element. Then for every

w\in\widetilde{W}
the Demazure product
w*w_{J}
(resp.
w_{J}*w
) is the longest element of the
coset
wW_{J}
(resp.
W_{J}w
).

(c) For every
w\in\widetilde{W}
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
, we have inequality

l(w*w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J})\leq l(w*w_{J})+l(\lambda).

Proof.

(a) Argue as in Section
3.2.2
(a),(b) or see
[
He
, Lemma 1]
.

(b) By part (a), we have
w*w_{J}=wu
for some
u\leq w_{J}
. Then
u\in W_{J}
, thus
w*w_{J}\in wW_{J}
. Next, for every
u\in W_{J}
we
have
u\leq w_{J}
, hence
wu\leq w*w_{J}
by the definition of
*
.
Thus
w*w_{J}
is the longest element of
wW_{J}
, as claimed.

(c) By part (a), there exist elements
u,u^{\prime}\leq w_{J}
such that

w_{J}*\lambda=u^{\prime}\lambda
and
(w_{J}*\lambda)*w_{J}=u^{\prime}\lambda u
.
Thus

w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J}=u^{\prime}\lambda u=u^{\prime}u(u^{-1}\lambda u).

Note that
u,u^{\prime}\in W_{J}
, thus
u^{\prime}u\in W_{J}
, hence
l(u^{\prime}u)\leq l(w_{J})
.
Since
l(u^{-1}\lambda u)=l(\lambda)

(by formula (
3.1
)), we thus conclude that

(3.4)

l(w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J})\leq l(uu^{\prime})+l(u^{-1}\lambda u)\leq l(w_{J})+l(\lambda).

Using part (a) again, there exists
w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}
such that we have
w*w_{J}=w^{\prime}*w_{J}
and

l(w*w_{J})=l(w^{\prime})+l(w_{J})
. Then we have an inequality

l(w*w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J})=l(w^{\prime}*w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J})\leq l(w^{\prime})+l(w_{J}*\lambda*w_{J})

and equality

l(w*w_{J})+l(\lambda)=l(w^{\prime})+l(w_{J})+l(\lambda).

Now the assertion follows from inequality (
3.4
).
∎

Notation 3.2.13
.

For every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, we say that an element

w\in\widetilde{W}
is
right
(resp.
left
)
J
-longest, if
w
is the longest element of the
coset
wW_{J}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
(resp.
W_{J}w\subseteq\widetilde{W}
). Notice that by Lemma
3.2.12
(b) this happens if and only if

w*w_{J}=w
(resp.
w_{J}*w=w
).

Lemma 3.2.14
.

For each elements
w,w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}
we have
w*w^{\prime}\geq w
and
w^{\prime}*w\geq w
. Moreover, if an element

w\in\widetilde{W}
is
m
-regular (see Section
2.1.9
), then elements
w*w^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}*w
are

m
-regular as well.

Proof.

Since the map
w\mapsto w^{-1}
preserves
m
-regularity (see Section
2.1.9
(d)) and Bruhat ordering, and therefore we have

(w*w^{\prime})^{-1}=(w^{\prime})^{-1}*w^{-1}
, it suffices to show the assertion for
w*w^{\prime}
.

By induction on
l(w^{\prime})
, it is enough to show the assertion in the
case when
l(w^{\prime})=1
, thus
w^{\prime}=s
is a simple affine reflection. In this
case, we see that
w*s=\max\{w,ws\}\geq w
. If
w*s=w
, there is nothing to prove, while if
ws>w
, then
for every
\alpha\in\widetilde{\Phi}
such that
\alpha>0
and
w^{-1}(\alpha)<0
, we have
(ws)^{-1}(\alpha)=sw^{-1}(\alpha)<0
,
from which the
m
-regularity assertion follows.
∎

3.3.
Application to homology of affine Springer fibers

Let
G
be a split connected reductive group over
K
, and let
\gamma\in G(K)
be a regular semisimple element.

3.3.1
.

Set up.

(a) Denote by
S_{G,\gamma}\subseteq G_{\gamma}
the maximal split torus, and set
\Lambda_{G,\gamma}:=X_{*}(S_{G,\gamma})
. Consider an embedding

\Lambda_{G,\gamma}\hookrightarrow L(S_{G,\gamma})\subseteq LG_{\gamma}:\mu\mapsto\mu(t).

Then the group
\Lambda_{G,\gamma}
acts on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
. Moreover, arguing as in
[
KL
]
, there exists a closed subscheme of finite type
Y\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}

such that
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}=\Lambda_{G,\gamma}(Y)
.

(b) Let
\mathcal{L}
be a local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
, and let

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
be the corresponding local system on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
(see Section
2.2.2
(b)).
Recall that each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
has a
structure of a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module, while each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
has a structure of a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module (see Corollary
2.2.8
(a) and Lemma
2.2.10
(b)).

(c) Recall that the group
LG_{\gamma}
acts on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
, and the induced action on each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

factors through
\pi_{0}(LG_{\gamma})
. Furthermore, the subgroup
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma}:=L(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})\subseteq LG_{\gamma}
stabilizes

\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}=\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}},\gamma}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
, and hence
\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
acts on

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
.

Proposition 3.3.2
.

(a) Each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.

(b) Each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module.

Proof.

(a) Let
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{j}
be the

\Lambda_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-invariant filtration from Theorem
2.3.4
. It is enough to show that each graded piece
\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module. We will show that each

\operatorname{gr}^{j}{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}
-module.

By Theorem
2.3.4
, the action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}
on

\operatorname{gr}^{j}{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
factors through homomorphism

\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
, making

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
a finite

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}
-algebra. Therefore it is enough to show that each

\operatorname{gr}^{j}{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
-module.

Since the action of
\Lambda_{G,\gamma}
on
\operatorname{gr}^{j}{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

factors through the homomorphism
\Lambda_{G,\gamma}\to\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
, it suffices to show that
each
\operatorname{gr}^{j}{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a
finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G,\gamma}]
-module. Using Lemma
3.1.1
and the observation of Section
3.3.1
(a),
we conclude that
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G,\gamma}]
-module. Thus the assertion follows from the
fact that
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G,\gamma}]
is a Noetherian ring.

(b) Recall that the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is isomorphic
to the tensor product
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
(see Lemma
2.2.10
(b)).
Since
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a Noetherian
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module, by part (a), while
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
is a free (hence flat)
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module, the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is Noetherian, thus finitely generated.
∎

3.3.3
.

Remark.
The statement that homologies of affine Springer fibers are finitely generated over
\widetilde{W}_{G}
appears also as Conjecture 3.6 in
[
Lu3
]
. It is also mentioned in
loc. cit.
that the statement should follow from the result of
[
Yun
]
.

3.3.4
.

Length filtrations.

(a) Let
S_{\gamma}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma}
be the maximal split torus.
We equip
\Lambda_{\gamma}:=X_{*}(S_{\gamma})
and
X_{*}(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
with the canonical length functions (see Section
3.2.3
(b)), and let
\{\Lambda_{\gamma}^{\leq n}\}_{n}
and
\{X_{*}(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}\}_{n}
be the corresponding filtrations.

(b) Define
\{\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}\}_{n}
to be the filtration on
\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
defined as
the image of
\{X_{*}(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}\}_{n}
under the surjection
X_{*}(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})\to X_{*}(G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{K}}\simeq\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
(see Section
2.3.1
).

3.3.5
.

Filtrations on affine flag varieties.
Fix an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
.

(a) As in Section
2.2.9
, we denote by
\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}
the corresponding Iwahori subgroup of
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
,
and set
\operatorname{Fl}=\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
and
\widetilde{W}=\widetilde{W}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
.

(b) Recall (see Section
3.2.1
(a)) that the correspondence
w\mapsto\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{w}:=\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}w\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}
gives
a canonical bijection between elements of
\widetilde{W}
and
\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}}
-orbits in
\operatorname{Fl}=\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}
.

(c) For every element
w\in\widetilde{W}
, we denote by
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}=\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}_{\mathbf{I}}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
be the closure of
\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{w}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
.

(d) Using Section
3.2.1
, for every proper subset
J\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, an element
w
is right
J
-longest if and only if
\pi_{J}^{-1}(\pi_{J}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}))=\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}
, where
\pi_{J}
is the projection
\operatorname{Fl}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{J}
, and element
w
is left
J
-longest if and only if
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
is

\mathbf{P}_{J,\mathbf{I}}
-invariant.

(e) For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}=\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq n}
the union

\bigcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}}\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq w}
. Then
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}\}_{n}
is a filtration of
\operatorname{Fl}
(compare Section
3.1.2
(a)).

(f) For all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
J,J^{\prime}\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
, we denote by

\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}=\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}

the union
\bigcup_{w}\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq w}
, taken over
w\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}
such that
w
is right
J
-longest, left
J^{\prime}
-longest and
m
-regular (see Section
2.1.9
and Notation
3.2.13
).

(g) We claim that
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}\}_{n}
is also a filtration on
\operatorname{Fl}
. Equivalently, we claim that for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
there exists
w^{\prime}\geq w
such that
w^{\prime}
is right
J
-longest, left
J^{\prime}
-longest and
m
-regular. But it follows from Lemmas
3.2.14
and
3.2.12
(b) that for every
m
-regular element
\lambda\in\Lambda
, the element
w^{\prime}:=w_{J^{\prime}}*\lambda*w*w_{J}
satisfies all these properties.

3.3.6
.

Dependence on
\mathbf{I}
. Assume that we are in the situation of Section
3.3.5
.

(a) Let
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\subseteq LG
be another Iwahori subgroup, and let
h\in LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
be such that
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}=h\mathbf{I}h^{-1}
.
Then for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
we have an equality
\operatorname{Fl}^{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=h\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{w}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
, hence
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=h\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq w}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
.

(b) In the situation of part (a) assume further that
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime};\mathbf{I}}
for some subset
J^{\prime}\subsetneq\widetilde{S}
and that element
w\in\widetilde{W}
is left
J^{\prime}
-longest. Then
h\in\mathbf{P}^{\operatorname{sc}}_{J^{\prime};\mathbf{I}}
, hence by a combination of part (a) and Section
3.3.5
(d), we have
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=h\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq w}=\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq w}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
.

(c) By part (b), the filtration
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}\}_{n}
does not change, if the Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
is replaced by
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime};\mathbf{I}}
.

3.3.7
.

Filtrations on affine Springer fibers and their homology.

(a) For a closed subscheme
Y\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
, we denote by

Y_{\gamma}:=Y\cap\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
the corresponding closed subscheme of

\operatorname{Fl}
, and denote by
H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
the image of the
natural map

H_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

(b) By Section
3.3.5
, both
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
and
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
are filtrations of

\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
, thus both
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
and
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
are filtrations of

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
.

3.3.8
.

Good position.
We say that element
\gamma\in LG
is in
good position
with a parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}\subseteq LG
, if the maximal split subtorus
S_{G,\gamma}\subseteq G_{\gamma}
satisfies
L^{+}(S_{G,\gamma})\subseteq\mathbf{P}
.

Lemma 3.3.9
.

Let
\gamma
be in good position with
\mathbf{P}
. Then

(a) There exists an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
of
LG
such that

\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}
.

(b) There exists a maximal split torus
T\supseteq S_{G,\gamma}
such that
L^{+}(T)\subseteq\mathbf{P}
.

Proof.

(a) The composition
L^{+}(S_{G,\gamma})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}\to\mathbf{P}/\mathbf{P}^{+}=M_{\mathbf{P}}
induces an embedding
\overline{S}_{G,\gamma}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathbf{P}}
. Choose a Borel subgroup
B_{\mathbf{P}}\subseteq M_{\mathbf{P}}
, containing
\overline{S}_{G,\gamma}
. Then the preimage
\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
of
B_{\mathbf{P}}\subseteq M_{\mathbf{P}}
is an Iwahori subgroup such that
L^{+}(S_{G,\gamma})\subseteq\mathbf{P}
.

(b) Was shown during the course of the proof of Lemma
3.2.4
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.10
.

Assume that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}
. Then the filtration
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
of
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
is compatible with and finitely generated over the filtration
\{\Lambda_{\gamma}^{\leq n}\}_{n}
of
\Lambda_{\gamma}
.

Proof.

First we claim that filtration
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
is
compatible with filtration
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
on

\Lambda_{\gamma}
. For this we have to show that for every two elements

\lambda\in\Lambda_{\gamma}
and
g\in LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
we have
l_{\mathbf{I}}(\lambda g)\leq l(\lambda)+l_{\mathbf{I}}(g)
. But this immediately follows from a combination of Section
3.2.2
(c) and Lemma
3.2.4
.

It now follows from Section
3.3.1
(a) that we have
\Lambda_{\gamma}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{\leq n})=\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Now the fact that
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
is finitely generated over
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}_{\gamma}\}_{n}
follows immediately from
Lemma
3.2.5
applied to the subgroup
\Lambda^{\prime}:=\Lambda_{\gamma}
and subset
A:=\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.11
.

Let
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}\in\widetilde{W}
. Then for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
the isomorphism
a_{w,\mathcal{L}}
from Lemma
2.2.10
(a) satisfies

(3.5)

a_{w,\mathcal{L}}(H^{\prime}_{i}(\cup_{j}\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{\leq w_{j}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\subseteq H^{\prime}_{i}(\cup_{j}\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{\leq w_{j}*w^{-1}},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}(\mathcal{L})}).

Proof.

We are going to show the assertion by induction on
l(w)
.

Let
w=s
be a simple affine reflection. Then the closed subscheme

Y:=\bigcup_{j}\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w_{j}*s}
of
\operatorname{Fl}
satisfies

Y=\pi_{s}^{-1}(\pi_{s}(Y))
(see Section
3.3.5
(b) and Lemma
3.2.12
(b)), thus it follows from Section
2.2.6
(c)
that we have an equality

a_{s,\mathcal{L}}(H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))=H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}(\mathcal{L})}).

Since
w_{j}\leq w_{j}*s
(see Lemma
3.2.14
), we have
H^{\prime}_{i}(\bigcup_{j}\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{\leq w_{j}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\subseteq H^{\prime}_{i}(Y_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, and
inclusion (
3.5
) follows in this case.

For an arbitrary
w
choose
s\in\widetilde{S}
such that
w^{\prime}:=sw<w
.
Then
w=sw^{\prime}
, and
w^{-1}=w^{\prime-1}*s
, therefore inclusion
(
3.5
) for
w
follows from that for
w^{\prime}
and
s
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.12
.

Filtrations
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
, and
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
are
compatible with the length filtration
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
of
\Lambda
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3.11
, to show the assertion for
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
, it suffices to show an inclusion

\bigcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{\leq n}}\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w*\lambda^{-1}}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n+l(\lambda)}

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
. Since
l(w^{-1})=l(w)

for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
, we have to show that for every
w\in\widetilde{W}
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
we have an inclusion

\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w*\lambda}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq l(w)+l(\lambda)}
. This follows from the inequality

l(w*\lambda)\leq l(w)+l(\lambda)
(use Lemma
3.2.12
(a)).

Similarly, to show the assertion for
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
, it is enough to show that for every

\lambda\in\Lambda
and every
m
-regular, right
J
-longest and left
J^{\prime}
-longest element
w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}
, we have an inclusion

\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w^{\prime}*\lambda}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq l(w^{\prime})+l(\lambda);J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}.

Consider element
w:=w^{\prime}*\lambda*w_{J}
. Then
w^{\prime}*\lambda\leq w
(see Lemma
3.2.14
), thus
\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w^{\prime}*\lambda}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq w}
, and
it remains to show that element
w
is right
J
-longest, left
J^{\prime}
-longest,
m
-regular, and satisfies
l(w)\leq l(w^{\prime})+l(\lambda)
.

Note that
w
is right
J
-longest by Lemma
3.2.12
(b), and
w
is
m
-regular, since
w^{\prime}
is such (use Lemma
3.2.14
). Next, since
w^{\prime}
is left
J^{\prime}
-longest, we have an equality
w^{\prime}=w_{J^{\prime}}*w^{\prime}
(by Lemma
3.2.12
(b)), hence
w_{J^{\prime}}*w=w
, thus
w
is left
J^{\prime}
-longest (by Lemma
3.2.12
(b)). Finally, since
w^{\prime}
is right
J
-longest, we have
w^{\prime}=w^{\prime}*w_{J}
(by Lemma
3.2.12
(b)), hence
l(w)\leq l(w^{\prime})+l(\lambda)
(by Lemma
3.2.12
(c)).
∎

Theorem 3.3.13
.

Assume that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}
. Then for each

i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the filtration
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}

of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is finitely
generated over
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
.

Proof.

We are going to deduce the result from Lemma
3.3.10
and Corollary
3.3.12
,
mimicking the argument of Proposition
3.3.2
. For simplicity, we divide our argument into six steps:

Step 1.
We equip
\Lambda,\Lambda_{\gamma},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}}
and
\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
with length filtrations
(see Sections
3.2.8
and
3.3.4
), and let
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]),R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}],R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})
and
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])

be the corresponding Rees algebras (see Section
3.1.2
(c)). By definitions, the homomorphisms

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]\supseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}}\overset{\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]\overset{\eta}{\longleftarrow}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}]

preserve length filtrations, thus give rise to homomorphisms

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])\supseteq R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})\overset{R(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma})}{\longrightarrow}R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])\overset{R(\eta_{\gamma})}{\longleftarrow}R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}]).

Step 2.
Let
R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
be the Rees module, corresponding to the filtration

\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. We have to show that
R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a finitely generated

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
-module (use Corollary
3.3.12
and see Section
A.4.1
(c)).

Let
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{j}
be the finite

\Lambda\times\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
-invariant filtration from Theorem
2.3.4
. It induces a finite filtration

\{F^{j}R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\}_{j}
of the Rees module
R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
, and the
graded piece
\operatorname{gr}^{j}R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is naturally identified with the Rees module

R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
corresponding to the induced filtration

\{\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}

of
\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. It suffices to show that each
R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a finitely generated

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
-module.

Step 3.
Unfortunately, we do not know whether the filtration
\{\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\}_{n}
of
\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
