is compatible with filtration

\{\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}\}_{n}
.

To overcome this difficulty, we define a subspace

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]^{\prime}_{n}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}]

consisting of all
x\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})^{\leq n}]
such that for all
j
and
m
we have an inclusion

x(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq m}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\subseteq\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n+m}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}),

and set

R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]):=\bigoplus_{n}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]^{\prime}_{n}\subseteq R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]).

By construction,
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])\subseteq R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])

is a graded subalgebra, and
R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a
graded
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
-module.

Step 4.
By Theorem
2.3.4
, the action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}}
on
\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
factors through homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
. Since
filtration
\{\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
of

\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is compatible with
\{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda^{\leq n}]^{\overline{W}}\}_{n}
, the
image of the homomorphism
R(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma})
(see Step 1)
lies in
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
, and the action of
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})
on
R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))

is induced by the
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
-action via

R(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}):R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\Gamma_{K}}]^{\overline{W}})\to R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})]).

Step 5.
By the construction of the length filtration on
\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})
(see Section
3.3.4
), the map
R(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}):R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])\to R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
is surjective. Since
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra, it is a finite
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\Gamma_{K}}]^{\overline{W}})=R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\Gamma_{K}}])^{\overline{W}}
-algebra.
Therefore
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
is finite
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})
-algebra.
Since
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\Gamma_{K}}]^{\overline{W}})
is a Noetherian ring (see Section
3.2.10
), the subalgebra

R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])\subseteq R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])

is a finite
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]^{\overline{W}})
-algebra as well. Thus it suffices to show that each Rees module
R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is finitely generated over
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
.

Step 6.

Since filtration
\{\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
is compatible
with
\{\Lambda_{\gamma}^{\leq n}\}_{n}
(by Lemma
3.3.10
), the homomorphism

R(\eta_{\gamma})
factors through
R^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\gamma})])
, thus it suffices to show
that each

R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a finitely generated

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}])
-module.

On the other hand, it follows from the combination of Lemmas

3.3.10
and
3.1.3
, that the Rees module

R(H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a finitely generated

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}])
-module. Since the Rees module
R(\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))\simeq\operatorname{gr}^{j}R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is a
subquotient of
R(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
, the assertion follows from the fact that the Rees algebra

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{\gamma}])
is Noetherian (see Corollary
3.2.9
).
∎

Corollary 3.3.14
.

Assume that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}
. Then the filtration
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}

of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is finitely generated over
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3.9
(a), there exists an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}
such that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
.
Then, by Section
3.3.6
(c), we can replace
\mathbf{I}
by
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}
, thus assuming that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}
.

Hence, by Theorem
3.3.13
, the Rees module
\bigoplus_{n}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a finitely generated

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
-module. Since

R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
is Noetherian (see Corollary
3.2.9
), the submodule
\bigoplus_{n}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
of

\bigoplus_{n}H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is
finitely generated over
R(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda])
as well. Therefore the filtration

\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{n}
is finitely
generated over
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
, as claimed.
∎

Theorem 3.3.15
.

Assume that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}
. Then for every sufficiently large
m\in\mathbb{N}
and every
n\in\mathbb{N}

the canonical surjection

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have to show that for every sufficiently large
m
, the morphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{\operatorname{reg}}}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

is injective.
By Section
2.2.3
, we can assume that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
. Next, arguing as in the proof of Corollary
3.3.14
, we can assume that
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{I}
. Then by Lemma
3.3.9
(b), there exists a maximal split torus
T\supseteq S_{G,\gamma}
such that
\mathbf{T}\subseteq\mathbf{I}
. Thus the assertion follows from a combination of Section
2.1.11
(c) and
[
BV
, Theorem 0.1]
.
∎

4.
Geometric interpretation of characters and stability

Let
G
be a connected reductive group over
F=\mathbb{F}_{q}((t))
. Starting from Section
4.2
we will assume that
G
splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}:=F\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, hence split over
K:=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}((t))
, thus the construction of Section
2.1
applies.

4.1.
Endoscopy

4.1.1
.

Cohomology of tori.

(a) Recall that for every torus
S
over
F
we have
H^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}},S)=1
, thus we have an isomorphism

H^{1}(F,S)\simeq H^{1}(\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F},S(F^{\operatorname{nr}}))
.

(b) Furthermore, it follows from the results of Kottwitz (
[
Ko1
, Lemma 2.2
and Proposition 2.3]
) or Borovoi (
[
Bo
]
) that we have a natural isomorphism

H^{1}(F,S)\simeq H^{1}(\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F},S(F^{\operatorname{nr}}))\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{F},\operatorname{tor}},

functorial in
S
, where
(-)_{\Gamma_{F}}
stands for
\Gamma_{F}
-coinvariants, and
(-)_{\operatorname{tor}}
for torsion.

(c) More precisely, if
x\in H^{1}(\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F},S(F^{\operatorname{nr}}))
is the class of the cocycle

\tau\mapsto x_{\tau}
, then its image in
X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{F}}
is the projection of the class

[x_{\sigma}]\in\pi_{0}(LS)\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}
(see Section
2.3.1
) of
x_{\sigma}\in S(K)=(LS)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

4.1.2
.

Stable conjugacy.

(a) Recall that two elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
are called
G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
-conjugate
, if there exists an element
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
such that
g\gamma g^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}
. Note that in this case, for every
\tau\in\Gamma_{F}
the element
g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g\in G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
belongs to

G_{\gamma}(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
.

Furthermore, two elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
are called
stably conjugate
, if there exists
g
as above such that

g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g\in G^{0}_{\gamma}(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
for every
\tau\in\Gamma_{F}
. Then we can form an invariant
\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})
, depending only on the
G(F)
-conjugacy classes of
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
, defined to be the class of the cocycle
\tau\mapsto g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g
.

(b) Since
H^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}},G^{0}_{\gamma})=1
(see Section
4.1.1
(a)), for every stable conjugate elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
there exists
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
g\gamma g^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}
and

g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g\in G^{0}_{\gamma}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})\subseteq G^{0}_{\gamma}(K)=LG^{0}_{\gamma}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}).

Moreover, the image of
\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})
in
X_{*}(G^{0}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{F}}
(see Sections
4.1.1
(b),(c)) equals the projection of

[g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g]\in\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\simeq X_{*}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{K}}
.

(c) Recall that two embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:S\hookrightarrow G
of a maximal torus defined over
F
are called
stably conjugate
if there exists
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
such that
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Ad}_{g}\circ\mathfrak{a}
and the induced morphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:\mathfrak{a}(S)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}(S)
is defined over
F
. Moreover, since
H^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}},S)=1
, there exists
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
satisfying this property.

As in part (a), we can form an invariant
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,S)
, depending only on the
G(F)
-conjugacy classes of
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. Explicitly,
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})
is the class of the cocycle
\tau\mapsto\mathfrak{a}^{-1}(g^{-1}{}^{\tau}g)\in S(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
.

(d) Notice that elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
are stably conjugate if and only if there exists an embedding
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{\gamma}:G^{0}_{\gamma}\hookrightarrow G
, which is stably conjugate to the inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}:G^{0}_{\gamma}\hookrightarrow G
and satisfies
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{\gamma}(\gamma)=\gamma^{\prime}
.

(e) In the situation of part (c), assume that
G
is semisimple and simply connected. Then
H^{1}(F,G)=1
, therefore the correspondence

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})
defines a bijection between the set of
G(F)
-conjugacy classes of elmeddings
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:S\hookrightarrow G
,
stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}
and
H^{1}(F,S)
.

4.1.3
.

Langlands dual groups.

(a) Let
\widehat{G}
be the (connected) Langlands dual group over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
. Then
\widehat{G}
is equipped with a canonical continuous homomorphism
\rho_{G}:\Gamma_{F}\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{G})
. In particular, we have a natural action of

\Gamma_{F}
on
Z(\widehat{G})
, so we can consider the group of fixed points
Z(\widehat{G})^{\Gamma_{F}}
. Moreover, composing
\rho_{G}
with a section of the natural projection
\operatorname{Aut}(\widehat{G})\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{G})
, we get an action of
\Gamma_{F}
on
\widehat{G}
, defined uniquely up to a
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy.

(b) Every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:S\hookrightarrow G
of a maximal torus over
F
gives rise to a
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy class of embeddings

[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}]:\widehat{S}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
of maximal tori, hence canonical
\Gamma_{F}
-equivariant embedding
Z_{\mathfrak{a}}:Z(\widehat{G})\hookrightarrow\widehat{S}
. Furthermore, two
embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:S\hookrightarrow G
are stably conjugate if and only if
[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}]=[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}]
.

(c) Notice that for every torus
S
over
F
, we have a natural isomorphism of groups
\operatorname{Hom}(X_{*}(S),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times})\simeq\widehat{S}
, hence

\operatorname{Hom}(X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{F}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times})\simeq\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
. In particular, for every
\xi\in\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
and
x\in H^{1}(F,S)\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{F}}
, we can form a pairing
\langle\xi,x\rangle\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

4.1.4
.

Endoscopic data
(compare
[
Ko1
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
and
[
KV
, Section 1.3]
).
Suppose we are in the situation of Section
4.1.3
.

(a) Slightly modifying the terminology of
[
Ko1
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
, by an
endoscopic datum
for
G
, we mean a triple
\mathcal{E}=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
, where

\bullet

s\in\widehat{G}
is a semisimple element;

\bullet

\widehat{H}:=(\widehat{G}_{s})^{0}\subseteq\widehat{G}
is a connected centralizer;

\bullet

\rho:\Gamma_{F}\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H})
is a continuous homomorphism

such that

\bullet
the
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy class of the inclusion
\widehat{H}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant,

\bullet
we have
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{F}}\subseteq\widehat{H}\subseteq\widehat{G}
.

(b) We say that two endoscopic data
\mathcal{E}=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=(s^{\prime},\widehat{H}^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})

conjugate
and write
\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
if there exists
g\in\widehat{G}
such that
gsg^{-1}=s^{\prime}
and the induced isomorphism
(\operatorname{Ad}_{g})_{*}:\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H}^{\prime})
induced by isomorphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:H\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{\prime}
satisfies
\rho^{\prime}=\rho\circ(\operatorname{Ad}_{g})_{*}
.

4.1.5
.

Examples.

(a) As in
[
KV
, Section 1.3.8]
, to each pair
(S,\kappa)
, where
S\subseteq G
is a maximal torus over
F
and
\kappa\in\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, we associate an endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{S,\kappa}=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
for
G
, defined up to a
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy.

Namely, let
\mathfrak{a}:S\hookrightarrow G
be the inclusion and choose an embedding
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}:\widehat{S}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
belonging to a canonical conjugacy class of embeddings
[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}]
from Section
4.1.3
(b), set
s:=\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}(\kappa)\in\widehat{G}
, put
\widehat{H}:=(\widehat{G}_{s})^{0}
, and let
\rho:\Gamma_{F}\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H})
be the unique homomorphism such that
s\in Z(\widehat{H})^{\Gamma_{F}}
and the
\widehat{H}
-conjugacy class of the inclusion
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}}:\widehat{S}\hookrightarrow\widehat{H}
is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant.

(b)
11
11

11

The example in part (b) (and compatibility in part (c) below) is not used in this work, it is included only in order to relate
results of this work to the general picture outlined in the introduction.

Let
\lambda:W_{F}\to{}^{L}G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
be a continuous homomorphism, lifting the inclusion
W_{F}\hookrightarrow\Gamma_{F}
. Following
[
Lan
]
, every semisimple element
s\in Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda)
gives rise to the endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
, where
\widehat{H}=(\widehat{G}_{s})^{0}
, and
\rho:\Gamma_{F}\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H})
is characterised by the condition that the restriction
\rho|_{W_{F}}
is the composition

(4.1)

W_{F}\overset{\lambda}{\longrightarrow}Z_{{}^{L}G}(s)\overset{\operatorname{Ad}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Aut}(\widehat{H})\overset{\operatorname{pr}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H}).

Namely, it is easy to see that the composition (
4.1
) is continuous with finite image, so it uniquely extends to
\Gamma_{F}
.

(c) The construction in parts (a) and (b) are compatible. Namely, assume that
Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda)=\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
for some maximal torus
T\subseteq G
. Then for every
\kappa\in Z_{\widehat{G}}(\lambda)=\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
the endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
from part (a) is conjugate to the endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda,\kappa}
from part (b).

4.1.6
.

Remark.
One can show that if
G
is quasi-split, then every endoscopic datum for
G
is conjugate to an endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{S,\kappa}
obtained by the construction of Section
4.1.5
(a). For an arbitrary
G
the assertion holds if the endoscopic datum is
elliptic
.
We do not need these facts in this work.

4.1.7
.

\mathcal{E}
-stable functions.
Let
\mathcal{E}=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
be an endoscopic datum for
G
.

(a) Fix a pair
(\gamma,\xi)
, where
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\xi\in\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, and
let
\mathcal{E}_{\gamma,\xi}:=\mathcal{E}_{G^{0}_{\gamma},\xi}
be the endoscopic datum, defined in Section
4.1.5
(a). We write
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
, if
\mathcal{E}_{\gamma,\xi}\simeq\mathcal{E}
.

(b) For a pair
(\gamma,\xi)
as in part (a), let
\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}:G^{0}_{\gamma}\hookrightarrow G
be the inclusion. Then we have
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
if and only if there exists an embedding

\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
such that

\bullet
we have
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}\in[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}]
(see Section
4.1.3
(b));

\bullet
we have
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}(\xi)=s
, thus
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}(\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}})\subseteq\widehat{H}
;

\bullet
the
\widehat{H}
-conjugacy class of
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{H}
is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant.

(c) Let
\operatorname{Orb}_{G(F)}^{\operatorname{st}}(\gamma)\subseteq G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
be the locus of all stable conjugates of
\gamma
also referred as the
stable orbit

of
\gamma
. Then using Sections
4.1.2
(a) and
4.1.3
(c), every
\chi\in\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
defines a function

\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Orb}_{G(F)}^{\operatorname{st}}(\gamma)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
given by the formula

\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\gamma}(\gamma^{\prime})=\langle\xi,\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\rangle.

(d) We say that an
\operatorname{Ad}G(F)
-invariant function
f:G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{E}
-stable
, if for every element
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
the restriction
f|_{\operatorname{Orb}_{G(F)}^{\operatorname{st}}(\gamma)}
belongs to the linear span of
\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\gamma}
, where
\xi
runs over all elements of
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
such that
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
.

4.1.8
.

Stable case.

For every connected reductive group
G
we have a trivial endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{triv}}:=(1,\widehat{G},\rho_{G})
. Then for every pair
(\gamma,\xi)
as in Section
4.1.7
(a) we have
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{triv}}
if and only if
\xi=1
. Therefore a function
f:G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is
\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{triv}}
-stable if and only if
its restriction to each stable orbit
\operatorname{Orb}_{G(F)}^{\operatorname{st}}(\gamma)
is constant, and such function is usually called
stable
.

The following simple observation will play an important role later.

4.1.9
.

Observations.
Let
(\gamma,\xi)
and
\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}
be as in Section
4.1.7
(b).

(a) The composition
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}:=\nu_{\widehat{G}}\circ\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\to\widehat{G}\to c_{\widehat{G}}
is independent of
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}\in[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}]
. It follows from the description of
4.1.7
(c) that if
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
, then
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}(\xi)=\nu_{\widehat{G}}(s)
.

(b) Conversely, assume now that
Z(G)
is connected, then for every pair
(\gamma,\xi)
as in Section
4.1.7
(a) such that
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}(\xi)=\nu_{\widehat{G}}(s)
, we have
(\gamma,\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

By assumption, we have an equality
\nu_{\widehat{G}}\circ\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}(\xi)=\nu_{\widehat{G}}(s)
for every
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}\in[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}]
. Therefore there exits
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}\in[\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}]
such that
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}(\xi)=s
. So it remains to show that the
\widehat{H}
-conjugacy class of
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant, that is, every
\Gamma_{F}
-Galois conjugate
{}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
is
\widehat{H}
-conjugate.

Since the
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy classes of the inclusion
\eta:\widehat{H}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
and of the composition

\eta\circ\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\hookrightarrow\widehat{H}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
are
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant, we conclude that the composition

\eta\circ{}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
is a
\widehat{G}
-conjugate of
\eta\circ\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
. Since
\eta
is an embedding, elements
\xi\in\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}
and
s\in\widehat{H}
are
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant, while
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}(\xi)=s
, we thus conclude that
{}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
is a
\widehat{G}_{s}
-conjugate of
\widehat{\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma}}
.

Since
\widehat{H}=(\widehat{G}_{s})^{0}
, it therefore suffices to show that the centralizer
\widehat{G}_{s}
is connected. But this follows from the fact
that
Z(G)
is connected, thus the derived group of
\widehat{G}
is simply connected.
∎

4.1.10
.

Remark.
The assertion of Section
4.1.9
(b) is completely false, if
Z(G)
is not connected.
For example, it is false for
G=\operatorname{SL}_{2}
.

The following notion will be used in order to deduce the general case to the case of groups with connected center.

Definition 4.1.11
.

Following
[
KV
, Definition 1.1.12]
, by a
quasi-isogeny
we call a homomorphism
\pi:G\to G_{1}
of connected reductive groups over
F
such that
\pi(Z(G))\subseteq Z(G_{1})
and the induced homomorphism
\pi^{\operatorname{ad}}:G^{\operatorname{ad}}\to G_{1}^{\operatorname{ad}}
of adjoint groups is an
isomorphism.

4.1.12
.

Functorial properties.
Let
\pi:G\to G_{1}
be a quasi-isogeny.

(a) Notice that for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}_{1}:S_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}
of a maximal torus over
F
its pullback
\mathfrak{a}:S\to G

is an embedding of a maximal torus and
\pi
induces a morphism
S\to S_{1}
hence a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism

\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{S_{1}}\to\widehat{S}
.

(b) Conversely, every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:S\hookrightarrow G
of a maximal torus over
F
gives rise to an embedding of a maximal torus
\mathfrak{a}_{1}:S_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}
, whose pullback is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{a}
, hence to a morphism
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{S_{1}}^{\Gamma_{F}}\to\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
.

(c) Note that
\pi
induces a quasi-isogeny
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{G_{1}}\to\widehat{G}
of dual groups, canonical up to a
\widehat{G}
-conjugacy.
In particular, the conjugacy class of
\widehat{\pi}
is
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant.

(d) As in
[
KV
, Lemma 1.3.10(a)]
, an endoscopic datum
\mathcal{E}=(s_{1},\widehat{H}_{1},\rho_{1})
for
G_{1}
gives rise to the endoscopic datum

\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E})=(s,\widehat{H},\rho)
for
G
, unique up to conjugacy. Namely, it is characterised by the property that if
\widehat{\pi}
be a quasi-isogeny from part (b), then
s=\widehat{\pi}(s_{1})
,
\widehat{H}=((\widehat{G_{1}})_{s_{1}})^{0}
and
\rho:\Gamma_{F}\to\operatorname{Out}(\widehat{H})
is the unique homomorphism such that the conjugacy classes of
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{H_{1}}\to\widehat{H}
and
\widehat{H}\hookrightarrow\widehat{G}
are
\Gamma_{F}
-invariant.

(e) In the situation of part (a), for every
\kappa\in\widehat{S_{1}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
we have
\widehat{\pi}(\kappa)\in\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
.
Moroever, the endoscopic datum
\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\kappa})
(see part (d) and Section
4.1.5
(a)) is conjugate to
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{a},\widehat{\pi}(\kappa)}
.

4.1.13
.

Observations.
Let
\pi:G\to G_{1}
be a quasi-isogeny.

(a) Note that
\pi
satisfies
\pi(G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F))\subseteq G_{1}^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\pi^{-1}(G_{1}^{\operatorname{rss}}(F))\subseteq G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
.
In particular, for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
we have
\pi(\gamma)\in G_{1}^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and

\pi
induces a homomorphism
\pi:G^{0}_{\gamma}\to(G_{1})^{0}_{\pi(\gamma)}
hence a homomorphism

\pi_{*}:H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})\to H^{1}(F,(G_{1})^{0}_{\pi(\gamma)})
and a

\Gamma_{F}
-equivariant homomorphism
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{(G_{1})^{0}_{\pi(\gamma)}}\to\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}
.

(b) The homomorphisms of part (a) are compatible with the pairing of Section
4.1.3
(c). In other words, for every

x\in H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})
and
\xi\in\widehat{(G_{1})^{0}_{\pi(\gamma)}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, we have an equality

\langle\widehat{\pi}(\xi),x\rangle=\langle\xi,\pi_{*}(x)\rangle.

(c) For every
\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
, stably conjugate to
\gamma
, its image
\pi(\gamma^{\prime})\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}_{1}(F)
is stably conjugate to
\pi(\gamma)
and we have an equality
\operatorname{inv}(\pi(\gamma),\pi(\gamma^{\prime}))=\pi_{*}(\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}))
.

(d) Let
\mathcal{E}
be as in Section
4.1.12
(b). Using Section
4.1.7
(b) one sees that for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\xi\in\widehat{(G_{1})_{\pi(\gamma)}}
such that
(\pi(\gamma),\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
, we have
(\gamma,\widehat{\pi}(\xi))\in\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E})
.

Lemma 4.1.14
.

Let
\pi:G\to G_{1}
is a quasi-isogeny of connected reductive groups over
F
, and let
\mathcal{E}
be an endoscopic triple for
G_{1}
.
Then for every
\mathcal{E}
-stable function
f
on
G^{\operatorname{rss}}_{1}(F)
its pullback
\pi^{*}(f)
to
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
is
\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E})
-stable.

Proof.

Though the result can be deduced from
[
KV
, Corollary 1.6.13]
where a more general result is proven,
we provide the argument for completeness.

We want to show that for any element
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
, the restriction
\pi^{*}(f)|_{\operatorname{Orb}^{\operatorname{st}}_{G(F)}(\gamma)}
lies in the span of

\operatorname{ev}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{\gamma}
, where
\xi^{\prime}\in\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
such that
(\gamma,\xi^{\prime})\in\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E})
.

By assumption, function
f
is
\mathcal{E}
-stable. Since
\pi(\gamma)\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}_{1}(F)
the restriction
f|_{\operatorname{Orb}^{\operatorname{st}}_{G_{1}(F)}(\pi(\gamma))}
is a finite sum
\sum_{\xi}a_{\xi}\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\pi(\gamma)}
, where
\xi
runs over elements of
\widehat{(G_{1})_{\pi(\gamma)}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
such that
(\pi(\gamma),\xi)\in\mathcal{E}
.

Then by Sections
4.1.13
(b),(c), we conclude that the restriction
\pi^{*}(f)|_{\operatorname{Orb}^{\operatorname{st}}_{G(F)}(\gamma)}

equals
\sum_{\xi}a_{\xi}\operatorname{ev}^{\widehat{\pi}(\xi)}_{\gamma}
, so the assertion follows from Section
4.1.13
(d).
∎

4.2.
Rationality

4.2.1
.

General observations.

(a) For every algebraic group
H
over
F
, the loop group
LH
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. For example, this applies
to
H=G
, to
H=G^{0}_{\gamma}
where
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and more generally for every torus
S
over
F
. In particular,
the group of connected components
\pi_{0}(LS)
is equipped with an action of
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
.

(b) Notice that since
F^{\operatorname{nr}}\subseteq K
is dense the restriction map
\Gamma_{K}\to\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}
is an isomorphism.
In particular, for every torus
S
over
F
, the group of coinvariants
X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}
and invariants
\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{K}}
are equipped with an action of
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
and we have identifications
(X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}})_{\sigma}\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{F}}
and
(\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{K}})^{\sigma}=\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{F}}
.

(c) For a torus
S
over
F
, the Kottwitz isomorphism
\pi_{0}(LS)\simeq X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}
(see Section
2.3.1
) is
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-equivariant.

4.2.2
.

The affine Weyl group.

(a) We claim that torus
\overline{T}_{G}
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, the affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}_{G}
is equipped with a
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-action, while the subgroups

\widetilde{W},\Lambda_{G}
and
\Omega_{G}
and
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-invariant. Indeed, this follows repeating the argument of Section
1.1.5
word-by-word. In particular, the factor group
\overline{W}=\widetilde{W}_{G}/\Lambda_{G}
is equipped with a
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-action, and the action of
\overline{W}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
is
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-equivariant.

(b) By the same argument as in part (a) one sees that the
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-action preserves the Bruhat order on
\widetilde{W}
. In particular, the subset

\widetilde{S}\subseteq\widetilde{W}
is
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-invariant, and the
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-action preserves the Demazure product (see Lemma
3.2.12
).

(c) For every pair of Iwahori subgroups
\mathbf{I}_{1},\mathbf{I}_{2}\subseteq LG
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, we have an equality
\widetilde{w}_{{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}_{1},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}_{2}}={}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{1},\mathbf{I}_{2}})
.

(d) Note that the sets of roots
\overline{\Phi}
, of affine roots
\widetilde{\Phi}
, and positive affine roots
\widetilde{\Phi}^{+}
are equipped with a

\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-action, and that the action of
\widetilde{W}
on
\widetilde{\Phi}
is
\Gamma_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
-equivariant. Therefore for every
m
-regular element
w\in\widetilde{W}
its Galois conjugate
{}^{\sigma}w
is
m
-regular as well.

(e) Since
G
splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, it splits over
F^{(n)}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{n}}((t))
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\sigma^{n}
acts trivially
on
\widetilde{W}_{G}
, and the torus
\overline{T}_{G}
splits over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{n}}
.

4.2.3
.

Parahoric subgroups.

(a) Note that the correspondence
\mathbf{P}\mapsto\mathbf{P}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
defines a bijection between

\sigma
-invariant parahoric subgroups of
LG
and parahoric subgroups of
LG(\mathbb{F}_{q})=G(F)
in the classical sense.

(b) Notice that if
\mathbf{P}
is a
\sigma
-invariant parahoric subgroup of
LG
, then its type
J_{\mathbf{P}}\subseteq\widetilde{S}
is

\sigma
-invariant.

(c) For every torus
S\subseteq G
over
F
, which splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, there exists a
\sigma
-stable parahoric subgroup

\mathbf{P}\subseteq LG
containing
L^{+}(S)
.

Proof.

First we claim that there exists a maximal torus
T^{\prime}\subseteq G
defined over
F
, which contains
S
and splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
. Indeed, since
G
and
S
split over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, the centralizer
H:=G_{S}
splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
as well. Then every maximal torus
T^{\prime}\subseteq H
defined over
F
and splitting over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
satisfies the required property.

Next, the embedding
T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow G
defines a morphism of Bruhat-Tits buildings
i:\mathcal{B}(T^{\prime})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(G)
, and every point in the image of
i
defines a parahoric subgroup of
G(F)
, hence (by part (a)) a
\sigma
-invariant parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}
of
LG
, containing
L^{+}(T^{\prime})\supseteq L^{+}(S)
.
∎

4.2.4
.

Affine flag varieties and affine Springer fibers.

(a) Since
G
is defined over
F
, the loop group
LG
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Moreover, the affine flag variety
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
is equipped with a structure over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. Namely, for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
the canonical isomorphism
{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
decomposes as

{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}{}^{\sigma}(LG/\mathbf{I})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG/{}^{\sigma}{\mathbf{I}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G},

where the first and the last isomorphisms are from Section
2.2.1
(a). Moreover, the closed ind-subscheme
\operatorname{Fl}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}

and the action map
LG\times\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
are
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational.

(b) For every parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{Q}
of
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
and
w\in\widetilde{W}
,
we set
\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{w}:=\mathbf{Q}w\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}
. Then the isomorphism
{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}
induces an identification

{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{Q}}^{w}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{Q}}^{{}^{\sigma}w}
.

(c) Let
\mathbf{I}\subseteq LG
be an Iwahori subgroup, and let
J,J^{\prime}\subseteq\widetilde{S}

be
\sigma
-invariant subsets such that parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}\subseteq LG
is
\sigma
-invariant.
Then it follows from parts (a),(b) and Section
4.2.2
that the filtration

\{\operatorname{Fl}_{\mathbf{I}}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{reg}}\}_{n}
of
\operatorname{Fl}
is
\sigma
-invariant.

(d) For every
\gamma\in G(K)=LG(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, the isomorphism
{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
from part (a) induces an isomorphism

{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\simeq\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma}
and this isomorphism identifies the reduction map
{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to{}^{\sigma}\overline{T}_{G}\simeq\overline{T}_{G}
with
\operatorname{red}_{{}^{\sigma}\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
.

In particular, for every
\gamma\in G(F)=LG(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
and the reduction map
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
are
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational.

4.2.5
.

Application to homology.
Let
\gamma\in LG(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
and let
\mathcal{L}
be a local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
.

(a) Using observations of Section
4.2.4
(d), we have a natural isomorphism

a_{\sigma,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}({}^{\sigma}\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\sigma_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}}).

Furthermore, for every
g\in LG(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
the following diagram is commutative

\begin{CD}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})@>{a_{\sigma,\mathcal{L}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}})\\
@V{l_{g}}V{}V@V{}V{l_{{}^{\sigma}g}}V\\
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})@>{a_{\sigma,\mathcal{L}}}>{}>H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}}),\end{CD}

with
\gamma^{\prime}=g\gamma g^{-1}
(compare Proposition
2.2.7
(d)).

(b) For every
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
we have an natural isomorphism
\sigma_{*}\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}\simeq({}^{\sigma}\overline{w})_{*}\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}
.
Furthermore, this isomorphism identifies an isomorphism

a_{\sigma,w_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{w,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{*}\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})

with

a_{{}^{\sigma}w,\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}}\circ a_{\sigma,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,{}^{\sigma}\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{({}^{\sigma}\overline{w})_{*}\sigma_{*}\mathcal{L}}).

Indeed, it suffices to show this assertion for
w=s_{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in\widetilde{S}
and
w\in\Omega_{G}
. In both cases, the assertion follows
by unwinding the definitions (see part (a) and Proposition
2.2.7
).

(c) Assume now that
\gamma\in LG(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Then, by part (b), for every
u\in\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
we have a natural isomorphism

a_{u,\mathcal{L}}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{u}_{*}\mathcal{L}}),

where
\overline{u}\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
is the image of
u
, which coincides with that of part (a) when
u=\sigma
, coincides with that of
Proposition
2.2.7
when
u\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
, and such that the assignment
u\mapsto a_{u,\mathcal{L}}
is compatible with products/compositions.

(d) Assume now that
\gamma\in LG(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and
\mathcal{L}
is a
\overline{W}
-equivariant Weil sheaf. Then for every
\overline{u}\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
we have a canonical
isomorphism
\overline{u}_{*}\mathcal{L}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathcal{L}
, thus the construction of part (c) provides each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
with a

(\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle)
-action.

4.3.
Maximal unramified tori

The goal of this section is to construct a generalization of the correspondence
Section
1.3.1
to the affine setting.

4.3.1
.

Relative position.

(a) Let
T\subseteq G
be a maximal torus over
F
, split over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, and let
\mathbf{I}
be an Iwahori subgroup of
LG
, defined over

\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and containing
\mathbf{T}
. Then
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}
is another Iwahori subgroup of
LG
, so we can consider their relative position
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}:=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}\in\widetilde{W}
, and let
\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\overline{W}
be the image of
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
.

(b) Let
\varphi=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
be an admissible isomorphism. Then
{}^{\sigma}\varphi
is also an admissible isomorphism
\varphi_{T,{}^{\sigma}{\mathbf{I}}}
, and we have an equality
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=\overline{w}^{-1}_{\mathbf{I}}\circ\varphi
(see Section
2.1.5
(b)).
In particular, element
\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\in\overline{W}
only depends on
T
and
\varphi
, so we can set
\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}:=\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
.

4.3.2
.

Example.
Let
(T,\mathbf{I})
be as in Section
4.3.1
, and let
\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}:N_{LG}(LT)/\mathbf{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\widetilde{W}_{G}
be the canonical isomorphism. Then
{}^{\sigma}{\mathbf{I}}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
is an Iwahori subgroup as well, and we have an equality

\operatorname{pr}_{{}^{\sigma}{\mathbf{I}}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}\circ\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Therefore for every element
g\in N_{LG}(LT)
we have an equality

{}^{\sigma}\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}([g])=\operatorname{pr}_{{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}([{}^{\sigma}g])=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{\mathbf{I}}([{}^{\sigma}g])\circ\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}.

Notation 4.3.3
.

(a) Denote by
\widetilde{W}_{\sigma-\operatorname{tor}}
the set of
\sigma
-torsion

elements of
\widetilde{W}
, that is, those elements

\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
such that

N_{n}(\widetilde{w}):=\widetilde{w}\cdot{}^{\sigma}\widetilde{w}\cdot\ldots\cdot{}^{\sigma^{n-1}}\widetilde{w}
is
1

for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(b) For every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
4.3.1
, we denote by
T^{\operatorname{sc}}
(resp
\mathbf{I}^{\operatorname{sc}})
) the corresponding maximal torus of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}
(resp. Iwahori subgroup of
LG^{\operatorname{sc}}
), and we set
\mathbf{T}^{\operatorname{sc}}:=L^{+}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})
. In particular, we have a canonical isomorphism

\widetilde{W}\simeq N_{LG^{\operatorname{sc}}}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})/\mathbf{T}^{\operatorname{sc}}
.

(c) For every two pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
as in Section
4.3.1
there exists an element

g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}(K)=LG^{\operatorname{sc}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
such that
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})=g(T,\mathbf{I})g^{-1}
.
Then element
h:=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g
satisfies
hTh^{-1}=T
, thus
h\in N_{LG^{\operatorname{sc}}}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})
.

(d) We say that
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
from part (c) are
stably conjugate
, if there exists
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
as in part (c) such that the isomorphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T^{\prime}:t\mapsto gtg^{-1}
is defined over
F
. Notice that this happens if and only if element
h:=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g
belongs to
LT^{\operatorname{sc}}
.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

(a) A correspondence
(T,\mathbf{I})\mapsto\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
gives an injection
from the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
as is Section
4.3.1
to the set
\widetilde{W}_{\sigma-\operatorname{tor}}
.

(b) A correspondence
(T,\mathbf{I})\mapsto\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
gives an injection from the set of stably conjugacy classes of pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
as above to
the set
\overline{W}
.

(c) If
G
is quasi-split, then the correspondences of parts (a) and (b) are bijective.

Proof.

For the convenience of the reader, we will divide the proof into five steps:

Step 1.
For every pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
4.3.1
, the conjugate
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}
is another Iwahori subgroup
of
LG
containing
\mathbf{T}
. Hence there exists
g\in N_{LG^{\operatorname{sc}}}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})
such that
{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}=g\mathbf{I}g^{-1}
, and by definition

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
is the class
[g]\in N_{LG^{\operatorname{sc}}}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})/\mathbf{T}^{\operatorname{sc}}\simeq\widetilde{W}
.

Step 2.
By definition,
T
splits over a finite unramified extension
F^{(n)}:=\mathbb{F}_{q^{n}}((t))
of
F
. Then
{}^{\sigma^{n}}\mathbf{I}=\mathbf{I}
. Since each

{}^{\sigma^{i}}\mathbf{I}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
is an Iwahori subgroup of
LG
, we deduce from Section
2.1.3
(e) that

N_{n}(\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}})=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot{}^{\sigma}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot\ldots\cdot{}^{\sigma^{n-1}}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{I}}\cdot\ldots\cdot\widetilde{w}_{{}^{\sigma^{n-1}}\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma^{n}}\mathbf{I}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma^{n}}\mathbf{I}}=1,

thus
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
is
\sigma
-torsion.

Step 3.
Let
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
be two pairs as in Section
4.3.3
(c), choose element
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that

(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})=g(T,\mathbf{I})g^{-1}
, and set
h:=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g
. Then we have
h\in N_{LG^{\operatorname{sc}}}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})
, and we have an equality

(4.2)

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=[h]\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}.

Indeed, choose element
g_{0}\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that

(T,{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I})=g_{0}(T,\mathbf{I})g_{0}^{-1}
. Then

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{g\mathbf{I}g^{-1},({}^{\sigma}g)({}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I})({}^{\sigma}g)^{-1}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},h({}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I})h^{-1}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},(hg_{0})\mathbf{I}(hg_{0})^{-1}},

and it follows from Step 1 that

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=[hg_{0}]=[h]\cdot[g_{0}]=[h]\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}.

Step 4.
Assertions (a) and (b) easily follows from equality (
4.2
): Indeed, if pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate,
then we can assume that
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
, thus
h=1
, hence
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Conversely, if
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
, then
[h]=1
, thus
h\in L^{+}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})
. Then, by Lang’s theorem, there exists an element
t\in L^{+}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})
such that
h=t^{-1}{}^{\sigma}t
. Then
g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g=t^{-1}{}^{\sigma}t
, hence
gt^{-1}\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
, so pair

(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})=g(T,\mathbf{I})g^{-1}=gt^{-1}(T,\mathbf{I})(gt^{-1})^{-1}

is
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate to
(\mathbf{T},I)
.

Next,
\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}=\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
if and only if

h=g^{-1}g^{\sigma}\in LT^{\operatorname{sc}}
, or, what is the same, if and only if pairs
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
are stably conjugate.

Step 5.
Assume now that
G
is quasi-split, thus there exists a pair

(T_{0},\mathbf{I}_{0})
as in Section
4.3.1
, where
\mathbf{I}_{0}
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
. We set
N_{0}:=N_{G}(T_{0})
.

To show that the correspondence of part (a) is surjective, we need to show that every
\sigma
-torsion element

\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
comes from some pair
(T,\mathbf{I})
. First we claim that element

\widetilde{w}
lifts to a
\sigma
-torsion element
h_{\widetilde{w}}\in N_{0}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
. Indeed, choose
m
such that
N_{m}(\widetilde{w})=1
and

\sigma^{m}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{W}
. Then
\widetilde{w}
gives
rise to a
1
-cocycle

c_{\widetilde{w}}\in Z^{1}(F^{(m)}/F,\widetilde{W}_{G})\subseteq Z^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F,\widetilde{W}_{G})

such that

c_{\widetilde{w}}(\sigma)=\widetilde{w}
. Since
\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
,
we have
H^{2}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F,A)=0
for every

\Gamma_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F}
-module
A
. Hence by
[
Se
, Corollary, page 54]
,
1
-cocycle
c_{\widetilde{w}}
lifts to a
1
-cocycle
\widetilde{c}_{\widetilde{w}}\in Z^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F,N_{0}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}))
.

Since
G^{\operatorname{sc}}
is semisimple and simply connected, we get that

H^{1}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}/F,G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}}))=H^{1}(F,G^{\operatorname{sc}})=0.

Thus there exists
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
\widetilde{c}_{\widetilde{w}}(\sigma)=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g
. Then

(T,\mathbf{I}):=g(T_{0},\mathbf{I}_{0})g^{-1}
is the pair satisfying

\widetilde{w}_{I}=\widetilde{w}
.

Finally, since
G
is quasi-split and split over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, group
G(F)
has a hyperspecial parahoric subgroup.
This subgroup gives rise to a
\sigma
-invariant parahoric subgroup
L^{+}G\subseteq LG
, hence (by Section
2.1.8
(b)) to a
\sigma
-equivariant section

s:\overline{W}\to\widetilde{W}
of the projection
\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}:\widetilde{W}\to\overline{W}
. Therefore every
\overline{w}\in\overline{W}
has a
\sigma
-torsion
lift
s(\overline{w})\in\widetilde{W}
. Thus the correspondence of part (b) is surjective as well.
∎

4.3.5
.

Compatibility.
The constructions of Sections
1.3.1
and
4.3.1
are
compatible: Let
T\subseteq G
be a maximal
unramified torus over
F
such that
\mathbf{T}=L^{+}(T)
is contained in a parahoric
subgroup
\mathbf{P}
of
LG
. Then
\overline{T}
is a maximal torus of

M_{\mathbf{P}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(compare Section
2.1.6
(a)), while the finite Weyl group
W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}
is naturally a
subgroup of
\widetilde{W}_{G}
.

Moreover, any Borel subgroup

\overline{B}\supseteq\overline{T}
of
M_{\mathbf{P}}
(defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
) gives rise to
the unique Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
of
LG
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
such that

\mathbf{T}\subseteq\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{I}/\mathbf{P}^{+}=\overline{B}
.

We claim that element
w_{\overline{T},\overline{B}}\in W_{M_{\mathbf{P}}}\subseteq\widetilde{W}

equals
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}
. Indeed, since

w_{\overline{B}}=w_{\overline{B},{}^{\sigma}\overline{B}}
, while

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}
, the assertion follows from
the equality
w_{\overline{B},{}^{\sigma}\overline{B}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}
from
Section
2.1.6
(f).

Notation 4.3.6
.

Consider two pairs
(T,\varphi)
and
(T^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
, where
T,T^{\prime}\subseteq G

are maximal unramified tori and
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
and
\varphi^{\prime}:\overline{T}^{\prime}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}

are admissible isomorphisms (see Section
2.1.5
(a)).

(a) We say that
(T,\varphi)
and
(T^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
are
conjugate
(resp.
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate
), if there exists
g\in G(F)
(resp.
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
) such that we have
gTg^{-1}=T^{\prime}
and
\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{Int}g:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
.

(b) We say that
(T,\varphi)
and
(T^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
are
stably conjugate
, if there exists an element
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
gTg^{-1}=T^{\prime},\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
, and the inner isomorphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:T\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T^{\prime}
is defined over
F
.
12
12

12

As in Section
4.1.2
(b), this happens if and only if there exists
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
satisfying this property.

Corollary 4.3.7
.

(a) The correspondence
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})\mapsto[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}]

defines an injection from the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of
pairs
(T,\varphi)
as in Section
4.3.6
to the set
\Lambda_{\sigma-conj}\backslash\widetilde{W}_{\sigma-\operatorname{tor}}
of

\sigma
-conjugacy classes of
\Lambda
acting on
\widetilde{W}_{\sigma-\operatorname{tor}}
.

(b) The correspondence
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})\mapsto\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}}

defines an injection from the set of stably conjugacy classes of
pairs
(T,\varphi)
as in Section
4.3.6
to the set
\overline{W}
.

(c) If
G
is quasi-split, then correspondences of parts (a) and (b) are bijective.

Proof.

(a) Note that if
\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{I}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
are two Iwahori subgroups of
LG

defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, then we have an equality

(4.3)

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I},{}^{\sigma}\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}^{-1}\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\cdot{}^{\sigma}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}

(compare Section
2.1.2
(e)). If in addition
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
, then we have

\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=1
(see Section
2.1.5
(b)), or, what is the same,

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}\in\Lambda
. Thus the correspondence
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})\mapsto[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}]\in\Lambda_{\sigma-\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\widetilde{W}_{\sigma-\operatorname{tor}}
is well-defined.

Next, let
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
be two pairs as in Section
4.3.1
such that

(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})
and
(T^{\prime},\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate. Then there exists
an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
such that
(T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate and
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}
.
Then, by the proven above, we have
[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}]=[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}]
, and
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
by
Lemma
4.3.4
(a). Thus,
[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}]=[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}]
, as claimed.

Conversely, assume that
(T,\mathbf{I})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
be such that
[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}]=[\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}]
.
Then there exists
\lambda\in\Lambda
such that
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}=\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}{}^{\sigma}\lambda
.
Choose an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
such that
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\lambda
. Then, arguing as above, we see that

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}{}^{\sigma}\lambda
, thus
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}}
. So, by Lemma
4.3.4
(a),
the pairs
(T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime})
and
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate, thus pairs
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})=(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}})
and

(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate.

(b)-(c) Observe that
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})
is stably conjugate to
(T^{\prime},\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime}})
if and only if there exists
an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
such that
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime}}=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}
and
(T,\mathbf{I}^{\prime\prime})
is stably conjugate to
(T^{\prime},\mathbf{I}^{\prime})
. Now, as in part (a), all assertions follow from the corresponding assertions of Lemma
4.3.4
.
∎

Notation 4.3.8
.

Choose a minimal
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sigma^{n}
acts trivially on
\widetilde{W}_{G}
, and let
\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}

be the cyclic group of order
n
, generated by
\sigma
.

(a) Note that an element
w\in\widetilde{W}
is
\sigma
-torsion if and only if the element

w\sigma\in\widetilde{W}\sigma\subseteq\widetilde{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}

is torsion. Moreover, for every
\lambda\in\Lambda
we have an equality

\lambda^{-1}(w\sigma)\lambda=(\lambda^{-1}\cdot w\cdot{}^{\sigma}\lambda)\sigma
.

(b) For every
(T,\mathbf{I})
as in Section
4.3.1
, we set
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}}:=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}}\sigma\in\widetilde{W}\sigma
.

(c) We denote by
(\widetilde{W}\sigma)_{\operatorname{tor}}
the set of elements of
\widetilde{W}\sigma
, which are torsion
as elements of
\widetilde{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
. Using observation of part (a), Corollary
4.3.7
implies that the correspondence
(T,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}})\mapsto[\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}}]
defines an injection from the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of
pairs
(T,\varphi)
as in Section
4.3.6
to the set of
conjugacy classes
\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash(\widetilde{W}\sigma)_{\operatorname{tor}}
.

Notation 4.3.9
.

Let
(T,\varphi)
be as in Section
4.3.6
, and let
n
be as in Section
4.3.8
.

(a) Denote by

\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\in\overline{W}
the image of
(T,\varphi)

under the correspondence of Corollary
4.3.7
(b), and set

\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}:=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\sigma\in\overline{W}\sigma\subseteq\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
. Notice that the notation
is compatible with that of Section
4.3.1
(b).

(b) Denote by
[\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash(\widetilde{W}\sigma)_{\operatorname{tor}}
the image of
(T,\varphi)

under the correspondence of Corollary
4.3.7
(a).

(c) For each embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the
inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
(see Section
4.1.2
), we set
T_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\mathfrak{a}(T)
and

\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\varphi\circ\mathfrak{a}^{-1}:\overline{T}_{\mathfrak{a}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
.
Then
\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}
is an admissible isomorphism, pair
(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
is stably conjugate to
(T,\varphi)
,
and we set

[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]:=[\widetilde{u}_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash(\widetilde{W}\sigma)_{\operatorname{tor}}.

4.3.10
.

Remarks.
Suppose that we are in the situation of Section
4.3.9
.

(a) The map
\mathfrak{a}\mapsto(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
gives a bijection between the set of
embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
, and the set of pairs
(T^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
, stably conjugate to
(T,\varphi)
. Moreover, the embeddings
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate (resp. conjugate) if and only if the pairs

(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
and
(T_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}})
are
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugate (resp. conjugate).

(b) Since
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\circ\varphi
(see Section
4.3.1
(b)),
\varphi
induces an isomorphism
X_{*}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(\overline{T}_{G})\simeq\Lambda_{G}
, which interchange the action of
\sigma
on
X_{*}(T)
with the action of
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
on
\Lambda_{G}
, so induces an isomorphism
X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{F}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}(\Lambda_{G})_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}
. Composing it with the isomorphism

H^{1}(F,T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{F},\operatorname{tor}}
(see Section
4.1.1
(b)), we get an isomorphism
H^{1}(F,T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}(\Lambda_{G})_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\operatorname{tor}}
,
hence a homomorphism

\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\operatorname{tor}}\to(\Lambda_{G})_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\operatorname{tor}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{1}(F,T).

(c) Let
T^{\operatorname{sc}}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}
be the pullback of
T\subseteq G
. Then
T^{\operatorname{sc}}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}
is a maximal elliptic torus, and
\varphi
induces an admissible isomorphism
\varphi:\overline{T}^{\operatorname{sc}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
and we have equalities
\overline{w}_{T^{\operatorname{sc}},\varphi}=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\in\overline{W}_{G}=\overline{G}_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}
, thus
\overline{u}_{T^{\operatorname{sc}},\varphi}=\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
. Therefore applying the observation of the part (b) to
T^{\operatorname{sc}}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}
instead of
T\subseteq G
, we get an isomorphism
H^{1}(F,T^{\operatorname{sc}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\operatorname{tor}}
.

(d) Let
\pi_{G}:\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}\to\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
be the natural projection.
Since for every
\widetilde{u}\in\pi_{G}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
and
\mu\in\Lambda_{G}
we have an identity

\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}\mu=(\mu^{-1}{}^{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}\mu)\widetilde{u}
, the map
([\lambda],[\widetilde{u}])\mapsto[\lambda\widetilde{u}]
gives an action of
the group
(\Lambda_{G})_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}
on
(\Lambda_{G})_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
.

Applying this observation to
T^{\operatorname{sc}}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}
instead of
T\subseteq G
, we get the action of the group
\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}
on
\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
. Moreover, by a straightforward calculation one checks that for every pair of elements
[\widetilde{u}],[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
there exists a unique element
\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}],[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}])\in\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}
such that
\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}],[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}])\cdot[\widetilde{u}]=[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}]
.

Furthermore, we have

\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}],[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}])\in(\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}})_{\operatorname{tor}}
, if
[\widetilde{u}],[\widetilde{u}^{\prime}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})_{\operatorname{tor}}
.

Lemma 4.3.11
.

In the notation of Section
4.3.9
,

(a) The map
\mathfrak{a}\mapsto[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]
gives a bijection between the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy
classes of embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the
inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
, and elements of
\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})_{\operatorname{tor}}\subseteq\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash(\widetilde{W}\sigma)_{\operatorname{tor}}
.

(b) For a pair of embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
as in part (a), the relative position
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,T)
(see Section
4.1.2
(c))
is the image of the relative position
\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}],[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}])\in(\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}})_{\operatorname{tor}}
(see Section
4.3.10
(d)) under the map of Section
4.3.10
(b).

(c) Two embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
as in part (a) are
G(F)
-conjugate if and only if there exists an element
\mu\in\Lambda_{G}
such that
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}]=\mu^{-1}[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]\mu
.

Proof.

(a) Each
(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
is stably conjugate to
(T,\varphi)
,
therefore we have an equality
\overline{w}_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}}=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}
(by Corollary
4.3.7
(b)), thus
\overline{u}_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}}=\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
,
hence
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\phi})_{\operatorname{tor}}
.

Next, observe that two embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
are conjugate if
and only if
(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
and
(T_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}})
are conjugate. Thus it follows from Corollary
4.3.7
that the correspondence is well defined and injective.

Finally, to show the surjectivity, we notice that
\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})_{\operatorname{tor}}

is a principal homogeneous space with respect to the group
\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\operatorname{tor}}
(by Section
4.3.10
(d)), so the assertion follows by a combination of observations of Sections
4.3.10
(c),
4.1.2
(e) and
part (b) of the lemma, applied to
T^{\operatorname{sc}}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}
instead of
T\subseteq G
.

(b) By assumption, there exists an element
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}\circ\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
and
h:=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g\in LT^{\operatorname{sc}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.
Then the relative position
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})
is the projection of
[h]\in\pi_{0}(LT^{\operatorname{sc}})\simeq X_{*}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})
under the map

X_{*}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})\to X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{F}}\simeq H^{1}(F,T)

(see Sections
4.1.1
(b),(c)), while the relative position
\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}],[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}])

is the image of
[h]
under the map

X_{*}(T^{\operatorname{sc}})\simeq\Lambda\to\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}

(see equality (
4.2
) and Sections
4.3.10
(b),(c)).

(c) By definition,
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
is
G(F)
-conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}
if and only if
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})=1
. Combining part (b) with observations of
Section
4.3.10
(c), this follows if and only if elements

[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}],[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
have the same image in

(\Lambda_{G})_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
, that is, there exists an element
\mu\in\Lambda_{G}
such that
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}]=\mu^{-1}[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]\mu
.
∎

4.3.12
.

Elliptic case.
Assume that maximal torus
T\subseteq G
is elliptic, and let
Z_{0}\subseteq Z(G)^{0}
be the maximal split torus.
Then
\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}\simeq\Lambda_{G}/X_{*}(Z_{0})
, and the endomorphisms
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}|_{\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}}
and
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}|_{\Lambda}
are elliptic in the sense of Section
A.4.3
(a) (use Section
4.3.2
and compare Section
1.3.1
(d)).

(a) Since automorphism
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}|_{\Lambda}
is elliptic, all elements of
\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})\subseteq\widetilde{W}\sigma

are torsion in
\widetilde{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
. More precisely, using Lemma
A.4.4
(b) one sees that the order of every
\widetilde{u}\in\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})\subseteq\widetilde{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
equals the order of
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle_{n}
. Thus Lemma
4.3.11
(a) asserts that in this case the map
\mathfrak{a}\mapsto[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]
gives a bijection between the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy
classes of embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the
inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
, and the set
\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
.

(b) By Lemma
4.3.11
(c), for every
\mu\in\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}/\Lambda
such that
\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu\in\Lambda\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
there exists
an embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
such that
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]=[\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu]
. Moreover,
\mathfrak{a}
is unique up to a
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy.

(c) Since
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}|_{\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}}
is elliptic, for every
1\neq\mu\in\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}
we have

\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu\neq\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
. Therefore it follows from Lemma
4.3.11
that for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, there exists a unique
\mu\in\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}/\Lambda
such that
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]=[\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu]
.

4.4.
Main results

The goal of this section is to formulate a geometric description of characters of cuspidal Deligne–Lusztig representations and of their stable
and
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
-stable linear combinations.

4.4.1
.

Deligne–Lusztig representations over local non-archimedean
fields
. Let
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
be an embedding of a maximal unramified elliptic torus of
G
over
F
,
and let
\theta:T(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
be a tamely ramified character in general position (see parts (c),(d) below).

(a) Note that there exists a unique
\sigma
-invariant parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq LG
containing
\mathbf{T}_{\mathfrak{a}}=\mathfrak{a}(\mathbf{T})
(see, for example,

[
KV
, Section 2.1.3]
). We set
M_{\mathfrak{a}}:=M_{\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
and
G_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Then
G_{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq G(F)
is a parahoric subgroup,
G^{+}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is a pro-unipotent radical, and
G_{\mathfrak{a}}/G^{+}_{\mathfrak{a}}\simeq M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

(b) Since
T
is a torus over
F
, split over
K
, its reduction
\overline{T}
(see Section
2.1.1
) is a torus over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
Then the embedding
\mathfrak{a}:\mathbf{T}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}}
defines an embedding
\overline{\mathfrak{a}}:\overline{T}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{a}}
of a maximal elliptic torus of
M_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

(c) Assume that
\theta
is
tamely ramified
, that is, the restriction

\theta|_{T(\mathcal{O})}:T(\mathcal{O})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
factors through a character

\overline{\theta}:\overline{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
. Then
(\overline{T},\overline{\theta})
gives rise to a Deligne–Lusztig virtual representation

R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}:=R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}(\overline{T})}
of
M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

(d) Assume moreover that
\theta
is
in general position
,
that is, not fixed by any non-trivial element of the Weyl group
W_{G,\mathfrak{a}(T)}(F)
. Then
\overline{\theta}
is in general position in the sense of

[
DL
, Definition 5.15]
, thus
R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}
is cuspidal and irreducible (up to a sign), because
\overline{\mathfrak{a}}(\overline{T})\subseteq M_{\mathfrak{a}}
is elliptic.

(e) We consider subgroup
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=G_{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot Z(G)(F)\subseteq G(F)
and denote by
R^{\theta}_{\mathfrak{a}}
the virtual representation of
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
, whose restriction to
G_{\mathfrak{a}}
is the inflation of
R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}
, and the restriction to
Z(G)(F)
is
\theta|_{Z(G)(F)}
.

(f) Let
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
be the induced representation
\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}}^{G(F)}R_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\theta}
of
G(F)
. Since
R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}
is cuspidal and irreducible, we deduce from
[
MP
, Proposition 6.6]
, that
\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
is an irreducible virtual cuspidal representation. In particular, its character
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}
is an invariant generalized function on
G(F)
supported on elements, conjugate to
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
. Moreover, the restriction
f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
of
\chi_{\pi_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}}
to
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
is a locally constant function. Notice that
f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
only depends on the conjugacy class of
\mathfrak{a}
.

(g) If
T\subseteq G
is a maximal unramified elliptic torus and
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
is an inclusion, we write
f_{T,\theta}
instead of
f_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}
.

Our first main result gives a geometric interpretation of
f_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
for compact regular semisimple elements
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
.

4.4.2
.

Notation.
Let
T
be an unramified torus over
F
, and let

\theta:T(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
be a tamely ramified character, that is, its restriction

\theta|_{T(\mathcal{O})}:T(\mathcal{O})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
factors through a character

\overline{\theta}:\overline{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

(a) Character
\overline{\theta}:\overline{T}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
gives rise to a
one-dimensional local system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
on
\overline{T}
,
equipped with a Weil structure.

(b) Then every admissible isomorphism
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
as in Section
2.1.5
(a) gives
rise to local system
\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\varphi_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta})
on
\overline{T}_{G}
.

(c) The group
\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
acts naturally on
the set admissible isomorphisms by
the rule
w(\varphi):=w\circ\varphi
and
\sigma(\varphi):={}^{\sigma}\varphi
.
Using the identity
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi
(see Section
4.3.1
(b)), the cyclic group

\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle\subseteq\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
is the stabilizer of
\varphi
.

(d) As in Section
1.3.2
, we have natural isomorphisms

\sigma_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\theta,{}^{\sigma}\varphi}
and
w_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\theta,w\circ\varphi}
.
Moreover, we have natural isomorphisms
u_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\theta,u(\varphi)}
for
u\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
compatible with compositions.

(e) Consider local system
\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}:=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})
of
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
. Then it follows from part (e) and Section
4.2.5
(c) that
for every
\widetilde{u}\in\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
we have a natural isomorphism

a_{\widetilde{u},\theta,\varphi}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{u}(\varphi)}).

Moreover, these isomorphisms are compatible with compositions.

(f) Combining parts (c)-(e), each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is equipped with an action of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
. In particular, each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is equipped with an action of
\Lambda_{G}
.
Furthermore, each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module (by Proposition
3.3.2
(a)).

4.4.3
.

Construction.
Let
(T,\varphi)
be as in Section
4.3.6
, and let
\theta
be as in Section
4.4.2
.

(a) Let
[\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\widetilde{W}\sigma
be the class, corresponding to a pair
(T,\varphi)
(see Notation
4.3.9
(b)), and fix a representative
\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\in\widetilde{W}\sigma
of
[\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}]
. Then element
\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\in\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)\subseteq\widetilde{W}\ltimes\langle\sigma\rangle
is a lift of
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
, therefore we have a natural isomorphism
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)\simeq\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
.

(b) By part (a) and Section
4.4.2
(e), we see that each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
(resp.
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
) is a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module (resp.
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module), which is finitely generated as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module (resp.
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda]
-module).

(c) Let
Z_{0}\subseteq Z(G)
be the maximal
F
-split torus, and set
\Lambda_{0}:=X_{*}(Z_{0})\subseteq\Lambda_{G}
.
Then
\Lambda_{0}\subseteq\Omega_{G}
naturally acts on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
(compare Proposition
2.2.7
).
Since
\Lambda_{G}/\Lambda_{0}\simeq\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}
, we conclude that the space of coinvariants
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}
is a

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module, which is finitely generated as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}]
-module.

(d) Since maximal torus
T\subseteq G
is elliptic, the element
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
induces an elliptic automorphisms of
\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}
and
\Lambda

(compare Sections
1.3.1
(d) and
A.4.3
(a)). Moreover, modules
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}
and
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
are
\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
-locally finite (see Theorem
4.4.4
(a) below).

(e) By parts (b)-(d) and Section
A.4.5
(a) we can form the generalized traces

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}})\text{ and }\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

Therefore we can form the generalized trace

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}):=\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\varphi},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}})

and similarly
\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))
.

(f) For an embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to the inclusion
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
, we fix a representative
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}\in\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
of
[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}]=[\widetilde{u}_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}}}]\in\Lambda_{\operatorname{conj}}\backslash\pi_{G^{\operatorname{sc}}}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
(see Lemma
4.3.11
(a) and Notation
4.3.9
(c)).

Theorem 4.4.4
.

Let
T\subseteq G
be a maximal unramified elliptic torus of
G
, let
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
be the inclusion, let
\theta:T(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
be a tamely ramified character in general position, let
\gamma\in G(F)
be a compact regular semisimple element, and let
\varphi:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
be an admissible isomorphism.

Then, in the notation of Section
4.4.3
,

(a) modules
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
and
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
are

\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
-locally finite;

(b) we have equalities

(4.4)

f_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=\sum_{\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))=\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}),

where

\bullet

\mathfrak{a}
runs over a set of representatives of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
;

\bullet
the generalized trace
\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))
exists by the observation of Section
4.4.3
(e) applied to the pair
(T_{\mathfrak{a}},\varphi_{\mathfrak{a}})
(see Notation
4.3.9
(c)).

4.4.5
.

Notation.

Let
T,\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\theta
be as in Theorem
4.4.4
.

(a) We set

f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}:=\sum_{\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G}f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta},

where the sum runs over a set of representatives of conjugacy classes of embeddings

\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, which are stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.

(b) More generally, for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, we set
