f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}:=\sum_{\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G}\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})\rangle f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta},

where the
\mathfrak{a}
’s are is as in part (a), the invariant
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})\in H^{1}(F,T)
is defined in
Section
4.1.2
(c), and the pairing is defined in Section
4.1.3
(c). Note that
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}=f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}
when
\kappa=1
.

Our second main result gives a geometric interpretation of
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
and
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
for compact regular semisimple elements
\gamma
.

4.4.6
.

Notation.

(a) Consider the local system

\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}:=\bigoplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}
on

\overline{T}_{G}
, where
\varphi
runs over the set of all admissible
isomorphisms
\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
. Then it follows from Section
4.4.2
(d) that for every
u\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
we have a canonical isomorphism

u_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})=\bigoplus_{\varphi}u_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\bigoplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,u(\varphi)}=\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}},

making
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}
is a
\overline{W}
-equivariant Weil sheaf.

(b) Set
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}:=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})=\oplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}
. By part (a) and Section
4.2.5
(d), each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is equipped with an action of

\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
. In particular, we have a
\langle\sigma\rangle
-action on each vector space

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))
.

(c) By Proposition
3.3.2
, each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
is a
finitely-generated
\Lambda_{G}
-module, therefore the space

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))=H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))^{\overline{W}}

is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module. Moreover,
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))
is non-zero only for finitely
many pairs
(i,j)
.

(d) By part (c), we can form the virtual finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module

H_{*}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))):=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))),

hence also its trace
\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{*}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})))\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

4.4.7
.

Notation.

(a) As it was already mentioned in Section
4.4.2
(f), each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is equipped with an action of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
and is finitely generated as a

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.

(b) Note the algebra of regular functions
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{T}^{\Gamma}]
is naturally identified with the group algebra
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(T)_{\Gamma_{F}}]

(see Section
4.1.3
(c)), hence with
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[(\Lambda_{G})_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}]
(see Sections
4.3.10
(b) and
4.4.2
(c))). In particular, each
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma}
can be viewed as a
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
-invariant character

\Lambda_{G}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
.

(c) As it was already mentioned in Section
4.4.3
(a), we have a natural isomorphism

\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)\simeq\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
. Therefore character

\kappa:\Lambda_{G}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
from part (b) uniquely extends to a character of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
, trivial on
\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
. We denote by
(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}
the one-dimensional representation of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
, corresponding to
\kappa
.

(d) Consider the representation
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}:=H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}
of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
. It is a finitely generated
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module, so the space

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})

is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module. Again it is non-zero only for finitely
many pairs
(i,j)
.

(e) By part (d), we can form the virtual finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-module

H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}):=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}),

hence also its trace
\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta})_{\kappa}))\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

Theorem 4.4.8
.

(a) For every
T,\theta
and
\gamma
as in Theorem
4.4.4
, we have an equality

f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{*}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))).

(b) Furthermore, for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
and
\varphi
as in Theorem
4.4.4
, we have an equality

f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda_{G},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta})_{\kappa})).

As an application, we will prove stability properties of
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}
and
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
, thus establishing
the equal characteristic version of
[
KV
, Theorem 2.1.6(a)]
.

Theorem 4.4.9
.

Let
T
and
\theta
be as in Theorem
4.4.4
.

(a) Then the function
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}
is stable (see Section
4.1.8
). In other words, for every pair of stably conjugate elements
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
, we have an equality

f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}(\gamma^{\prime}).

(b) More generally, for every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, the function
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
is
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
-stable
(see Sections
4.1.5
(a) and
4.1.7
(d)).

Finally, we prove a generalization of Theorem
4.4.9
(a) which takes into an account inner twistings.

4.4.10
.

Inner twistings and stable equivalence.

(a) Let
\phi:G\overset{\thicksim}{\to}G^{\prime}
be an inner twisting (compare
[
KV
, Section 1.1.1]
).
Using theorem of Steinberg, replacing
\phi
by its conjugate
\phi\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g}
with
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{sep}})
, we can (and will) assume that

\phi
is an isomorphism over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
such that
\phi^{-1}\circ{}^{\sigma}\phi
is an inner automorphism of
G
.

(b) For every
g\in G
, we denote by
G^{\operatorname{ad}}_{g}\subseteq G^{\operatorname{ad}}
the stabilizer of
g
. Generalizing Section
4.1.2
(a),(b), we call a pair of elements
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\prime\operatorname{rss}}(F)

stably conjugate
, if there exists an element
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
\phi(g\gamma g^{-1})=\gamma^{\prime}
and
an element
h:=\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}\circ(\phi^{-1}\circ{}^{\sigma}\phi)\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{{}^{\sigma}g}\in G^{\operatorname{ad}}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
belongs to
(G_{\gamma}^{\operatorname{ad}})^{0}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
.

(c) Generalizing Section
4.1.2
(c), two embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
and
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
of maximal tori defined over
F
are called
stably conjugate
, if there exists an element
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=\phi\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g}\circ\mathfrak{a}
and the induced morphism

\phi\circ\operatorname{Ad}_{g}:\mathfrak{a}(T)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}(T)
is defined over
F
. Note that for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
of a maximal elliptic torus over
F
there exists a stably conjugate embedding
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
(see
[
KV
, Lemma 1.5.3]
).

(d) We say that invariant functions
f
on
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
f^{\prime}
on
G^{\prime\operatorname{rss}}(F)
are
stably equivalent
,
if for every pair
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\prime\operatorname{rss}}(F)
of stably conjugate elements, we have an equality
f(\gamma)=f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
.
In particular, this implies that
f
and
f^{\prime}
are stable.

Now we are ready to state a particular case of the equal characteristic version of
[
KV
, Theorem 2.1.6(b)]
, generalizing Theorem
4.4.9
(a).

Theorem 4.4.11
.

Let
\phi:G\overset{\thicksim}{\to}G^{\prime}
be an inner twisting, let
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
and
\theta
be as in Theorem
4.4.4
, let
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
be an embedding stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}
, let
T^{\prime}\subseteq G^{\prime}
be the image of
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
, and let
\theta^{\prime}:T^{\prime}(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
be the composition

\theta\circ\mathfrak{a}^{\prime-1}_{0}:T^{\prime}(F)\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}.

Then the invariant functions
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}
and
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}
are stably equivalent.

4.4.12
.

Remark.
It should not be difficult to prove the equal characteristic version of the general case of
[
KV
, Theorem 2.1.6(b)]
, thus providing a generalization of Theorem
4.4.9
(b), which takes into an account inner twistings.

5.
Completion of proofs

5.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.4

5.1.1
.

First, we assume that the assertion Theorem
4.4.4
(a) holds, and are going to show the right equality of formula (
4.4
).

(a) Using Lemma
2.2.10
and isomorphism
\Lambda_{G}/\Lambda_{0}\simeq\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}

we conclude that

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}}\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\Lambda}^{\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}).

Thus, by Lemma
A.4.7
, we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\Lambda_{0}})=\sum_{\mu}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu,H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})),

where
\mu
runs over a set of representatives in
\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}
of classes in
\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}/\Lambda
such that
\mu^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\mu\in\Lambda\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
. Moreover, and each summand on the RHS
only depends on the class
\mu\Lambda\in\Lambda_{G/Z_{0}}/\Lambda
.

(b) By Sections
4.3.12
(b),(c), the sum in part (a) can be written as

\sum_{\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})),

where
\mathfrak{a}
runs over a set of representatives of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings

T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.

5.1.2
.

Next, we are going to write an explicit formula for
f_{T,\theta}
.

(a) Let
P\subseteq G(F)
be an open subset and let
\Theta:G(F)/P\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
be a function.
We say that the sum
\sum_{g\in G(F)/P}\Theta(g)

uniformly stabilizes
, if for every open compact subgroup
Q\subseteq G(F)

the set

C_{Q}:=\{QxP\in Q\backslash G(F)/P\,|\,\sum_{g\in QxP/P}\Theta(g)\neq 0\}

is finite. Notice that the set
QxP/P\simeq Q/(Q\cap xPx^{-1})
is finite.

In this case, the sum
\sum_{QxP\in C_{Q}}\sum_{g\in QxP/P}\Theta(g)
is independent of
Q
and we denote the resulting sum by

\sum_{g\in G(F)/P}\Theta(g)
.

(b) For every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
, we denote by
t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
the smooth function on
G(F)
such that
t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g)=\operatorname{Tr}R^{\theta}_{\mathfrak{a}}(g)
if
g\in\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
, and

t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g)=0
, if
g\notin\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
. Since representation
R_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}^{\overline{\theta}}
is a cuspidal representation of
M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, it follows from the formula of the induced representation that we have an equality

f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(\gamma)=\sum_{g\in G(F)/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g),

where the sum of the right hand side stabilizes uniformly.

5.1.3
.

Our next goal is to rewrite
f_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
as a sum of terms, parameterized by a set of representatives of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, which are conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. Namely, we claim that

(i) for every
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, the sum

\sum_{g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g)
, where
G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)\cap G_{\mathfrak{a}}

stabilizes uniformly. Moreover, the resulting sum only depends of the
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy class of
\mathfrak{a}
;

(ii) we have an equality

f_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=\sum_{\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G}\sum_{g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g),

where
\mathfrak{a}
runs over a set of representatives of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.

Proof.

(a) Choose
h\in G(F)
such that
\mathfrak{a}=h\mathfrak{a}_{0}h^{-1}
, then for every
g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}
we have an equality

t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g)=t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(h^{-1}g^{-1}\gamma gh)
. Moreover, the correspondence
g\mapsto gh

induces a bijection

G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}=G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}=G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)h\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}h^{-1}/h\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}h^{-1}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)h\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}.

(b) By part (a) the sum in (i) can be rewritten as

\sum_{g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)h\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g),

therefore it conversely uniformly, because the sum
\sum_{g\in G(F)/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g)
does.
Next, the second assertion in (i) follows immediately from the first one.

(c) Note that for every
h\in G(F)
we have an equality
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)h\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}=G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)hT(F)
(see
[
KV
]
), and
the map
h\mapsto h\mathfrak{a}h^{-1}
defines a bijection between the double quotient
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)\backslash G(F)/T(F)
and the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, which are
G(F)
-conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.
Therefore assertion (ii) follows from part (b).
∎

5.1.4
.

Reduction steps.

(a) By Lemma
2.2.10
and Sections
5.1.1
and
5.1.3
, it remains to show that for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
, which is conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, the module
H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi}
-locally finite, and we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))=\sum_{g\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g).

Moreover, we can replace
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
by
\mathfrak{a}
, thus assuming that
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}_{0}
.

(b) By definition (see Corollary
4.3.7
and Section
4.4.3
(a)), there exists an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}_{0}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
such that
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}=\varphi
and
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}=\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
. Thus it remains to show that
for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}_{0}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
,

(i) the module
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})
is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
-locally finite, and

(ii) we have an equality

(5.1)

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))=\sum_{[g]\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g).

(c) It suffices to show the assertions of part (b) for some Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}_{0}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
.
Indeed, let
\mathbf{I}\supseteq\mathbf{T}
is another such Iwahori subgroup. Since
\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0},\mathbf{I}}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}=\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}
(see Section
2.1.5
(b)) and
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}}=\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0},\mathbf{I}}^{-1}\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0},\mathbf{I}}
(see (
4.3
)), it follows from Proposition
2.2.7
that
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0},\mathbf{I}}
induces an isomorphism
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}})
and interchanges the action of
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})
with the action of
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}})
. Therefore both assertions in part (b) for
\mathbf{I}
follow from that for
\mathbf{I}_{0}
.

(d) By part (c), we can assume that
\mathbf{I}_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
. Let
J=J_{\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}\subseteq\widetilde{S}
be the type of
\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
.
Then
J
is
\sigma
-invariant (see Section
4.2.3
), and
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
is contained in
W_{J}\sigma
(see Section
4.3.5
).

5.1.5
.

In this subsection we are going to show the assertion (i) of Section
5.1.4
(b).

(a) Let
S_{\gamma}\subseteq G^{0}_{\gamma}
be the maximal subtorus which splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
, and let
\mathbf{Q}\subseteq LG
be a
\sigma
-stable parahoric subgroup containing
L^{+}(S_{\gamma})
(see Section
4.2.3
(c)). Choose an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}\subseteq\mathbf{Q}
, and let
J^{\prime}=J_{\mathbf{Q}}
be the type of
\mathbf{Q}
, thus
\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}
.

(b) Choose
m
sufficiently large to satisfy Theorem
3.3.15
, and equip
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}
with a filtration

\{\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)}\}_{n}
, defined by
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)}:=\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{\leq n;J_{r};J^{\prime}_{l};m_{reg}}
(see Section
3.3.5
(f)). Then each
\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)}
is
\sigma
-invariant (by Section
4.2.4
(d)), hence

\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})\}_{n}
is a
\sigma
-invariant filtration of

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))
(see Section
3.3.7
(b)).

(c) Since
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\in W_{J}\sigma
(see Section
5.1.4
(d)), it follows from Lemma
3.3.11
that the filtration
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})\}_{n}
is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
-invariant. Since each
H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})
is finite-dimensional, we conclude that the module
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))
is
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
-locally finite. This completes the proof of assertion (i) of Section
5.1.4
(b).

In the next two subsections we will make the left hand side of (
5.1
) more explicit.

5.1.6
.

(a) We equip
\Lambda
with a length filtration
\Lambda^{\leq n}
(see Section
3.2.8
), which is finitely generated (by Corollary
3.2.9
),
\sigma
-invariant (see Section
4.2.2
(b)) and
\overline{W}
-invariant (see Section
3.2.2
(d)). Thus it is
\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
-invariant, thus
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
-invariant.

(b) By Section
5.1.5
(a),
\gamma
is in good position with
\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{P}_{J^{\prime},\mathbf{I}}
. Therefore, by Section
5.1.5
(c),
\{H^{\prime}_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma}^{(\leq n)},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}}})\}_{n}
is a
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
-invariant filtration of

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))
, which is finitely generated over
\{\Lambda^{\leq n}\}_{n}
(by Corollary
3.3.14
).

(c) Using the definition of the generalized trace and Theorem
3.3.15
, the left hand side of (
5.1
) therefore equals
\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))
for a sufficiently large
n
. Since
H_{i}
is a dual of
H^{i}
, the left hand side of (
5.1
) is therefore equal to
\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}},H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))
.

5.1.7
.

(a) Since
{}^{\sigma}\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}=\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}
(compare Sections
1.3.2
(c) and
1.3.3
), we have a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Fr}_{q}^{*}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}\simeq\sigma_{*}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{\theta,{}^{\sigma}\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}=\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}.

(b) Set
\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J}:=\pi_{J}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)})\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{J}
, where
\pi_{J}:\operatorname{Fl}\to\operatorname{Fl}_{J}
is the projection from Section
2.2.1
(c). Then
\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}:=\pi^{-1}_{J}(\operatorname{Fl}_{J}^{(\leq n)})
(see Section
3.3.5
(d)), the endomorphism
\widetilde{u}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}
of

H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})=H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))

decomposes as a composition of

\operatorname{Fr}_{q}^{*}:H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))\to H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})),

induced by the isomorphism of part (a), and the isomorphism

H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}_{I}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}}))\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H^{*}(\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma},(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})),

induced by the isomorphism

\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}:(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}_{I}^{-1}\circ\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})
(see
Sections
1.3.2
(d) and
1.2.1
(d)).

(c) Now it follows from Section
5.1.6
(c) and the Grothendieck’s Lefschetz trace formula,
that the left hand side of (
5.1
) equals the sum

\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\circ\operatorname{Fr}_{x}^{*},[(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})]_{x}).

5.1.8
.

Reformulation of assertion (ii) of Section
5.1.4
(b).

(a) Consider a parahoric subgroup
\mathbf{Q}^{\operatorname{sc}}:=\mathbf{Q}\cap L(G^{\operatorname{sc}})
of
L(G^{\operatorname{sc}})
and an open compact subgroup
Q^{\operatorname{sc}}:=\mathbf{Q}^{\operatorname{sc}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
.

(b) Notice that we have a natural identification
\operatorname{Fl}_{J}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\simeq G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
.
Moreover, since
\{\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}\}_{n}
is a
\mathbf{Q}^{\operatorname{sc}}
-invariant filtration of
\operatorname{Fl}
, we get that

\{\operatorname{Fl}_{J}^{(\leq n)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\}_{n}
is a
Q^{\operatorname{sc}}
-invariant filtration of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
, which we
denote by
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)^{(\leq n)}/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
. Furthermore, a class
x\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
corresponds
to a point of
\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J,\gamma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}^{(\leq n)}_{J}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
if an only if
x^{-1}\gamma x\in G_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
.

(c) By definition, for each sufficiently large
n
, the right hand side of (
5.1
) equals the sum

\sum_{x\in G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)^{(\leq n)}/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(x^{-1}\gamma x)
.
By definition, we have
t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(x^{-1}\gamma x)=0
, if
x^{-1}\gamma x\notin G_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
. Likewise, we have

t_{\mathfrak{a}_{0},\theta}(x^{-1}\gamma x)=\operatorname{Tr}R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}}(\overline{x^{-1}\gamma x})
, if
x^{-1}\gamma x\in G_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
, where

\overline{x^{-1}\gamma x}\in M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is the class of
x^{-1}\gamma x
.

(d) Using observations of parts (b), (c) and Section
5.1.7
(c), it remains to show that for every

x\in\operatorname{Fl}_{J,\gamma}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\subseteq G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)/G^{\operatorname{sc}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
, we have an equality

(5.2)

\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\circ\operatorname{Fr}_{x}^{*},[(\pi_{J})_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}})]_{x})=\operatorname{Tr}R^{\theta}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}}(\overline{x^{-1}\gamma x}).

5.1.9
.

Completion of the proof.

(a) Recall that an Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}
corresponds to the Borel subgroup
\overline{B}:=\mathbf{I}_{0}/\mathbf{P}_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}^{+}
of
M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
, containing the maximal torus
\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}(\overline{T})\subseteq M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}
.
Next, the abstract Cartan subgroup
T_{M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}
is canonically identified with
\overline{T}_{G}
, and under this identification
the admissible isomorphism
\varphi_{T,\mathbf{I}_{0}}:\overline{T}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{T}_{G}
corresponds to the admissible isomorphism

\varphi_{\overline{B}}:\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}(\overline{T})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}T_{M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}
. Moreover, we have a canonical isomorphism
W_{J}\simeq W_{M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}}}
, under which element
\widetilde{w}_{\mathbf{I}_{0}}\in W_{J}
corresponds to
w_{\overline{B}}=w_{\varphi_{\overline{B}}}\in W_{J}
(see Section
2.1.6
and
Section
4.3.5
).

(b) Since the commutative square (
2.4
) is
Cartesian, it follows by proper base change that the left hand side of (
5.2
) equals

\operatorname{Tr}(w_{\varphi_{\overline{B}}}\circ\sigma,H^{*}(\overline{\operatorname{Fl}}_{M_{\mathfrak{a}_{0}},\overline{x^{-1}\gamma x}},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{\theta},\varphi_{\overline{B}}})
. Therefore, by Lusztig’s theorem (Theorem
1.3.4
) it is equal to
R^{\theta}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{0}}(\overline{x^{-1}\gamma x})
. This completes the proof of equality (
5.2
), from which
equality (
5.1
) and hence Theorem
4.4.4
follows.

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.8

5.2.1
.

Observations.

(a) Note that for every
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\overline{W}]
-module
V
, the idempotent

\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}w\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\overline{W}]
defines a projection

V\to V^{\overline{W}}
. Therefore the functor of
\overline{W}
-invariants is exact on

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-vector spaces and the natural composition
V^{\overline{W}}\to V\to V_{\overline{W}}
is an isomorphism.

(b) Using part (a), for every finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module
V
, we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,V^{\overline{W}})=\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{w\in\overline{W}}\operatorname{Tr}(w\sigma,V)
.

(c) Let
V
be a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module, which is finitely generated as
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}]
-module. Then each
homology group
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V)
is a finite-dimensional
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle]
-module. Then it follows from parts (a) and (b) that
we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},V))=\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V)^{\overline{W}})=\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{w}\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V)).

Now we are ready to proof Theorem
4.4.8
. For convenience, we divide the proof into five steps:

Step 1.
By Section
5.2.1
, for every
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
we have an equality

(5.3)

\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma,H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})))=\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{w}\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))).

Step 2.
The decomposition
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}\simeq\bigoplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}
(see Section
4.4.6
) induces decompositions
\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}\simeq\bigoplus_{\varphi}\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}
(see Section
4.4.2
) and

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta})\simeq\bigoplus_{\varphi}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
. Moreover,
\sigma
sends
summand
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})\subseteq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta})
to
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,{}^{\sigma}\varphi})
, while each
w\in\widetilde{W}_{G}
sends
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
to
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,w\circ\varphi})
(see Proposition
2.2.7
).

In particular,
\Lambda_{G}
stabilizes each
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
, so we have a decomposition

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}^{\operatorname{st}}_{\theta}))\simeq\bigoplus_{\varphi}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

Furthermore, under this decomposition
\sigma
sends summand
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))
to
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,{}^{\sigma}\varphi}))
, while each
\overline{w}\in\overline{W}
sends
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))
to
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\overline{w}\circ\varphi})
.

Since
{}^{\sigma}\varphi=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}\circ\varphi
(see Section
4.3.1
(b)), the right hand side of
(
5.3
) can be therefore rewritten in the form

(5.4)

\frac{1}{|\overline{W}|}\sum_{\varphi}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\circ\sigma,H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi}))).

Step 3.

As in Notation
4.3.9
, we set
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}=\overline{w}_{T,\varphi}\circ\sigma\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
.
We claim that the trace
\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})))
is independent
of
\varphi
, hence the expression of (
5.4
) equals

\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})))
for each admissible

\varphi
.

Indeed, for each pair of admissible isomorphisms
\varphi
and
\varphi^{\prime}
, we have an equality
\varphi^{\prime}=\overline{w}\circ\varphi
for some

\overline{w}\in\overline{W}
(see
Section
2.1.5
(b)). Then we have the equality
u_{T,\varphi^{\prime}}=\overline{w}^{-1}\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\overline{w}\in\overline{W}\rtimes\langle\sigma\rangle
.
Since
\overline{w}\in\overline{W}
induces an isomorphism

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi}))\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi^{\prime}}))
(by Proposition
2.2.7
), the independence of
\varphi
follows.

Step 4.
By Step 3, the assertion of Theorem
4.4.8
(a) follows from Theorem
4.4.8
(b) with
\kappa=1
. So it remains to show
Theorem
4.4.8
(b).

As it was explained in Section
4.4.7
(c), every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
gives rise to character
\kappa:\Lambda_{G}\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
,
and we denote by the same letter its restriction to
\Lambda\rtimes\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
.

Step 5.
Using Lemma
2.2.10
(b), we have a natural isomorphism of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-modules

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\Lambda}^{\Lambda_{G}}(H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}),

hence a natural isomorphism of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\langle\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle]
-modules

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})\simeq H_{j}(\Lambda,H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}).

Thus it remains to show that the equality

f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda,H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{L}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})).

Step 6.

Since
T\subseteq G
is a maximal elliptic torus, the induced endomorphism
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}|_{\Lambda}\in\operatorname{Aut}(\Lambda)
is elliptic (see Sections
1.3.1
(d) and
A.4.3
(a)).
Hence by Proposition
A.4.6
(b), for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda,H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}))=\sum_{\widetilde{u}}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}),

where
\widetilde{u}
runs over a set of representatives of the set of
\Lambda
-conjugacy classes in

\Lambda\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\subseteq\Lambda\rtimes\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
.

Therefore we get an equality

(5.5)

\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{*}(\Lambda,H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}))=\sum_{\widetilde{u}}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}),

where the summation as in the previous formula.

Step 7.

By Section
4.3.12
(a), the map

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\mapsto[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}]
is a bijection
between the set of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
,
which are stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}:T\hookrightarrow G
, and
\Lambda
-conjugacy classes in

\Lambda\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\subseteq\Lambda\rtimes\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
.

In other words, the right hand side of (
5.5
) can be
written in the form

(5.6)

\sum_{\mathfrak{a}}(\sum_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})),

where

\bullet

\mathfrak{a}
runs over a set of representatives of
G(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
T\hookrightarrow G

which are stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
;

\bullet

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
runs over a set of representatives of
G^{\operatorname{sc}}(F)
-conjugacy classes of embeddings
T\hookrightarrow G

which are
G(F)
-conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}
.

Step 8.

By construction (see Section
4.4.3
(f)) for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
, which is stably conjugate to
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, we have

\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}=\lambda_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}
with
\lambda_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}\in\Lambda
. Then by definition (see Section
4.4.7
(d)),
we have an
equality

(5.7)

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})=\langle\kappa,\lambda_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}\rangle\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{gen}}(\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi},H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})).

Moreover, we have equalities

(5.8)

\langle\kappa,\lambda_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}\rangle=\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}],[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}])\rangle=\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\rangle,

the first of which follows from the fact that
\operatorname{inv}([\widetilde{u}_{T,\varphi}],[\widetilde{u}_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},\varphi}])\in\Lambda_{\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}}

(see Section
4.3.10
(c)) is the projection of
\lambda_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}
, and the second one follows from Lemma
4.3.11
(b).

Combining equalities (
5.7
) and (
5.8
) with Theorem
4.4.4
we see that the expression (
5.6
) is equal
to
\sum_{\mathfrak{a}}\langle\kappa,\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{0},\mathfrak{a})\rangle f_{T_{\mathfrak{a}},\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}}(\gamma)=f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
. This completes the proof of the theorem.

5.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.9

We start the proof with the following observations:

5.3.1
.

The canonical maps of Section
2.3.3
(c) revisited.

(a) Recall that for every torus
S
over
K
, the group
algebra
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)]
is naturally isomorphic to the algebra of
regular functions
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{S}]
, where
\widehat{S}
is the dual
torus of
S
defined over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
. Therefore we have natural isomorphisms

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)]_{\Gamma_{K}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{S}]_{\Gamma_{K}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{K}}].

(b) By part (a) and Section
2.3.1
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LS)]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(S)_{\Gamma_{K}}]\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{S}^{\Gamma_{K}}].

(c) The dual torus
\widehat{T_{G}}
is canonically isomorphic the
abstract Cartan
T_{\widehat{G}}
of the dual group
\widehat{G}
. Therefore we have canonical isomorphisms of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebras

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(T_{G})]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{T_{G}}]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[T_{\widehat{G}}],

which induces an isomorphism

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[X_{*}(T_{G})]^{W_{G}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[T_{\widehat{G}}]^{W_{\widehat{G}}}\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[T_{\widehat{G}}/W_{\widehat{G}}]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[c_{\widehat{G}}],

where the first isomorphism is from (
2.1
), and
c_{\widehat{G}}
is the Chevalley space of
\widehat{G}
.

(d) By the identification of the parts (b) and (c), for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(K)
, the canonical morphism
\operatorname{pr}_{\gamma}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
defined
in Section
2.3.3
(c) can be viewed as a morphism
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[c_{\widehat{G}}]\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}]
, hence comes from a morphism of affine algebraic varieties

(5.9)

\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}\to c_{\widehat{G}}

Furthermore, it is easy to see that the morphism (
5.9
) is noting but the restriction
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}|_{\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}}
, where

\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\to c_{\widehat{G}}
was defined in Section
4.1.9
(a).

(e) By part (d), morphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
from Section
2.3.3
(c) comes from a morphism
\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma}|_{\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}}
, where

\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}\circ\iota
and
\iota:\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}\to\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}
is the inverse map
g\mapsto g^{-1}
.

5.3.2
.

Correspondence between representations and sheaves.

(a) For every finitely generated commutative
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebra
A
,
there is a natural equivalence of categories
M\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{M}
between
A
-modules and (coherent) quasi-coherent
sheaves on
\operatorname{Spec}A
. Moreover,
M
is finitely-generated if and only if
\mathcal{F}_{M}
is coherent.

(b) By part (a), for every finitely-generated abelian group
\Theta
,
there is a natural equivalence of categories between
representations of
\Theta
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and quasi-coherent sheaves on
\operatorname{Spec}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Theta]
.

(c) Let
f:A\to B
be a homomorphism finitely-generated
commutative
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
-algebras, and let
\operatorname{Spec}f
be the
corresponding morphism
\operatorname{Spec}B\to\operatorname{Spec}A
. Then the restriction
functor
f^{*}:\operatorname{Mod}(B)\to\operatorname{Mod}(A)
corresponds via the
correspondence of part (a) to the push-forward of quasi-coherent
sheaves.

(d) Note that the multiplication map
m:A\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}A\to A
is an algebra homomorphism, and
the restriction functor
m^{*}:\operatorname{Mod}(A)\to\operatorname{Mod}(A\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}A)
corresponds via the correspondence of part (a)
to the push-forward
\Delta_{*}
of quasi-coherent sheaves, where
\Delta:\operatorname{Spec}(A)\to\operatorname{Spec}(A)\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
is the diagonal map.

(e) Using Sections
5.3.1
(b),(c), the correspondence of parts (a),(b) gives an
equivalence between

\bullet
representations of
\pi_{0}(L{G}_{\gamma})
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and quasi-coherent
sheaves on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
;

\bullet
representations of
\Lambda_{G}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
and quasi-coherent sheaves on

T_{\widehat{G}}
;

\bullet

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}
-modules and quasi-coherent sheaves
on
c_{\widehat{G}}
.

5.3.3
.

Observations.

(a) Using Proposition
2.2.7
(d) one shows that for every local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, the natural (left) action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
induces an action on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, commuting with the
\Lambda_{G}
-action. Moreover, the action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
factors though the action of

\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
(compare Section
3.3.1
(c)).

(b) As in Section
4.4.7
(d), for every character
\kappa:\Lambda_{G}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
we can form a representation
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}
of
\Lambda_{G}
. Moreover, taking tensor product of the action of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
from part (a) and the trivial action on
(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}
, we gen an action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}
, commuting with the
\Lambda_{G}
-action. In particular, we have an action of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
on each
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})
.

(c) Recall that
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
a finitely-generated

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module (see Proposition
3.3.2
, Lemma
3.1.1
and the observation of Section
3.3.1
(a)).
Therefore each
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})
is finite-dimensional over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

(d) By parts (b),(c) and the correspondence of Section
5.3.2
,
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})

corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{A}
on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
.

(e) Using isomorphism of Section
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\simeq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[T_{\widehat{G}}]
, character
\kappa:\Lambda_{G}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
gives rise to a point

\kappa\in T_{\widehat{G}}
, hence to a point
\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa)\in c_{\widehat{G}}
.

The following claim is crucial for what follows.

Claim 5.3.4
.

The coherent sheaf
\mathcal{A}
on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
defined in Section
5.3.3
(d)
is supported on a finite set
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
.

Proof.

We present the argument in steps.

Step 1.
Let
\{F^{r}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{r}
be the filtration of

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
from Theorem
2.3.4
. Then each graded
piece
V:=\operatorname{gr}^{r}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is a representation of the product

\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\times\Lambda_{G}
, hence the vector space
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V_{\kappa})

is a representation
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
. Thus, by the correspondence of Section
5.3.2
,
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V_{\kappa})

corresponds to a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{W}
on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
, and it suffices to show that
\mathcal{W}
is
supported on
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))
.

Step 2.
Recall that
V
is a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\times\Lambda_{G}]
-module, that is, a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module.
In particular,
V
has a natural structure of a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module, hence a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module
(see Section
5.3.2
(d)). Moreover, it follows from Theorem
2.3.4
that the action of a subalgebra

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
on
V
is induced from the action of

\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
via homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\otimes\operatorname{Id}
(see Section
5.3.1
(e)).

Step 3.
Now we apply the correspondence Section
5.3.2
. Namely,

\bullet

V
viewed as a
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module corresponds to a quasi-coherent sheaf

\mathcal{V}
on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}\times T_{\widehat{G}}
;

\bullet

V
viewed as
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module corresponds to a quasi-coherent sheaf

\mathcal{U}:=(\operatorname{pr}_{2})_{*}(\mathcal{V})
on
T_{\widehat{G}}
;

\bullet

V
viewed as
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module corresponds to a quasi-coherent sheaf

\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{U})
on
T_{\widehat{G}}\times T_{\widehat{G}}
;

\bullet
the compatibility assertion, mentioned above, implies that we have an isomorphism

(5.10)

(\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma}\times\operatorname{Id})_{*}(\mathcal{V})\simeq(\nu_{\widehat{G}}\times\operatorname{Id})_{*}\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{U})

of quasi-coherent sheaves on
c_{\widehat{G}}\times T_{\widehat{G}}
.

Step 4.
For every
t\in T_{\widehat{G}}
we denote by
m_{t}:T_{\widehat{G}}\to T_{\widehat{G}}
the multiplication map by
t
and by

i_{t}:\operatorname{pt}\to T_{\widehat{G}}
the embedding of a closed point with image
t\in T
.

Then the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]
-module
V_{\kappa}

corresponds to the quasi-coherent sheaf
(\operatorname{Id}\times m_{\kappa})_{*}(\mathcal{V})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\times m_{\kappa^{-1}})^{*}(\mathcal{V})
, hence the
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
-module

H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},V_{\kappa})
corresponds to the derived pullback

\mathcal{W}:=R^{j}(\operatorname{Id}\times i_{1})^{*}(\operatorname{Id}\times m_{\kappa^{-1}})^{*}(\mathcal{V})\simeq R^{j}(\operatorname{Id}\times i_{\kappa^{-1}})^{*}(\mathcal{V}),

and we claim that
\mathcal{W}
is supported at
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))=\operatorname{can}^{\prime-1}_{\gamma}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1}))
.
It suffices to show that the pushforward
(\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma})_{*}(\mathcal{W})
is supported at a closed point
\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1})
.

Step 5.
Using base change and equation (
5.10
), we have an isomorphism

(5.11)

(\operatorname{can}^{\prime}_{\gamma})_{*}(\mathcal{W})\simeq R^{j}(\operatorname{Id}\times i_{\kappa^{-1}})^{*}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}\times\operatorname{Id})_{*}\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{U}),

so it remains to show that the RHS of (
5.11
) is supported at
\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1})
.

But this is easy: the pushforward
(\nu_{\widehat{G}}\times\operatorname{Id})_{*}\Delta_{*}(\mathcal{U})
is supported at the graph
\Gamma_{\nu_{\widehat{G}}}\subseteq c_{\widehat{G}}\times T_{\widehat{G}}
, so the RHS of (
5.11
) is supported at

\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\Gamma_{\nu_{\widehat{G}}}\cap\left(c_{\widehat{G}}\times\{\kappa^{-1}\}\right))=\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\{(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1}),\kappa^{-1})\})=\{\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa^{-1})\}.

∎

5.3.5
.

Observations.
Consider semidirect product
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
given by the formula
\widetilde{u}\lambda\widetilde{u}^{-1}={}^{\sigma}\lambda
for every
\widetilde{u}\in\pi_{G}^{-1}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi})
and
\lambda\in\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
.

(a) Using Proposition
2.2.7
(d) and the commutative diagram of Section
4.2.5
(a), there exists a unique action of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})
, extending the action of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
from Section
2.3.3
(a) and the action of
\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
from Section
4.4.2
(f).

(b) Character
\kappa
from Section
4.4.7
(c) uniquely extends to a character of the semidirect product
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
trivial on
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
. Then, as in
Section
4.4.7
(d), we can form a representation
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}
of

\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\pi_{G}^{-1}(\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle)
, making
H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa})
becomes a representation of the semi-direct product
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
, where
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\cdot\lambda\cdot\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}={}^{\sigma}\lambda
for every
\lambda\in\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
.

(c) Let
\gamma^{\prime}
be a stable conjugate of
\gamma
, and
g\in G(K)
be such that

g\gamma g^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}
. Using Proposition
2.2.7
(d), we can tensor the isomorphism of
\Lambda_{G}
-representations

l_{g}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
with the identity map of
(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})_{\kappa}
, thus getting an isomorphism
l_{g}:H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}
of
\Lambda_{G}
-representations, hence an isomorphism

l_{g}:H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}).

Since
({}^{\sigma}g)\gamma({}^{\sigma}g)^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}
we similarly get an isomorphism
l_{{}^{\sigma}g}
. Moreover, arguing as in part (b), these isomorphisms are connected by the identity
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\circ l_{g}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}=l_{{}^{\sigma}g}
.

5.3.6
.

Construction.

(a) Consider reductive group
G_{1}:=G\times^{Z(G)}T
, that is,
G_{1}:=(G\times T)/Z(G)
, where
Z(G)
acts on
G\times T
by the formula

(g,t)z=(gz,z^{-1}t)
, and let
\pi:G\hookrightarrow G_{1}
be the isogeny
g\mapsto[g,1]
.
Then
T_{1}:=T\times^{Z(G)}T\subseteq G\times^{Z(G)}T=G^{\prime}
is a maximal elliptic torus. Clearly,
T_{1}
splits of
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
,
so
G_{1}
splits over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
.

(b) Note that
\pi
gives rise to an embedding
\pi:T\hookrightarrow T_{1}:t\mapsto[t,1]
, which has a left inverse
m:[a,b]\mapsto ab
.
Therefore the induced map
\pi_{*}:H^{1}(F,T)\to H^{1}(F,T_{1})
is injective. Similarly, the induced map
\widehat{\pi}:\widehat{T_{1}}\to\widehat{T}
has a right inverse
\widehat{m}:\widehat{T}\to\widehat{T_{1}}
both of which
are
\Gamma_{F}
-equivariant. In particular, every
\kappa\in\widehat{T}^{\Gamma_{F}}
gives rise to an element
\kappa_{1}:=\widehat{m}(\kappa)\in\widehat{T_{1}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, and we have
\widehat{\pi}(\kappa_{1})=\kappa
.

(c) We choose a tamely ramified character
\theta_{1}:T_{1}(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
, extending
\theta
. Then
\theta_{1}
is in general position,
since
\theta
is such. So
(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1})
satisfy the assumptions of Section
4.4.1
, so as in Section
4.4.5
(b) we can form a
\operatorname{Ad}G_{1}(F)
-invariant function
f^{\kappa_{1}}_{T_{1},\theta_{1}}
on
G_{1}^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
.

Proposition 5.3.7
.

In the situation of Section
5.3.6
, the pullback
\pi^{*}(f^{\kappa_{1}}_{T_{1},\theta_{1}})
to
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
equals
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
.

Proof.

To expose the structure of the proof we divide it into steps.

5.3.8
.

Every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
stably conjugate to the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
gives rise to the embedding
\mathfrak{a}_{1}:T_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}

stably conjugate to the inclusion, given by the formula
\mathfrak{a}_{1}([a,b])=(\mathfrak{a}(a),b)
. Furthermore,
the assignment
\mathfrak{a}\mapsto\mathfrak{a}_{1}
is a bijection, where the inverse map is the restriction.

Moreover, for two stably conjugate embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
with the corresponding embeddings

\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}:T_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}
the relative positions
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,T)
and
\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1})\in H^{1}(F,T_{1})

are connected by the identity

\pi_{*}(\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}))=\operatorname{inv}(\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}).

Since
\pi_{*}
is injective (by Section
5.3.6
(b)),
we thus conclude that two embeddings
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}:T\hookrightarrow G
are
G(F)
-conjugate if and only if embeddings
\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}_{1}:T_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}
are
G_{1}(F)
-conjugate.

5.3.9
.

By Section
5.3.8
it suffices to show that for every embedding
\mathfrak{a}:T\hookrightarrow G
stably conjugate to the inclusion
T\hookrightarrow G
with the corresponding embedding
\mathfrak{a}_{1}:T_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{1}
the pullback
\pi^{*}(f_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1}})
equals
f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
, that is, for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
we have an equality

f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(\gamma)=f_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1}}(\pi(\gamma)).

We will show this assertion in Section
5.3.12
, using an analogous assertion for Deligne–Lusztig representations over finite fields shown in
Lemma
5.3.10
and two identities shown in Lemma
5.3.11
.

Lemma 5.3.10
.

Let
\pi:H\to H_{1}
be a quasi-isogeny of connected reductive groups over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
,
S_{1}\subseteq H_{1}
a maximal torus over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and
\zeta_{1}:S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}^{\times}
a character.

Let
S:=\pi^{-1}(S_{1})\subseteq H
be the induced maximal torus of
H
,
and let
\zeta:=\zeta_{1}|_{S(\mathbb{F}_{q})}
be the pullback of
\zeta_{1}
. Then the Deligne–Lusztig virtual representation
R^{\zeta}_{S}
of
H(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is isomorphic to the pullback of
R^{\zeta_{1}}_{S_{1}}
.

Proof.

Note that (see
[
DM
, Definition 11.1]
) the virtual representation
R^{\zeta}_{S}
can be described as the image of character
\theta\in\operatorname{Rep}S(\mathbb{F}_{q})
under the Lusztig induction functor, given by the virtual
(H(\mathbb{F}_{q}),S(\mathbb{F}_{q}))
-bimodule
V:=H^{*}_{c}(\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, where
B=SU\supseteq S
is a Borel subgroup defined over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
,
\mathcal{L}_{H}:H\to H
is the Lang isogeny
h\mapsto h^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h
, and the action of

H(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times S(\mathbb{F}_{q})
on
V
is induced by the action
(h,s)(x)=hxs^{-1}
on
\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)\subseteq H
.

Similarly,
R^{\zeta_{1}}_{S_{1}}
is isomorphic to the image of
\theta_{1}
by the functor, given by the virtual
(H_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q}),S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q}))
-bimodule
V_{1}:=H^{*}_{c}(\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1}),\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
, so we have to show that
the
(H(\mathbb{F}_{q}),S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q}))
-bimodules
\operatorname{Ind}_{S(\mathbb{F}_{q})}^{S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(V)
and
\operatorname{Res}_{H_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}^{H(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(V_{1})
are isomorphic.

Note that quasi-isogeny
\pi:H\to H_{1}
induces an isomorphism
\pi|_{U}:U\overset{\thicksim}{\to}U_{1}
. Hence it induces an
(H(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times S(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-equivariant map
\phi:\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)\to\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})
, hence an
(H(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q}))
-equivariant map

\phi^{\prime}:\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)\times^{S(\mathbb{F}_{q})}{S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\to\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})
,
and it suffices to show that
\phi^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Moreover, since
\phi^{\prime}
is
S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
-equivariant, and
S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
acts freely on both sides, we can divide by the action of
S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, so it suffices to show that the induced map

\overline{\phi}:\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})/S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})

is an isomorphism. Since Lang isogenies
\mathcal{L}_{H}
and
\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}
are étale, and
\pi|_{U}
is an isomorphism, we conclude that the map
\phi
is étale. Hence
\overline{\phi}
is étale, so it suffices to check that it induces a bijection on
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-points.

To show the injectivity of
\overline{\phi}
, note that the map
\overline{\pi}:H/S\to H_{1}/S_{1}
, induced by
\pi
, is an isomorphism. It remains to show that the map
\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)/S(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to H/S
, induced by the inclusion
\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)\hookrightarrow H
, is injective. Explicitly, we have to show that

(5.12)

\text{if }h\in\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\text{ and }s\in S(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\text{ satisfy }hs\in\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U),\text{ then }s\in S(\mathbb{F}_{q}).

Since
h\in\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)
, we have

(5.13)

(hs)^{-1}{}^{\sigma}(hs)=s^{-1}(h^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h){}^{\sigma}s\in s^{-1}U{}^{\sigma}s=U(s^{-1}{}^{\sigma}s),

so our assumption
hs\in\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)
implies that
s^{-1}{}^{\sigma}s=1
, thus
s\in S(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

To show the surjectivity of
\overline{\phi}
, we can assume that
\pi
is the projection
H\to H^{\operatorname{ad}}
. Choose any
h_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
and
h\in\pi^{-1}(h_{1})\subseteq H(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
. Then we have
\pi(h^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h)=h_{1}^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h_{1}\in U_{1}
, thus
h^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h\in Uz
for some
z\in Z(H)(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})\subseteq S(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

By Lang’s theorem, there exists
s\in S(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q})
such that
s^{-1}{}^{\sigma}s=z^{-1}
. Then we have
h^{-1}{}^{\sigma}h\in U(s{}^{\sigma}(s^{-1}))
and arguing as in (
5.13
) we conclude that
(hs)^{-1}{}^{\sigma}(hs)\in U
, that is,
hs\in\mathcal{L}_{H}^{-1}(U)
. Then
\pi(hs)=h_{1}\pi(s)\in\mathcal{L}_{H_{1}}^{-1}(U_{1})
so we conclude from assertion (
5.12
) that
\pi(s)\in S_{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, thus
\overline{\phi}([hs])=[h_{1}\pi(s)]=[h_{1}]
, as claimed.
∎

Lemma 5.3.11
.

We claim that we have identities

G(F)\cdot(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}=G_{1}(F)
and
G(F)\cap(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}=\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Proof.

We have to show that the composition
c:(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\hookrightarrow G_{1}(F)\overset{\operatorname{pr}}{\longrightarrow}G_{1}(F)/G(F)
is surjective and its kernel is

\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Since torus
\mathfrak{a}(T)
is unramified, the torus
\mathfrak{a}(T)/Z(G)\simeq G_{1}/G
is unramied. Thus, as in
[
KV
, Lemma 1.8.17(b)]
, an exact sequence
1\to G\to G_{1}\to G_{1}/G\to 1
gives rise to an exact sequence

(5.14)

1\to G_{\mathfrak{a}}\to(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\to(G_{1}/G)(\mathcal{O})\to 1.

Since
\mathfrak{a}(T)\subseteq G
is elliptic, the torus
\mathfrak{a}(T)/Z(G)\simeq G_{1}/G
is unisotropic, thus

(G_{1}/G)(\mathcal{O})=(G_{1}/G)(F)
(see
[
KV
, proof of Lemma 2.2.6]
).
So the surjectivity of the projection
(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\to(G_{1}/G)(\mathcal{O})=(G_{1}/G)(F)
implies the surjectivity of the composition

(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\hookrightarrow G_{1}(F)\overset{\operatorname{pr}}{\longrightarrow}G_{1}(F)/G(F)
, hence the surjectivity of
c
.

Next let
Z_{0}\subseteq Z(G)^{0}
be the maximal split torus, and we claim that
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}=G_{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot Z_{0}(F)
. Indeed,
this follows from equalities
\widetilde{G_{\mathfrak{a}}}=G_{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot\mathfrak{a}(T)(F)
(see
[
KV
, Corollary 2.2.7(a)]
),
\mathfrak{a}(T)(F)=\mathfrak{a}(T)(\mathcal{O})\cdot Z_{0}(F)

(see
[
KV
, Lemma 2.2.6]
) and inclusion
\mathfrak{a}(T(\mathcal{O}))\subseteq G_{\mathfrak{a}}
(see Section
4.4.1
(a)). Moreover, since
\mathfrak{a}(T)\subseteq G
is an elliptic torus, we conclude that
Z_{0}\subseteq Z(G_{1})^{0}
is a maximal split torus as well, hence
(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}=(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}\cdot Z_{0}(F)
.

Write
g_{1}\in\operatorname{Ker}c\cap(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
in the form
g_{1}=gz
, where
g\in(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
and
z\in Z_{0}(F)
.
Then
z\in Z_{0}(F)\in G(F)\subseteq\operatorname{Ker}c
, so
g\in\operatorname{Ker}c\cap(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
. Then, by the exact sequence (
5.14
) we have

g\in G_{\mathfrak{a}}
, thus
g_{1}\in\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
∎

5.3.12
.

Now we are ready to finish the proof of Proposition
5.3.7
.
Recall (see Section
5.1.1
(b)) that for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
we have an equality

f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(\gamma)=\sum_{g\in G(F)/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}(g^{-1}\gamma g),

where the sum of the right hand side stabilizes uniformly, and similarly

f_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1}}(\gamma)=\sum_{g\in G_{1}(F)/(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}}t_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1}}(g^{-1}\gamma g).

On the other hand, it follows from equalities of Lemma
5.3.11
that the natural map
G(F)/\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}\to G_{1}(F)/(\widetilde{G_{1}})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
is a bijection, so (by Section
5.3.9
) it remains to show that function
t_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}:G(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
is the restriction of
t_{\mathfrak{a}_{1},\theta_{1}}:G_{1}(F)\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

Using the second equality of Lemma
5.3.11
it suffices to show that the restriction
R^{\theta_{1}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}|_{\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
is isomorphic to
R^{\theta}_{\mathfrak{a}}
. By definition, the restriction
R^{\theta_{1}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}|_{Z(G)(F)}
is
\theta_{1}|_{Z(G)(F)}=\theta|_{Z(G)(F)}
, while the restriction
R^{\theta_{1}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}|_{G_{\mathfrak{a}}}
is the pullback of the inflation of virtual representation
R^{\overline{\theta}_{1}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}}

of
M_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
with respect to the inclusion
G_{\mathfrak{a}}\hookrightarrow(G_{1})_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
. Alternatively,
R^{\theta_{1}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}|_{G_{\mathfrak{a}}}
is isomorphic to the inflation of the restriction
R^{\overline{\theta}_{1}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}}|_{M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}
, corresponding to the injective quasi-isogeny
M_{\mathfrak{a}}\hookrightarrow M_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}
. Since
R^{\overline{\theta}_{1}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}_{1}}|_{M_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\simeq R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}
by Lemma
5.3.10
, the restriction

R^{\theta_{1}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}}|_{G_{\mathfrak{a}}}
is thus isomorphic to the inflation of
R^{\overline{\theta}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{a}}}
, as claimed.

∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
4.4.9
.

5.3.13
.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.9
.

To expose the structure of the proof we divide it into steps.

Step 1.
It suffices to show that the restriction of
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
to
G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
of compact regular semisimple elements in
G(F)
is
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
-stable.

Proof.

Note that for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
z\in Z(G)(F)
, we have an equality
f_{T,\theta}(z\gamma)=\theta(z)\cdot f_{T,\theta}(\gamma)
, hence

(5.15)

f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(z\gamma)=\theta(z)\cdot f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}(\gamma).

Moreover, we have
G^{0}_{z\gamma}=G^{0}_{\gamma}
, and if
\gamma^{\prime}
is stably conjugate to
\gamma
, then
z\gamma^{\prime}
is stably conjugate to
z\gamma
and we have an equality
\operatorname{inv}(z\gamma^{\prime},z\gamma)=\operatorname{inv}(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma)
. Now the assertion follows from the fact that each
f_{\mathfrak{a},\theta}
is supported on
\widetilde{G}_{\mathfrak{a}}=G_{\mathfrak{a}}\cdot Z(G)(F)
.
∎

Step 2.
We can assume that
Z(G)
is connected.

Proof.

Consider the quasi-isogeny
\pi:G\hookrightarrow G_{1}
from Section
5.3.6
. Since
Z(G_{1})\simeq T
is connected, it suffices to show that the assertion for
G
follows from that for
G_{1}
. Let
T_{1},\theta_{1}
and
\kappa_{1}
be as in Section
5.3.6
.
Then
\widehat{\pi}(\mathcal{E}_{T_{1},\kappa_{1}})
is conjugate to
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
(see Section
4.1.12
(e)), and

\pi^{*}(f^{\kappa_{1}}_{T_{1},\theta_{1}})=f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
by Proposition
5.3.7
. Since
f^{\kappa_{1}}_{T_{1},\theta_{1}}
is
\mathcal{E}_{T_{1},\kappa_{1}}
-stable by assumption, function
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}
is
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
-stable by Lemma
4.1.14
.
∎

Step 3.
By Theorem
4.4.8
, the restriction
f_{T,\theta}^{\kappa}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}
can be written in the form
f^{\kappa}_{T,\theta}|_{G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}}=\sum_{i,j}(-1)^{i+j}f_{i,j}^{\kappa}
, where

f_{i,j}^{\kappa}
is the function

\gamma\mapsto\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{j}(\Lambda,H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\theta,\varphi})_{\kappa}).

Thus, by Step 1 it suffices to show that each function
f_{i,j}^{\kappa}
is
\mathcal{E}_{T,\kappa}
-stable.
Moreover, by Step 2 we can assume that Z(G) is connected. Hence, using Section
4.1.9
(b) it suffices to show that for every
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)_{c}
the restriction
f^{\kappa}_{i,j}|_{\operatorname{Orb}^{\operatorname{st}}_{G(F)}(\gamma)}
lies in the span of
\{\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\gamma}\}_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\kappa)\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}}
.

Step 4.
By Claim
5.3.4
, the coherent sheaf on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
corresponding to the
representation
V:=H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})
of
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
is supported on
a finite set
\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
.
Therefore vector space
V
decomposes as a direct sum
V=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\kappa)\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}}V_{\xi}
,
where the coherent sheaf on
\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
, corresponding to the
representation
V_{\xi}
, is supported at
\xi
.

Step 5.
As it was mentioned in Section
5.3.5
(b), vector space
V
is naturally a representation of the semi-direct product

\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\ltimes\langle\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\rangle
, where
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\cdot\lambda\cdot\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}={}^{\sigma}\lambda
for every
\lambda\in\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
. Combining this with observations of Section
4.2.1
(a)-(c) one gets that for every
\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\kappa)\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}
, we have an inclusion

\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}(V_{\xi})\subseteq(V_{{}^{\sigma}\xi})
. In particular, for every
\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\kappa)\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
, the subspace
V_{\xi}\subseteq V
is
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
-invariant.

Step 6.
We claim that we have an equality

f^{\kappa}_{i,j}|_{\operatorname{Orb}^{\operatorname{st}}_{G(F)}(\gamma)}=\sum_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}}\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi})\operatorname{ev}^{\xi}_{\gamma},

which by Step 3 implies our assertion. Explicitly, we have to show that for every stably conjugate
\gamma^{\prime}
of
\gamma
, we have an equality

(5.16)

\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})=\sum_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}}\langle\xi,\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\rangle\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi}).

Step 7.
Since
\gamma^{\prime}
is a stable conjugate of
\gamma
, there exists
g\in G(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
such that
g\gamma g^{-1}=\gamma^{\prime}

and
h:=g^{-1}{}^{\sigma}g\in G^{0}_{\gamma}(F^{\operatorname{nr}})
(see Section
4.1.2
(a),(b)).
As it was mentioned in Section
5.3.5
(c), we have natural isomorphisms

l_{g},l_{{}^{\sigma}g}:H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa})\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}),

which are related by the identity
\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\circ l_{g}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}^{-1}=l_{{}^{\sigma}g}
. Therefore we have

(l_{g})^{-1}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\circ l_{g}=l_{g^{-1}}\circ l_{{}^{\sigma}g}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}=l_{h}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},

hence the left hand side of equation (
5.16
) equals

\operatorname{Tr}((l_{g})^{-1}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}\circ l_{g},H_{j}(\Lambda_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})_{\kappa}))=\operatorname{Tr}(l_{h}\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V).

Furthermore, as in Section
2.3.3
(a), the last expression equals
\operatorname{Tr}([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V)
, where
[h]\in\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
is the class of
h\in LG^{0}_{\gamma}
.

To show equality (
5.16
), it suffices to show that

(5.17)

\operatorname{Tr}([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V)=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}}\operatorname{Tr}([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi})

and that for every
\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
we have

(5.18)

\operatorname{Tr}([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi})=\langle\xi,\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\rangle\cdot\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi}).

Step 8.
To show equality (
5.17
), notice that

\operatorname{Tr}\left([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},\bigoplus_{\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap(\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{K}}\smallsetminus\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}})}V_{\xi}\right)=0,

which follows from the fact that endomorphism
[h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi}
maps each
V_{\xi}
to
V_{{}^{\sigma}\xi}
(see Step 5), so it
permutes factors without fixed points.

Next notice that
\xi\in\operatorname{can}_{\gamma}^{-1}(\nu_{\widehat{G}}(\kappa))\cap\widehat{G^{0}_{\gamma}}^{\Gamma_{F}}
operator
[h]
acts on the semi-simplication
(V_{\xi})^{\operatorname{ss}}
of
V_{\xi}
by the scalar
\langle\xi,[h]\rangle
, where we identify
[h]
with its image under isomorphism
\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})\simeq X_{*}(G^{0}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{K}}
from Section
2.3.1
, and the pairing is as in Section
4.1.3
(c).
So we have an equality

\operatorname{Tr}([h]\circ\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi})=\langle\xi,[h]\rangle\cdot\operatorname{Tr}(\overline{u}_{T,\varphi},V_{\xi}).

Since
\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\in H^{1}(F,G^{0}_{\gamma})\simeq X_{*}(G^{0}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{F}}

is the class of
[h]\in X_{*}(G^{0}_{\gamma})_{\Gamma_{F}}
(see Section
4.1.1
(c)), we have an equality

\langle\xi,[h]\rangle=\langle\xi,\operatorname{inv}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\rangle
, from which equality (
5.18
) follows.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.11

5.4.1
.

Observations.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system on
\overline{T}_{G}
.

(a) Using Proposition
2.2.7
(d), the natural (left) action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
induces an action on
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, commuting with the
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-action.
In particular, we have an action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on each
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
.

(b) Moreover, applying Claim
5.3.4
with
\kappa=1
we conclude that the action of
LG^{0}_{\gamma}
on each

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is unipotent.

(c) By Section
2.2.12
, we have
the adjoint action of
LG^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma}=L(G^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma})
on each

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
.

Lemma 5.4.2
.

The adjoint action of
L((G^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma})^{0})
on

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
is unipotent.

Proof.

We will carry out the proof in three steps:

Step 1
. We can assume that
\mathcal{L}
is a constant
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
V
.

Proof.

By adjointness, a
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{L}
has a natural embedding

\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{ind}_{1}^{\overline{W}}(\mathcal{L})=\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\overline{w}^{*}(\mathcal{L}).

Thus the assertion for
\mathcal{L}
follows from that for
\operatorname{ind}_{1}^{\overline{W}}(\mathcal{L})
, so we can assume that
\mathcal{L}=\operatorname{ind}_{1}^{\overline{W}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
for some local
system
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
on
T_{G}
.

Next, the pullback
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}=\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(\mathcal{L})
only depends on the restriction of
\mathcal{L}
to the image
\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}
. Since the image

\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}\subseteq\overline{T}_{G}
is a
\overline{W}
-orbit, it is finite. Then

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}
is a constant local system with fiber
V^{\prime}
, hence
\mathcal{L}|_{\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}}
is a constant local system with fiber

V=\operatorname{ind}_{1}^{\overline{W}}(V^{\prime})
. Thus
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}\simeq\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}^{*}(V)
, and the assertion follows.
∎

Step 2
. We can assume that
G=G^{\operatorname{ad}}
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
2.2.10
, one sees that the projection
\pi:G\to G^{\operatorname{ad}}

induces a natural
LG^{\operatorname{ad}}_{\gamma}
-equivariant isomorphism

H_{j}(\widetilde{W},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{V}))\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{V}))\overset{\thicksim}{\to}H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G^{\operatorname{ad}}},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G^{\operatorname{ad}},\pi(\gamma)},\mathcal{F}_{V})).

Thus we can replace
(G,\gamma)
by
(G^{\operatorname{ad}},\pi(\gamma))
.
∎

Step 3
. Assume now that
G=G^{\operatorname{ad}}
. Then for every
g\in LG^{0}_{\gamma}
, the action of
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
decomposes as
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}=l_{g}\circ r_{g}
, where
l_{g}
is the left action (induced by the endomorphism
xI\mapsto gxI
of
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
),
while
r_{g}
is the right action

\operatorname{Fl}_{G}=LG/I\to LG/gIg^{-1}\simeq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}:xI\mapsto xIg^{-1}=xg^{-1}(gIg^{-1}).

In particular,
r_{g}
is the automorphism of
Fl_{G,\gamma}
induced by
\omega_{g}\in\Omega_{G}\subseteq\widetilde{W}_{G}
(compare Section
2.2.12
), thus
r_{g}
induces the trivial action on

H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
.

Therefore the action of
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}=l_{g}\circ r_{g}
on
H_{j}(\widetilde{W}_{G},H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}))
coincide with the action of
l_{g}
, hence is it unipotent by Section
5.4.1
(b).
∎

Now we are ready to show Theorem
4.4.11
.

5.4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.11
.

We have to show that for every stably conjugate elements
\gamma\in G^{\operatorname{rss}}(F)
and
\gamma^{\prime}\in G^{\prime\operatorname{rss}}(F)
we have an equality
f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T,\theta}(\gamma)=f^{\operatorname{st}}_{T^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}}(\gamma^{\prime})
. Arguing as in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
4.4.9
,
we reduce to the case when
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
are compact.

By Theorem
4.4.8
, it suffices to show that for every
i
and
j
, we have the equality

(5.19)
