is the topological Jordan decomposition.

Choose a reduced decomposition
w=s_{n}\ldots s_{1}
, and
set
w^{\prime}:=s_{n-1}\ldots s_{1}
. By Lemma
B.1.7
(d), we have
w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}\cap\widetilde{W}
, and by
assumption
w=s_{n}w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
. Therefore by the induction
hypothesis,
w_{*}\circ\eta_{*}=(s_{n})_{*}\circ(w^{\prime}_{*}\circ\eta_{*})
equals

(s_{n})_{*}\circ(\eta_{w^{\prime}})_{*}
, while by Lemma
B.2.5
(a), the
latter expression equals
(\eta_{s_{n}w^{\prime}})_{*}=(\eta_{w})_{*}
.

Let now
w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
be arbitrary. Write
w
in the form

w=uw^{\prime}
, where
u\in\Omega_{G}
and
w^{\prime}\in\widetilde{W}
. Then
w^{\prime}=u^{-1}w

belongs to
\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
(by Lemma
B.1.7
(a)), therefore the
composition
w_{*}\circ\eta_{*}=u_{*}\circ w^{\prime}_{*}\circ\eta_{*}
equals

u_{*}\circ(\eta_{w^{\prime}})_{*}
by the case proven above, hence to

(\eta_{uw^{\prime}})_{*}=(\eta_{w})_{*}
by Lemma
B.2.3
(c).

(b) We have to show that
\eta_{*}
commutes
with
(s_{\beta})_{*}
for every simple affine root
\beta\in\widetilde{\Delta}_{s}
.
By Lemma
B.1.7
(c), there exists
w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
such that

\alpha:=w(\beta)\in\widetilde{\Delta}
. Hence, by
Lemma
B.2.5
(b), we have an equality

(s_{\alpha})_{*}\circ(\eta_{w})_{*}=(\eta_{w})_{*}\circ(s_{\beta})_{*}
, which by (a)
implies that

(s_{\alpha})_{*}\circ(w_{*}\circ\eta_{*})=(w_{*}\circ\eta_{*})\circ(s_{\beta})_{*}
,
or, equivalently, that

(w^{-1}s_{\alpha}w)_{*}\circ\eta_{*}=\eta_{*}\circ(s_{\beta})_{*}
. Since

w^{-1}s_{\alpha}w=s_{w^{-1}(\alpha)}=s_{\beta}
, this implies that

(s_{\beta})_{*}\circ\eta_{*}=\eta_{*}\circ(s_{\beta})_{*}
, and the proof is complete.

(c) Since
\eta
is
LG_{\gamma}
-equivariant, both assertions follow from (b).
∎

Proposition B.2.8
.

Let
\gamma\in G(K)
be a bounded semisimple element, and we have either
s=1
or
\gamma=su
is the
topological Jordan decomposition of
\gamma
.
Then for every
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, there
is a natural isomorphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{s}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{\operatorname{sc}},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

of
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG_{\gamma})
-representations.

Proof.

By Corollary
B.2.7
(c), the map
\eta_{*}
is
(\widetilde{W}_{s}\times LG_{\gamma})
-equivariant, therefore by the Frobenius reciprocity it
induces a unique
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG_{\gamma})
-equivariant map

\widetilde{\eta}_{*}:\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{s}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{sc},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

It remains to show that
\widetilde{\eta}_{*}
is an isomorphism of vector
spaces.

Since
\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
is a set of representatives of the quotient

\widetilde{W}_{G}/\widetilde{W}_{s}
(by Lemma
B.1.7
(b)), we see that the underlining vector
space of
\operatorname{ind}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}_{\widetilde{W}_{s}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{\operatorname{sc}},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

decomposes as a direct sum
\bigoplus_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}}\{w\}\otimes H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{sc},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. Moreover, by the
\widetilde{W}_{G}
-equivariance of

\widetilde{\eta}_{*}
, we have
\widetilde{\eta}_{*}(\{w\}\otimes x)=w_{*}(\eta_{*}(x))
for each
w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}
and
x\in H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{sc},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. Therefore by Corollary
B.2.7
(a),
we get
\widetilde{\eta}_{*}(\{w\}\otimes x)=(\eta_{w})_{*}(x)
.

In other words, as the map of vector spaces
\widetilde{\eta}_{*}
can be identified with

(\bigsqcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}}\eta_{w})_{*}:H_{i}(\bigsqcup_{w\in\widetilde{W}^{s}_{G}}\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{sc},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\to H_{*}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}),

hence it is an isomorphism by Lemma
B.2.3
(b).
∎

Corollary B.2.9
.

Let
\gamma\in G(K)
be a bounded semisimple element with the topological Jordan decomposition
\gamma=su
.
Then for every
\overline{W}
-equivariant local system
\mathcal{L}
on
\overline{T}_{G}
, there
is a natural isomorphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{G^{0}_{s}}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

of
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG_{\gamma})
-representations.

Proof.

Applying Proposition
B.2.8
to the groups
G_{s}^{0}
and
G
, we conclude that

\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{s}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{\operatorname{sc}},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}).

∎

B.2.10
.

Proof of Lemma
2.2.10
.

Note that part (a) follows from Corollary
B.2.7
(b) for
s=1
, while part (c) follows from part (a) and Proposition
B.2.8
for
s=1
.
Next, the first assertion of part (b) follows from (a), while the second assertion follows from part (c). Namely,
the local system
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}:=\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}
is naturally
\overline{W}
-equivariant, and the
isomorphism

\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{\operatorname{sc}},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}})

of
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG_{\gamma})
-representations from part (c) restricts to an isomorphism

\operatorname{ind}_{\Lambda}^{\Lambda_{G}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{sc},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})

of
(\Lambda_{G}\times LG_{\gamma})
-representations.
∎

B.3.
Completion of the proof

Lemma B.3.1
.

Assume that
\gamma^{\prime}=z\gamma
and
z\in Z(G)(\mathcal{O})
. Then Theorem
2.3.4
for
\gamma
implies that for
\gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Denote by
m_{z}:\overline{T}_{G}\to\overline{T}_{G}
the morphism induced by the multiplication by
\overline{z}\in Z(G)(k)
. Our assumptions imply that we have equalities
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}}=\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
and

LG^{0}_{\gamma}=LG^{0}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
, while the projections
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
and
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma^{\prime}}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}}\to\overline{T}_{G}
are connected by the rule
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma^{\prime}}=m_{z}\circ\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}
. Therefore we have a canonical
(\Lambda_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-equivariant isomorphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{m_{z}^{*}(\mathcal{L})})
, which is
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-equivariant, if

\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}
-equivariant. This implies the assertion.
∎

Lemma B.3.2
.

Set
\mathfrak{g}:=\operatorname{Lie}G
. Then there exists an
{L}(G^{\operatorname{ad}})
-equivariant isomorphism
\Phi:LG_{\operatorname{tu}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}{L}\mathfrak{g}_{\operatorname{tn}}
between locus of topologically unipotent elements of
LG
and topologically nilpotent elements of
{L}\mathfrak{g}
, and inducing an isomorphism
\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbf{I}^{+})
.

Proof.

Notice first that if
\pi:G^{\prime}\to G
is an isogeny, whose kernel is finite of order prime to the characteristic of
k
, then
\pi
induces isomorphisms

LG^{\prime}_{\operatorname{tu}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG_{\operatorname{tu}}
and
LG^{\prime}_{\operatorname{tn}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG_{\operatorname{tn}}
(compare
[
KV
, Lemma 1.8.17]
), thus the assertion for
G
follows from from that for
G^{\prime}
.

Now set
G^{\prime}:=G^{\operatorname{sc}}\times Z(G)^{0}
, and let
\pi:G^{\prime}\to G
be the canonical projection. Then the above observation together with
our assumption (see Section
0.13
(c)) on the characteristic of
k
implies that it suffice to show the assertion for
G^{\prime}
instead for
G
. Thus it suffices to show the assertion for

G=G^{\operatorname{sc}}
and
G=\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

In both cases we know that there exists a quasi-logarithm
\Phi:G\to\mathfrak{g}
in the sense of
[
KV
, Section 1.8]
, that is, an
G^{\operatorname{ad}}
-equivariant morphism, such that
\Phi(1)=0
,
and
d\Phi_{1}:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{g}
is the identity (see
[
KV
, Lemma 1.1.6]
). Then, as in
[
KV
, Proposition 1.8.16]
, the induced morphism
\Phi:LG\to{L}\mathfrak{g}
induces isomorphisms

\Phi:LG_{\operatorname{tu}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}{L}\mathfrak{g}_{\operatorname{tn}}
and
\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbf{I}^{+})
, as claimed.
∎

B.3.3
.

The Lie algebra case.
Fix
x\in\mathfrak{g}(K)=L\mathfrak{g}(k)
, and let
G_{x}\subseteq G
be the centralizer of
x
.

(a) We denote by
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
the closed sub-indscheme (called the

affine Springer fiber
), such that for every Iwahori subgroup
\mathbf{I}
, the image of
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x}
under the canonical isomorphism
\operatorname{Fl}_{G}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}LG/\mathbf{I}
(see Section
2.2.1
(b)) consists of all
[g]\in LG/\mathbf{I}
such that
\operatorname{Ad}g^{-1}(x)\in\operatorname{Lie}\mathbf{I}
.

(b) As in
[
Lu2
]
,
[
Yun
]
each homology group
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x}):=H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
is equipped with an action of the group

\widetilde{W}_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG_{x})
.

(c) Assume now that
x\in\mathfrak{g}(K)
is regular semisimple. Then
G_{x}\subseteq G
is a maximal torus, so the construction of
Section
2.3.2
gives rise to algebra homomorphisms
\operatorname{pr}_{x}:=\operatorname{pr}_{G_{x}}
and
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{x}:=\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{G_{x}}
from
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}
to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(L{G}_{x})]
.

The following result, proved in
[
Yun
]
, is a Lie algebra analog of Theorem
2.3.4
.

Theorem B.3.4
.

For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there exists a finite
filtration
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x})\}_{j}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x})
, stable under
the action of
\widetilde{W}_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG_{x})
,
such that the action of a subalgebra
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\widetilde{W}_{G}]
on each graded piece

\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x})
is induced from the action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(L{G}_{x})]
via homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{x}
.

B.3.5
.

Remark.
Notice that statement of Theorem
B.3.4
is incompatible with that of
[
Yun
, Theorem 2]
, because the algebra homomorphism
[
Yun
, formula (1)]
used in
[
Yun
]
coincides with our homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}_{x}
(see Section
B.3.3
(c)), and thus differs from our homomorphism
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{x}
by involution
\iota
.

On the other hand, as it was confirmed by Zhiwei Yun, his argument actually proves our Theorem
B.3.4
rather than
[
Yun
, Theorem 2]
. Namely, Yun reduces the assertion to its particular case
13
13

13

actually a global version of it
, when element
x
has a regular semi-simple reduction. In this case, Lusztig’s action on homology comes from the right geometric action of
\widetilde{W}_{G}
on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,x}
, which is “compatible” with the left action of
\pi_{0}(LG_{x})
. Therefore one has to precompose one of the actions with involution
\iota
in order to have a compatibility of left actions on homology.

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
2.3.4
.

B.3.6
.

Proof of Theorem
2.3.4
.

To expose the structure of the proof we divide it into steps.

Step 1.
If suffices to assume that
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}
-equivariant.

Proof.

The local system
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}:=\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}
is naturally
\overline{W}
-equivariant, and we claim that assertion for

\mathcal{L}
follows from that for
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}
. Indeed, we have a
(\Lambda_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-equivariant decomposition

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}})=\bigoplus_{\overline{w}\in\overline{W}}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}}).

Therefore the
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-stable filtration
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}})\}_{j}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}})
satisfying Theorem
2.3.4
for
\mathcal{L}^{\operatorname{st}}
decomposes as a direct sum of

(\Lambda_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-stable filtrations
\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})\}_{j}
of the

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}})
’s satisfying Theorem
2.3.4
for
\overline{w}_{*}\mathcal{L}
.
∎

From now on we will assume that
\mathcal{L}
is
\overline{W}
-equivariant.

Step 2.
The assertion holds, if
\gamma
is topologically unipotent and
\mathcal{L}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
.

Proof.

Let
\Phi:LG_{\operatorname{tu}}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}{L}\mathfrak{g}_{\operatorname{tn}}
be an
{L}(G^{\operatorname{ad}})
-equivariant isomorphism
of Lemma
B.3.2
, inducing an isomorphism
\mathbf{I}^{+}\overset{\thicksim}{\to}\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbf{I}^{+})
. By an
{L}(G^{\operatorname{ad}})
-equivariance, we have an equality
G_{\gamma}=G_{\Phi(\gamma)}\subseteq G
, thus
\Phi(\gamma)\in\mathfrak{g}(K)
is regular semisimple (and
G_{\gamma}=G_{\gamma}^{0}
). Similarly, the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
of
\gamma\in LG
coincides with the affine Springer fiber
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\Phi(\gamma)}\subseteq\operatorname{Fl}_{G}
of
\Phi(\gamma)\in{L}\mathfrak{g}
.
Moreover, unwinding the definitions, the identification
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\Phi(\gamma)},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
is
(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-equivariant, so the assertion of Theorem
2.3.4
for
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
is equivalent to the assertion of Theorem
B.3.4
for
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\Phi(\gamma)},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})
.
∎

Step 3.
The assertion holds, if
\gamma
is topologically unipotent.

Proof.

Since
\gamma
is topologically unipotent, the map
\operatorname{red}_{\gamma}:\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}\to\overline{T}_{G}
maps all of
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
to the
identity element. Therefore
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}}
is the constant sheaf on
\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma}
with fiber
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is
\overline{W}
-equivariant (hence

\widetilde{W}_{G}
-equivariant), so we have a

(\widetilde{W}_{G}\times LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-equivariant isomorphism

H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\simeq H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}}\mathcal{L}_{1}.

Therefore the assertion for general
\mathcal{L}
follows from the particular case
\mathcal{L}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
, shown in Step 2.
∎

Step 4.
Let
\gamma=su
be the topological Jordan decomposition. Then the assertion holds if
s\in Z(G)
.

Proof.

Since
u
is topologically unipotent, the assertion follows from Step 3 and Lemma
B.3.1
.
∎

Step 5.
The assertion holds in general.

Proof.

Let
\gamma=su
be the topological Jordan decomposition. Since
G_{s}^{0}
is a split reductive group over
K
(by Lemma
B.1.5
(a)), and
s\in Z(G_{s}^{0})(K)
(by Lemma
B.1.3
(b)), the assertion holds for
\gamma\in G^{0}_{s}
(by Step 3). Since
(G_{s}^{0})_{\gamma}=G_{\gamma}
(by Lemma
B.1.3
(b)), this means that there exists a finite filtration

\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\}_{j}
of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
, stable under the action of
\widetilde{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
, such that the action of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}
on each
graded piece
\operatorname{gr}^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
is induced from
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
via the homomorphism

\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})]
.

Using Corollary
B.2.9
, we obtain the finite
\widetilde{W}_{G}\times\pi_{0}(LG^{0}_{\gamma})
-stable filtration

\Big\{F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\Big\}_{j}:=\Big\{\operatorname{ind}_{\widetilde{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}^{\widetilde{W}_{G}}F^{j}H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})\Big\}_{j}

of
H_{i}(\operatorname{Fl}_{G,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{L}})
. Since projection
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{\gamma}
is the restriction of projection
\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{G_{s}^{0},\gamma}
to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G}]^{\overline{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}[\Lambda_{G_{s}^{0}}]^{\overline{W}_{G_{s}^{0}}}
,
this filtration satisfies the required property.
∎
